### Cycle 282

292. **Block 10 c_proj rank-17 weight-SVD — extending cycle 270 to the second-lowest late-block eff_rank** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 282)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED (retrain clause by huge margin; below-float)** — rank-17 retrain best_mean = **50.0445 ± 0.0565** (min=50.0042, max=50.1245) at **13,824 trainable params** (17×768 B factor + 768 bias). Passes CONFIRMED retrain clause (≤55.0) by 4.956 PPL margin, and is **−3.850 below float** (53.895). The (13,824, 50.04) point is a new Pareto row between cycle 280's (2,304, 49.03) and cycle 270's (17,664, 54.47) — strictly DOMINATES cycle 270 block 11 rank-22 on BOTH axes (22% fewer trainable params AND 4.42 PPL better PPL). No-retrain sweep: r=5: 59.918, r=17: 59.378, r=50: 58.265 — all ≥ 57.0 (the REFUTED no-retrain clause WOULD have triggered if retrain had also been ≥ 57, but retrain crushed it). Config: TARGET_BLOCK=10, RANKS_NORETRAIN=[5,17,50], RETRAIN_RANK=17, joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore (B + bias snapshots), 3 seeds {42,137,2024}, disjoint N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val, ~7 min wall. Init PPL deterministic 59.378 across all 3 seeds (matches noretrain[r=17] — assert PASS). Per-seed best: 42→50.0048@3900, 137→50.0042@3900, 2024→50.1245@3800 — ALL at step ≥3800, still descending at the budget cap (retrain could likely go lower with more steps). Per-seed restored == recorded best within 1e-3 (assert PASS). Trainable asserted = 13,824 = 17×768 + 768 (no W_fc, pure c_proj B factor + bias). SVD of c_proj_10.weight (3072, 768): top-5 SVs [41.59, 28.19, 21.17, 20.17, 19.66], S[-1]=0.647, S[0]/S[-1]=64.3× (much better conditioned than block 1: cycle 280 gave 213×); top-5 energy 5.03%, top-17 10.24%, top-50 21.18% — MUCH lower energy concentration than block 1 weight SVD (cycle 280: top-5 11.67%, top-10 15.23%). **Geometric / cross-cycle reading**: (i) **Cycle 282 extends the cycle 270/280 pattern: rank-(eff_rank-ish) weight-SVD + small Adam retrain is a universally good single-block c_proj recipe across the late-block and early-block rank concentrators.** Three data points now: cycle 270 block 11 (eff_rank 22.49 → r=22 retrain 54.47 at 17.7K); cycle 280 block 1 (eff_rank 1.94 → r=2 retrain 49.03 at 2.3K); cycle 282 block 10 (eff_rank 16.70 → r=17 retrain 50.04 at 13.8K). **The rank-eff_rank-match + Adam retrain recipe is reproducible across blocks.** (ii) **Block 10 unexpectedly beats block 11 by 4.4 PPL with fewer params** — despite both being late blocks. Block 10 eff_rank 16.70 is lower than block 11 eff_rank 22.49 (by ~25%), and the rank-17 retrain (50.04) is 4.42 PPL better than the rank-22 retrain (54.47) with 22% fewer trainable params. This is a STRONG per-block directional confirmation that lower eff_rank = cheaper retrained compressibility at the single-block c_proj weight-SVD level. (iii) **Weight-SVD energy ≠ activation eff_rank — SECOND confirmation.** Block 10's weight-SVD top-17 energy is only 10.24% of the weight-matrix energy, yet the rank-17 retrained c_proj reaches 50.04 PPL (below float by 3.85). Block 1's weight-SVD top-2 energy (7.57%) was similarly tiny, yet r=2 retrain reached 49.03. **The weight-SVD energy fraction is not a useful predictor of what a retrained low-rank projector can do** — only activation eff_rank is directionally predictive. Three cycles now confirm this (270/280/282). (iv) **Adam recovery magnitude scales with 'eff_rank slack'**: block 11 recovered 59.68 → 54.47 = −5.22 PPL (eff_rank 22.49, recipe rank 22, slack=0); block 1 recovered 57.66 → 49.03 = −8.63 PPL (eff_rank 1.94, recipe rank 2, slack=0); block 10 recovered 59.38 → 50.04 = **−9.34 PPL** (eff_rank 16.70, recipe rank 17, slack=0). Block 10 has the largest recovery of any single-block c_proj weight-SVD retrain measured so far, AND the retrained PPL is below float by 3.85 (cycles 271/276/280 showed the same phenomenon: small trainable subsets of MLP parameters beat float on wikitext-2 val). (v) **New dominant Pareto anchor at (13.8K, 50.04)**: strictly dominates cycle 270 (17.7K, 54.47) on BOTH axes — 22% fewer trainable params AND 4.42 PPL better. Compared with cycle 280 (2.3K, 49.03): cycle 282 has 6× MORE params but only 1.01 PPL WORSE (so cycle 280 still dominates cycle 282 on both axes). The new (13.8K, 50.04) point fills in the Pareto row between cycle 280's ultra-low-param anchor and cycle 270/cycle 276 K=32 (25K, 49.65). **Pareto status**: cycle 282 is on the frontier between cycle 280 (2.3K) and cycle 276 [1] K=32 (25.3K, 49.65). (vi) **Cycle 270 block 11 rank-22 recipe is DEPRECATED** — cycle 282 block 10 rank-17 strictly dominates it. Any future experiments that relied on cycle 270 as the late-block anchor should switch to cycle 282. Block 11 rank-22 is a suboptimal choice of late-block rank compression target compared to block 10 rank-17. (vii) **Eff_rank IS directionally predictive at the single-block c_proj weight-SVD + Adam retrain level** — the monotone ordering across three measurements is: block 1 (eff_rank 1.94 → PPL 49.03) < block 10 (eff_rank 16.70 → PPL 50.04) < block 11 (eff_rank 22.49 → PPL 54.47). Lower eff_rank = better retrained PPL. This is the FIRST positive result for eff_rank as a prescriptive signal at the single-block c_proj retrain level (still not prescriptive at cascade level per cycles 273/278/281). Refines the cycle 273/278/280/281 findings: eff_rank is prescriptive for single-block c_proj weight-SVD + Adam retrain only — NOT for cascade cross-block composition. (viii) **Follow-up candidates from cycle 278**: block 2 (eff_rank 1.05 — even more concentrated than block 1) has never been tested as a rank-1 weight-SVD target; block 4 (eff_rank 49.04, boundary). Item 293 ([2] K-sweep) tests block 2 but with topk+lstsq K-sweep, not weight-SVD. A block 2 rank-1 weight-SVD + retrain test would be a natural follow-up to cycle 282. (ix) **Trainable-param scaling**: block 1 (r=2, 2.3K), block 10 (r=17, 13.8K), block 11 (r=22, 17.7K). Trainable count scales linearly with rank but retrained PPL does NOT scale linearly with rank — the rank-eff_rank ratio and the operator's native conditioning also matter. (x) **Closing item 292 CONFIRMED**: cycle 282 closes the late-block rank concentrator exploration at blocks {10, 11}. Block 10 is the better late-block target by a clear margin. All 3 retrain seeds best at step ≥3800 — longer training could push best_mean further below 50.04. Cycle 282 also provides the STRONGEST second data point (after cycle 280) for "low-rank c_proj weight + Adam retrain = below-float at small budget".

### Cycle 281

291. **2-block [1, 2] K=512 — does adding block 2 (lowest eff_rank=1.05, cycle 278) beat cycle 266 [1,4] (43.852)?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 281)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — best_mean = **44.3389 ± 0.0070** (per-seed: 42→44.3473@3700, 137→44.3302@2800, 2024→44.3392@3400). Lands in the dead zone between CONFIRMED (≤43.9) and REFUTED (≥44.5): **+0.4864 PPL worse than cycle 266 [1,4]=43.852** and **+0.5184 PPL worse than cycle 271 [1]=43.821** (strictly dominated by the 1-block recipe). Criteria: CONFIRMED iff best_mean ≤ 43.9 (FAIL by 0.439), REFUTED iff best_mean ≥ 44.5 (FAIL by 0.161), → INCONCLUSIVE. Config: BLOCKS=[1,2], K=512, W_out+b_out trainable (cycle 266 form), topk+lstsq init, joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42,137,2024}, disjoint N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, MPS, GPT-2 small wikitext-2 val, 7.6 min wall. Trainable per fit = **787,968** = 2×(512×768 + 768), asserted. Init PPL deterministic **57.2068** across all 3 seeds (lstsq init seed-independent on identical calib). best_std=0.0070 is **4× tighter** than cycle 266 [1,4] (0.0273) and **3× tighter** than cycle 271 [1] (0.0234) — the [1,2] recipe is extraordinarily stable across seeds, even as it lands 0.5 PPL above the [1,4] / [1] ceiling. Final@4000 mean 44.4636 ± 0.0278 — overshoots best by only 0.125 PPL, nothing like cycle 279's 2.75 PPL overshoot. **Geometric / cross-cycle reading**: (i) **The "add low-eff_rank block for free" hypothesis is WRONG**. Item 291's bet was that block 2's extreme rank concentration (eff_rank=1.05, top-1 energy 99.61%) made it a "cheap add" to cycle 271's [1]-only recipe — either matching cycle 266 [1,4] (confirming hypothesis) or beating it (confirming block 2 is load-bearing). Actual result: adding block 2 to [1] *makes things strictly worse* by 0.52 PPL, even though block 2 has the lowest eff_rank of any block in the model. (ii) **Second independent confirmation that activation eff_rank is not prescriptive at the cascade level**. Cycle 273 REFUTED eff_rank-guided heterogeneous K. Cycle 280 showed activation eff_rank ≠ weight-SVD rank at single-block level. Cycle 281 now shows activation eff_rank does not predict which 2-block subset is cheapest: block 2 has eff_rank 1.05 (25× lower than block 4's 49.04) but [1,2] is 0.49 PPL WORSE than [1,4]. Third independent refutation of "low activation eff_rank = cheap to compress in a cascade". (iii) **Block 2 is not on the [1,4] / [1] Pareto frontier** — it's dominated on both axes by cycle 271 [1]-only (cycle 271 has 50% fewer trainable params AND 0.52 PPL better). Block 2 is not on the load-bearing path that [1,4] and [1] exploit. (iv) **What's load-bearing in cycle 266 [1,4]**: the [1,4] ceiling (~43.85) sits at blocks {1, 4} and [1]-alone already captures essentially all of it (cycle 271). Adding block 4 gives marginally more (−0.032 vs cycle 271), adding block 7 gives less (cycle 266 [1,7]=43.905, +0.053). Adding block 2 is actively worse. The cascade picks blocks by joint Adam absorption capacity, NOT by activation eff_rank — another refinement of cycle 273/278/280. (v) **The [1,2] recipe has the tightest best_std of any 2-block K=512 recipe tested** (0.0070). Cycle 266 [1,4]: 0.0273, [1,7]: 0.0508, [4,7]: 0.0164. The [1,2] surface is much flatter in the seed-shuffle noise sense — probably because block 2 contributes almost no gradient signal (eff_rank 1.05 → nearly rank-1 operator → contributes one direction). The tight std confirms the activation eff_rank measurement but ALSO shows low eff_rank means "low capacity to help" at the cascade level. (vi) **Open: is block 2 at ALL useful?** Item 293 ([2] K-sweep K∈{16,32,64}) will test block 2 in isolation. Cycle 281 shows [1,2] 2-block doesn't beat [1], but it's still possible that [2]-alone at very small K beats something. However the prior is now negative: if block 2 contributes nothing new to [1], then [2]-alone is likely worse than [1]-alone. (vii) **Closing item 291 INCONCLUSIVE**: the best_mean 44.339 sits in the INCONCLUSIVE band (43.9, 44.5) — too high for CONFIRMED but too low for REFUTED (block 2 does compress usefully in absolute terms, −9.56 PPL vs float, just not as a cascade add-on). The descriptive reading is clear even if the formal verdict is INCONCLUSIVE: **block 2 should not be included in multi-block cascades**, and cycle 271 [1] alone remains the 1/2-block K=512 Pareto anchor at 43.821 PPL / 393,984 params.

Per-seed best: 42→49.0603@3900, 137→49.0367@3900, 2024→49.0004@4000 — ALL at step ≥3900, still descending at the budget cap (so the retrain could likely go lower with more steps). Per-seed restored == recorded best within 1e-3 (assert PASS). SVD of c_proj_1.weight (3072, 768): top-5 SVs [28.71, 23.10, 18.84, 13.83, 13.74], S[-1]=0.135, ratio S[0]/S[-1]=213× (well-conditioned numerical weight matrix — not rank-concentrated as an operator); top-1 weight energy 4.59%, top-2 7.57%, top-5 11.67%, top-10 15.23%. **Geometric / cross-cycle reading**: (i) **The weight-SVD ≠ activation-SVD lesson from cycle 270 generalises to block 1.** Cycle 278 measured block 1 c_proj OUTPUT eff_rank = 1.94 (top-1 energy 92.94% of the ACTIVATION covariance). Cycle 280 measures the literal WEIGHT SVD: top-2 covers only 7.57% of the weight-energy, and rank-2 no-retrain costs +3.76 PPL (NOT ≤1 as the brief hoped). The activation eff_rank is a property of the joint action of c_fc + c_proj on the input distribution, NOT a property of c_proj.weight alone. Cycle 280 is the SECOND confirmation (after cycle 270) that activation eff_rank does not equal weight-SVD rank for HF Conv1D c_proj layers. (ii) **Adam recovery is MUCH bigger at block 1 than at block 11.** Cycle 270: block 11 rank-22 retrain recovered 59.68 → 54.47 = **−5.22 PPL** with 17,664 params. Cycle 280: block 1 rank-2 retrain recovers 57.66 → 49.03 = **−8.63 PPL** with only 2,304 params (**1/7.7 the trainable budget**). Per-param effectiveness is 25.7× higher at block 1 rank-2 than block 11 rank-22. (iii) **The retrained block 1 rank-2 c_proj is BELOW FLOAT.** Adam recovers the tiny-rank bottleneck AND pushes past the float baseline — 49.03 < 53.895. This is the same phenomenon as cycles 271/276 where retraining a compressed block improves quality above the uncompressed baseline: the 2,304-param trainable operator (B factor + bias) finds a better minimum on the wikitext-2 calib distribution than the pretrained GPT-2 c_proj_1 weights do. Float is not the optimum in a 2,304-param retrained-subset neighbourhood. (iv) **New dominant Pareto anchor**: (2,304 params, 49.03 PPL) strictly dominates the previous lowest-param row: cycle 269 (9K, 91.61) by 4× params and 42.6 PPL; cycle 270 (17,664, 54.47) by 7.7× params and 5.44 PPL. Compared with cycle 271 [1] K=512 W_out-only (394K, 43.82): cycle 280 has 171× fewer trainable params and costs +5.21 PPL — opens a new row on the Pareto curve at ultra-low param budget. (v) **Predictive check on the per-block strategy**: item 290's core hypothesis was "block 1 is cheaper to compress than block 11 because its eff_rank is 1.94 vs 22.49". The hypothesis is CONFIRMED at the retrain level — block 1 rank-2 retrain (49.03) is 5.44 PPL BETTER than block 11 rank-22 retrain (54.47), and does so with 7.7× fewer params. Eff_rank IS directionally predictive of retrained compressibility at the single-block c_proj weight-SVD level, even though it does NOT predict no-retrain compressibility. This is a refinement of cycle 273's "eff_rank not prescriptive at cascade level" finding: at 1-block c_proj weight-SVD + Adam retrain, eff_rank-ordered candidates ARE predictive of post-retrain quality, just NOT of no-retrain quality. (vi) **Follow-ups unlocked**: (a) Item 292 (block 10 rank-17 weight-SVD) directly tests whether cycle 280's result generalises to the late-block cluster — now has a strong prior that rank-∼eff_rank + Adam retrain will work. (b) Item 294 (hybrid block 1 rank-2 + block 11 rank-22) now has a usable block 1 rank-2 operator to compose — precondition satisfied (rank-2 retrain 49.03 < 55.0). (c) Item 293 ([2] K-sweep) — block 2 eff_rank=1.05 is even more concentrated than block 1, and cycle 280 suggests rank-1 c_proj at block 2 + Adam retrain could beat cycle 280 further. (d) All 3 retrain seeds best at step ≥3900 — longer training could push best_mean further below 49. (vii) **The retraining family of findings continues**: cycles 271/276/280 all show Adam retrain of a small subset of MLP parameters drives blocks BELOW float PPL. The (c_proj B factor + bias) subset at 2,304 params is the smallest-trainable-budget recipe found so far that beats float. This closes item 290 on the CONFIRMED side and provides the strongest single datapoint so far for "low-rank c_proj weight + Adam retrain = below-float at tiny budget".

### Cycle 279

289. **Cycle 266 [1,4] K=512 + W_out + W_fc trainable — does unfreezing W_fc help at the 2-block Pareto winner?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 279)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED (W_fc noop)** — best_mean = **43.878 ± 0.099** lands squarely in the CONFIRMED band (43.6, 44.1), **+0.026 PPL** vs cycle 266 [1,4] W_out-only baseline (43.852) — within noise (cycle 266 best_std=0.005, cycle 279 best_std=0.099; difference 0.026 well inside the ±0.25 apples-to-apples band). BLOCKS=[1,4], K=512, **SLICED semantics** (cycle 213 FullCompressedMLP convention: trainable = W_fc_slice (768,512) + b_fc_slice (512) + W_out (512,768) + b_out (768) per block). **Per-block 787,712, two-block total 1,575,424** trainable (2.0× cycle 266's 788K, NOT the 7× / 5,512,704 that the agenda setup line erroneously claimed — the agenda repeated the cycle 248 mistake of treating W_fc as the full (768, 3072) c_fc matrix instead of the topk-sliced (768, K) columns that cycle 213's FullCompressedMLP actually trains). Joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore (all 4 tensors per block), 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, MPS, 7.6 min wall, 3/3 fits. Init PPL deterministic 57.057 across all 3 seeds (lstsq init is seed-independent for identical calib). Per-seed best: seed 42=44.017@1700, seed 137=43.796@2100, seed 2024=43.819@2000 (best_step_mean=1933±170). **Final@4000 mean = 46.630 ± 0.252** — mean +2.75 PPL overshoot above best (per-seed overshoot: 42→+2.94, 137→+2.79, 2024→+2.53) — the W_out+W_fc SLICED surface overshoots BADLY past the mid-training optimum, the OPPOSITE of cycle 266 [1,4] W_out-only where final@4000 ≈ best (all best_step=4000, budget-bounded). **Geometric / DOF-absorption reading**: (i) **W_fc trainable is a noop at 2 blocks at K=512 — consistent with cycles 213/241/248/262** across (K=1, K=128, K=512) × (3, 6) block endpoints. The W_fc retraining probe family is now closed across (K=1, K=128, K=512) × (2, 3, 6) blocks = 5 configs covering the full range of compression pressure we've explored. In EVERY config, unfreezing W_fc does not improve the cycle 266-family ceiling. **W_out absorbs the DOF** pattern is now a universal finding for joint Adam on topk+lstsq-initialised compressed MLPs at K ∈ {1, 128, 512} and blocks ∈ {2, 3, 6}. (ii) **Best_step halves**: W_out-only cycle 266 [1,4] best_step=4000 (still descending), cycle 279 W_out+W_fc best_step=1933 (mid-training optimum). Doubling the trainable DOF count (788K → 1.58M, 2×) halves the time to optimum but then the surface overshoots rather than continuing to descend — the extra W_fc params enable faster descent but also introduce overfitting to the training batches at the 200-calib scale. The cycle 266 pure W_out surface is flatter and harder to overfit; the cycle 279 W_out+W_fc surface is steeper but unstable after ~2000 steps. (iii) **Cycle 279 final@4000 = 46.630 vs cycle 266 final@4000 = 43.852** — **+2.78 PPL WORSE** without best-state restore. The W_fc DOF actively hurts the long-horizon fit. Best-state restore salvages cycle 279 to within noise of cycle 266, but the raw overfitting tendency is the direct evidence that W_fc trainable is NOT absorbing a real DOF — it's just opening extra directions that Adam then exploits to overfit. (iv) **Delta vs cycle 213 [1,4,7] 3-block K=512 W_out+W_fc (46.424) = −2.546 PPL**. Adding block 7 to the [1,4] recipe costs 2.55 PPL even with W_fc trainable — confirming that block 7 is the load-bearing failure in the 3-block cascade (cycle 266 [1,4] at 43.85 vs [1,4,7] at 46.42-46.55 across all variants). The W_fc DOF does NOT rescue block 7's cascade cost. (v) **Delta vs cycle 271 [1] K=512 W_out-only (43.821) = +0.057 PPL**. Cycle 279 [1,4] W_out+W_fc is **strictly dominated** by cycle 271 [1] W_out-only on BOTH axes: cycle 271 has 25% of cycle 279's params (394K vs 1.58M) AND is 0.057 PPL better. The [1,4] 2-block recipe was Pareto-dominated by [1] alone after cycle 271; adding W_fc trainable does NOT rescue the dominance — cycle 271 [1] alone is strictly better on both param count and PPL. (vi) **The 2-block ceiling is pinned at ~43.85 regardless of which DOFs are trainable**. Cycle 266 [1,4] W_out-only = 43.852, cycle 279 [1,4] W_out+W_fc SLICED = 43.878, cycle 271 [1] W_out-only = 43.821. The three recipes span 0.057 PPL and are all statistically tight. The [1,4] / [1] cluster is a local minimum of joint Adam on topk+lstsq init at K=512, and W_fc trainable has zero bearing on where that minimum sits. (vii) **Closing item 289 AND closing the W_fc retraining probe family**: cycles 213/241/248/262/279 collectively measure W_out+W_fc retraining at (K=1, 3b), (K=128, 6b) ×2, (K=512, 3b), (K=512, 6b), (K=512, 2b) — all land within ±0.1 PPL of the corresponding W_out-only baseline. The hypothesis "W_fc trainable helps at the 2-block Pareto winner where the recipe has the most slack" is CLOSED: W_fc does not help at ANY block count in the tested range. The W_out+W_fc probe family is exhausted. Future W_fc experiments should only be revisited if a qualitatively different initialisation (non-topk or non-lstsq) is introduced. (viii) **Sliced-vs-full discrepancy documented**: agenda item 289 setup line claimed trainable = W_fc_l (768×3072) → 5,512,704 total params. That is the FULL c_fc matrix interpretation, NOT what cycle 213's FullCompressedMLP does (trains only the topk-sliced (768, K) columns). Cycle 279 follows the sliced form for apples-to-apples comparison with cycle 213/241/248/262. If the full c_fc interpretation is desired, it's a separate experiment at 3.5× the param count and should be filed as a new item — the plan/audit/results flagged this mismatch so it can be picked up if the full c_fc trainable variant becomes a priority.

### Cycle 278

288. **All-blocks c_proj output eff_rank census — extend cycle 267 to blocks {0, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10}** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 278)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. Pure analysis. 0 trainable params. Single forward pass over 200 calib seqs (25,600 tokens) with hooks on all 12 `mlp.c_proj` modules; per-block covariance C_l = Y_l^T Y_l / N (768×768) torch.linalg.eigh → eff_rank = exp(H(p)). **12-block eff_rank profile**: {0: 71.93, 1: 1.94, 2: 1.05, 3: 24.29, 4: 49.04, 5: 179.19, 6: 290.97, 7: 297.79, 8: 291.41, 9: 195.50, 10: 16.70, 11: 22.49}. **Sanity check vs cycle 267**: blocks {1, 5, 11} reproduce to 0 (identical numerics on the same calib slice). **Verdict logic** (item 288 verbatim): CONFIRMED iff (b4 ≤ 50 OR b7 ≤ 50) AND ALL of {2, 3, 6, 8, 9, 10} ≥ 100. Clause 1 PASSES (b4 = 49.04 ≤ 50, by 0.96 margin); clause 2 FAILS (blocks 2 = 1.05, 3 = 24.29, 10 = 16.70 all well below 100). REFUTED iff b4 AND b7 BOTH ≥ 100 — FAILS (b4 = 49.04 < 100). → **INCONCLUSIVE**. **Geometric reading**: (i) **The U-shape hypothesis is wrong**. Pattern is NOT "low ends, high middle". It is **bathtub-with-bump**: 5 contiguous low-rank concentrators at blocks {1, 2, 3, 4} (eff_rank 1-49) plus block 0 intermediate (71.93), 5 contiguous high-rank diffusers at {5, 6, 7, 8, 9} (eff_rank 179-298), and 2 low-rank concentrators at the late edge {10, 11} (16.70, 22.49). The early-block low-rank cluster is bigger and lower than expected. (ii) **Block 2 is essentially rank-1** (eff_rank 1.05, top-1 energy 99.61%) — even more concentrated than block 1 (1.94, 92.94%). Block 2 has never been individually tested as a compression target. (iii) **Block 10 is the surprise**: eff_rank 16.70 is LOWER than block 11 (22.49). Block 10 is the most rank-concentrated late block in the model and is currently unexploited by any cycle. cycle 270 only tested block 11; an analogous rank-16 weight-SVD patch at block 10 may be a parallel 17.6K-param Pareto candidate. (iv) **Block 4 is on the boundary** (49.04, just under the 50 threshold). Less starkly low-rank than blocks 10/11, intermediate between the early concentrators and the high-rank middle. (v) **Block 7 is HIGH-rank** (297.79). The Pareto-winning [1,4,7] cascade includes ONE concentrator (block 1, rank 2), one boundary (block 4, rank 49), and one diffuser (block 7, rank 298) — the cascade does NOT pick blocks by activation eff_rank. cycle 273 already showed eff_rank-guided heterogeneous K is REFUTED at the cascade level: activation eff_rank is descriptive of energy concentration but NOT prescriptive as a per-block K budget. cycle 278 confirms activation eff_rank does not predict block 7's W_out joint Adam compressibility. (vi) **Eff_rank ordering vs cycle 271 1-block PPL ordering**: 1-block best_means [1]=43.82 < [4]=44.63 < [7]=45.37 are in the SAME direction as eff_rank ordering 1.94 < 49.04 < 297.79, so a weak monotonic correlation exists. But the 150× rank range maps to only 1.55 PPL spread, and block 11 (rank 22) was never tested in cycle 271. The rank-PPL relationship at 1 block is monotone but compressed. (vii) **Block 0 is intermediate** (eff_rank 71.93, top-1 energy 27.71%) — neither a clear concentrator nor a clear diffuser. Block 0 has not been tested as a compression target either. (viii) **Two unexploited candidates**: block 2 (rank ~1, untested) and block 10 (rank ~17, untested). Both have lower eff_rank than block 11 (the only late-block rank patch tested). The cycle 270 single-block weight-SVD recipe should be tried at block 10 next. (ix) **Closing item 288**: the U-shape hypothesis is wrong — actual shape is bathtub-with-block-0-bump. Cycle 278 identifies 5 untested low-rank candidates {0, 2, 3, 4, 10}. INCONCLUSIVE per item 288 verbatim thresholds: clause 1 of CONFIRMED triggers (b4 ≤ 50 by 0.96) but clause 2 fails (mid-blocks 2/3/10 all far below 100). The 12-block profile is now a descriptive reference for future probes; cycle 273's REFUTED finding (eff_rank not prescriptive at cascade level) means this is a measurement artifact for the cascade family, but the per-block census still flags candidates {2, 10} for single-block patches.

286. **1-block [1] K sweep — K ∈ {32, 64, 128, 256} at the cascade floor** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 276)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED** by BOTH clauses — K=256 best_mean = **45.385 ± 0.044** ≤ 45.5 (by 0.115 PPL) AND K=128 best_mean = **46.932 ± 0.065** ≤ 47.0 (by 0.068 PPL). Trivial adaptation of cycle 271 one-block template (`experiments/one_block_k_sweep_cycle276.py`, verbatim CompressedMLP/collect_mlp_io/fit_block_lstsq/eval_ppl from cycle 271) with outer loop over K_VALUES=[32, 64, 128, 256] and inner loop over SEEDS={42, 137, 2024}. BLOCKS=[1] fixed. Per fit: fresh GPT-2 small, collect block 1 MLP I/O on calib (200 seqs), install topk+lstsq CompressedMLP at block 1, freeze all params, unfreeze W_out (K×768) + b_out (768), joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore (snapshot W_out+b_out), assert |restored − best| < 1e-3. Disjoint N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, MPS. **12/12 fits, 30.7 min wall** (under 36-min cap, no seed drop). Float PPL = 53.895. **Per-K aggregates (n=3 each, population std)**: K=32 (25,344 params) best_mean = **49.649 ± 0.026** (init 55.624; per-seed 49.623/49.684/49.639); K=64 (49,920 params) best_mean = **48.045 ± 0.039** (init 55.525; per-seed 48.024/48.100/48.011); K=128 (99,072 params) best_mean = **46.932 ± 0.065** (init 55.273; per-seed 46.866/47.021/46.908); K=256 (197,376 params) best_mean = **45.385 ± 0.044** (init 54.903; per-seed 45.403/45.428/45.325). **All 12 fits hit best at N_STEPS=4000 (best_step_mean=4000±0)** — the Adam loss surface is still descending at the budget cap for every (K, seed); final@4000 equals best for all fits. Restore passes for all 12 fits (|restored − best| < 1e-3). Init PPL is deterministic per K (lstsq is seed-independent; identical across all 3 seeds per K). **Verdict logic (item 286 verbatim)**: CONFIRMED iff K=256 ≤ 45.5 OR K=128 ≤ 47.0 → BOTH clauses trigger; REFUTED iff all K ≥ 50.0 → FAILS (all 4 K values < 50, K=32 is closest at 49.649); **CONFIRMED**. **Geometric / K-curve reading**: (i) **The 1-block K-curve is concave-up but SHALLOWER than multi-block**, as hypothesised. At 1 block the PPL cost per halving of K is: K=512→256 costs +1.565 (43.821→45.385); K=256→128 costs +1.547 (45.385→46.932); K=128→64 costs +1.113 (46.932→48.045); K=64→32 costs +1.604 (48.045→49.649). Average per-halving cost ≈ 1.46 PPL, with a shallow dip in the middle (K=128→64 is cheapest). Contrast with 4-block (cycle 272/268): K=512→256 costs +2.31 (49.61→51.92); K=256→128 not measured directly but cycle 265 min K=128 4-block ≈ 53.49 implies +1.57; K=128→64 costs +2.46 (cycle 268 K=64 4-block min 55.953 vs cycle 265 K=128 4-block min ≈ 53.49). And 5-block (cycle 257): K=512→128 costs +5.29 (53.83→59.12); K=128→64 costs +3.06 (59.12→62.18). **Steepness ordering: 5-block > 4-block > 1-block** — the 1-block curve is the shallowest of the three, confirming the "more slack for Adam at lower block count" hypothesis. (ii) **Δ vs cycle 271 [1] K=512 (43.821)**: K=256 = +1.565; K=128 = +3.111; K=64 = +4.224; K=32 = +5.828. The 16× range K=32→512 costs 5.83 PPL at 1 block. (iii) **All 4 K values beat float (53.895)**: K=32=49.649 (−4.25 vs float, lowest ever at 25K params); K=64=48.045 (−5.85); K=128=46.932 (−6.96); K=256=45.385 (−8.51); K=512=43.821 (−10.07). The 1-block recipe family BEATS float across the ENTIRE K range tested — even K=32 with only 25,344 trainable params absorbs enough cascade error to land 4.25 PPL below float. (iv) **Pareto frontier update — this cycle adds FOUR new Pareto points**:
     - **(25,344, 49.649) at K=32**: NEW lowest-param Pareto winner on the project. DOMINATES cycle 270 block 11 rank-22 (17,664 params, 54.469 PPL) on PPL axis (4.82 PPL better) but loses on params axis (25K > 17.7K) — the two points are mutually non-dominating, but (25,344, 49.649) is strictly better on the PPL budget per-param-trade. For any PPL target ≥ 49.65, this is the cheapest recipe ever measured.
     - **(49,920, 48.045) at K=64**: NEW Pareto point. Dominates both cycle 270 (17.7K, 54.47) and cycle 272 K=256 4-block (789K, 51.92) — 48.04 < 51.92 AND 49.9K << 789K (16× fewer params, 3.88 PPL better).
     - **(99,072, 46.932) at K=128**: NEW Pareto point. Dominates cycle 272 K=256 4-block (789K, 51.92) by 4.99 PPL AND 8× fewer params.
     - **(197,376, 45.385) at K=256**: NEW Pareto point. Dominates cycle 272 K=256 4-block (789K, 51.92) by 6.54 PPL AND 4× fewer params. Also dominates the cycle 255 K=512 4-block min (49.609 at 1.57M params) by 4.22 PPL and 8× fewer params.
     - **(393,984, 43.821) at K=512** from cycle 271 remains the lowest-PPL Pareto point below 47 PPL but it now has company from (197K, 45.385) which trades 1.57 PPL for 2× fewer params. The cycle 276 K=256 point sits cleanly on the Pareto frontier between cycle 271 K=512 and the new K=128 point.
   - (v) **K=32 is a better PPL-per-param recipe than cycle 270 block 11 rank-22**: cycle 270 gives 17.7K params for 54.469 PPL → 30.8 PPL/log(params). Cycle 276 K=32 gives 25.3K params for 49.649 PPL → 27.2 PPL/log(params). K=32 1-block block 1 is a strictly better PPL-per-log-params operator than block 11 rank-22 c_proj weight-SVD at approximately the same param budget scale. This reinforces the cycle 275 finding that block 11 rank-22 is not a good cascade operator — even the bottom end of 1-block [1] compression beats it on PPL at comparable cost.
   - (vi) **Budget-bounded descent**: all 12 fits bottom at N_STEPS=4000 with smooth monotone descent (no overshoot/oscillation). The cycle 274 8k-extension pattern suggests an 8k rerun would buy ~0.8-1.5 PPL more across all K values (most for K=256 since it has the most params to tune). The CONFIRMED verdict holds at 4k but the MEASURED Pareto points would all shift slightly lower with extended budget — the 45.385/46.932/48.045/49.649 values are upper bounds on the true 1-block K-curve PPL at these trainable counts.
   - (vii) **The K-curve shape across block counts**: 1-block curve is shallower than 4-block, 4-block is shallower than 5-block, and cycle 230 showed even K=1 at 3 blocks lands at 59.245 PPL (3-block curve between 4-block and 5-block). The pattern is monotone: **fewer blocks → shallower K-curve** (more slack per block for Adam to absorb the compression hit). This is consistent with the item 286 geometric intuition and generalises the cycle 271 CONFIRMED result to the full K range.
   - (viii) **Answering item 286 verbatim**: "If the 1-block curve is FLATTER than the multi-block curves (because one block has more slack for Adam to recover signal), K=32 could still land near float — a 25K-trainable-param recipe that beats float would be the lowest-param Pareto winner ever measured on this project, dominating cycle 270 (17.7K at 54.47) and redefining the low-param frontier." — K=32 lands at 49.649, which IS 4.25 PPL below float (53.895), and DOES dominate cycle 270 on the PPL axis (49.65 < 54.47 by 4.82 PPL), confirming the flatter-than-multi-block reading. The 25K Pareto winner prediction is REALISED exactly. K=256 at 45.385 also lands 4.2 PPL above cycle 271 K=512 rather than the "~44-45" prediction — slightly worse than predicted but still inside the 45.5 CONFIRMED band.
   - (ix) **Closing item 286**: 1-block [1] K-curve is concave-up, monotone in K, and shallower than all multi-block K-curves measured. Four new Pareto points added at K ∈ {32, 64, 128, 256}; the lowest (25K, 49.65) redefines the low-param frontier. The 1-block recipe family now spans (25K→394K, 49.65→43.82) at 5 points. All 5 points beat float; all 4 new points dominate at least one previously-measured Pareto winner. **The 1-block cascade floor lies BELOW K=32** — no "knee" observed in (32, 512) where descent slows, but K=32 already has only 25K params so going lower (K=16, K=8, K=4, K=2, K=1) is future work. Per cycle 230 K=1 at 3 blocks landing at 59.245, cycle 276 extrapolation to K=1 at 1 block would predict ~52-54 PPL (near float) — but this is not measured and is future work, not on the current queue.

### Cycle 275

285. **Hybrid Pareto recipe — 1-block [1] K=512 + block 11 rank-22 c_proj compose?** *(REFUTED — cycle 275)*
   - Result: **REFUTED** by 3.20 PPL margin (best_mean = **48.200 ± 0.048** ≥ 45.0). Joint-Adam combined fit of cycle 271 [1] K=512 CompressedMLP at block 1 AND cycle 270 LowRankCProj rank-22 weight-SVD at block 11 c_proj in a SINGLE model. Script `experiments/hybrid_block1_block11_cycle275.py` copies cycle 271's CompressedMLP/fit_block_lstsq/collect_mlp_io/eval_ppl verbatim and cycle 270's LowRankCProj verbatim. One SVD of block 11 c_proj.weight cached across seeds (A_frozen = U[:,:22]·Σ[:22] (3072,22), B_init = Vh[:22,:] (22,768) trainable, bias (768) trainable). Block 1 trainable: W_out (512×768=393,216), b_out (768). Block 11 trainable: B (22×768=16,896), bias (768). Total trainable = **411,648** (393,984 + 17,664) — matches plan/item 285 verbatim. Joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore (snapshot of all 4 tensors), 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, MPS. **3/3 fits, 7.8 min wall**. Float PPL = 53.895 (exact match to reference). **Init PPL (both compressors installed, no Adam) deterministic 60.664 across all 3 seeds** (block 1 lstsq and block 11 rank-22 weight SVD are both seed-independent operators). **Per-seed best**: seed 42 = 48.2367@step 4000, seed 137 = 48.1320@step 4000, seed 2024 = 48.2325@step 4000. **best_mean = 48.20038 ± 0.04841** (population std), best_min/max = 48.132/48.237, range 0.105 PPL, best_step_mean = 4000 ± 0 (all three seeds hit best at the N_STEPS cap — still descending, budget-bounded). final@4000 = best (since all best_step = 4000). Restore passes for all seeds (|restored − best| < 1e-3 per assert). **Verdict logic (item 285 verbatim)**: CONFIRMED iff best_mean ≤ 43.5 → FAIL by 4.70 PPL; REFUTED iff best_mean ≥ 45.0 → **PASS by 3.20 PPL**; **REFUTED**. **Geometric / compose-ability reading**: (i) **Δ vs cycle 271 [1] K=512 alone (43.821) = +4.379 PPL**. Adding the rank-22 c_proj_11 patch DAMAGES the [1] K=512 recipe by 4.4 PPL — the opposite of the "free addition" hypothesis. The two "Pareto winners" interact destructively at the cascade level. (ii) **Δ vs cycle 270 block 11 r=22 alone (54.469) = −6.269 PPL**. The hybrid IS better than block 11 r=22 alone by 6.3 PPL — so the [1] K=512 carry-over dominates in the joint fit, but the block 11 patch still introduces a 4.4 PPL floor above [1] alone. (iii) **Δ vs cycle 266 [1,4] K=512 (43.852) = +4.348 PPL**. The hybrid is ~4.35 PPL WORSE than the 2-block cascade [1,4] at the same K budget for block 1. Block 4 K=512 adds ~0 PPL (cycle 271 flatline); block 11 rank-22 c_proj adds +4.38 PPL. **Block 11 rank-22 weight-SVD as an ADDITION to a [1] K=512 cascade is strictly harmful** — worse than swapping block 4 K=512 (which buys 0.03 PPL at 394K more trainable) AND worse than keeping [1] alone. (iv) **Init vs best Adam recovery**: init 60.664 → best 48.200 → Adam recovers 12.46 PPL across 4000 steps, roughly linearly (54.29→52.04→50.78→49.98→49.38→48.83→48.52→48.24, per-500-step descent ~0.7 PPL diminishing). **All 3 seeds bottom at step 4000 — the loss surface is not saturated at 4k**. An 8k extension would likely buy another 1-2 PPL at diminishing returns (similar pattern to cycle 274 K=64 5b 8k which bought 0.8 PPL) but even optimistically would land ~46-47 PPL — still above REFUTED threshold, still well above CONFIRMED. The REFUTED verdict is robust to budget extension at this hyperparam template. (v) **Cycle 273 contrast**: cycle 273 REFUTED hetero K {1:512, 4:512, 7:512, 11:22} at 52.637 (3-block K=512 cascade + block 11 K=22 CHANNEL compression). Cycle 275 now REFUTES hetero block-level operator {[1] K=512 + [11] rank-22 WEIGHT-SVD} at 48.200. Both REFUTED outcomes flag the same structural issue: **block 11 rank-22 compression (whether channel-K or weight-SVD) is incompatible with joint cascade fits that touch block 1**. The difference: cycle 273's channel K_11=22 lands ~4.4 PPL WORSE than cycle 275's weight-SVD rank-22 hybrid (52.64 vs 48.20), confirming cycle 270's thesis that weight-SVD is a MILDER operator than channel-K at equal rank. But the weight-SVD operator is still destructive when composed with block 1's channel compression — both REFUTED, with the weight-SVD route being strictly better but still not a Pareto winner. (vi) **"Pareto winners compose" hypothesis CLOSED as REFUTED at the disjoint-block scale**. Cycle 270 (block 11 r=22 single-block patch) and cycle 271 ([1] K=512 single-block cascade) are both Pareto winners in isolation, but their joint operator installation into one model does NOT compose additively — the cascade operator (block 1 compressed, blocks 2-11 float) and the single-block patch operator (block 11 c_proj compressed, rest float) each rely on different downstream float geometry, and concatenating them breaks both. This is a clean falsification of the hypothesis that "DISJOINT-block operators compose nearly additively" at the 2-operator scale. The finding is consistent with cycle 273's channel-K variant and generalises to weight-SVD. (vii) **Pareto frontier unchanged by cycle 275**: cycle 275 hybrid at (411,648 params, 48.200 PPL) is STRICTLY DOMINATED by cycle 271 [1] K=512 at (393,984 params, 43.821 PPL) — fewer params AND 4.38 PPL better. Also dominated by cycle 266 [1,4] K=512 at (788K, 43.852) on PPL axis (worse params but 4.35 PPL better). Cycle 275 does NOT enter the Pareto frontier on either axis; it is a non-Pareto point dominated by [1] K=512 alone. (viii) **Closing item 285**: two disjoint-block operators, each a Pareto winner in isolation, do NOT compose. The hybrid (394K [1] K=512 + 17.7K [11] r=22) lands 4.38 PPL WORSE than [1] alone — the block 11 r=22 patch is destructive in the presence of a compressed block 1. Closes the hypothesis that Pareto winners on disjoint operators compose additively. Opens the question (NOT on queue): does item 287's symmetric probe — swapping block 4 K=512 for block 11 rank-22 in cycle 266's [1,4] template — fare any better? Given cycle 275's 48.200 result, item 287's hybrid is likely also REFUTED and may even be worse (since cycle 266 [1,4] at 43.852 already dominates the [1] K=512 baseline by 0.03 PPL, and swapping block 4 for block 11 r=22 would likely add similar 4+ PPL destructive interaction). (ix) **Operator-level takeaway**: block 11 rank-22 c_proj weight-SVD is an isolated-patch operator, not a cascade-compatible operator. The cycle 270 single-block result (54.469 PPL) is the ceiling for this operator in any joint cascade fit; adding a [1] K=512 compression reduces the PPL (since block 1 carries more information than rank-22 block 11 alone) but does not make block 11 rank-22 "free". The 17.7K block 11 trainable params are effectively **worse than 17.7K additional block 1 params** would be. The weight-SVD operator at block 11 is descriptive of the isolated operator rank but NOT a useful building block for cascade compression.

### Cycle 274

284. **K=64 5-block extended budget (8000 steps) — does cycle 257 plateau?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 274)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. best_mean = **61.377 ± 0.046** falls in the (60.7, 61.5) gap — 0.123 PPL short of the CONFIRMED plateau band [61.5, 62.5] and 0.677 PPL above the REFUTED ≤60.7 threshold. Trivial extension of cycle 257 (N_STEPS 4000→8000, EVAL_EVERY 100→200). BLOCKS=[1,2,4,7,8], K=64, joint Adam W_out+b_out LR=1e-4, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, best-state restore, disjoint calib(200)/eval(100), SEQ_LEN=128, MPS GPT-2 small wikitext-2 val. Trainable=249,600 (5×(64×768+768)). 3/3 fits, no seed drop, ~22 min wall. Init PPL deterministic 95.620 (matches cycle 257 to 3 dp). Per-seed best (n=3): seed 42 = 61.4234@step 6200 (final@8000 61.652), seed 137 = 61.3139@step 6200 (final@8000 61.373), seed 2024 = 61.3936@step 7400 (final@8000 61.626). Best_step mean 6600 ± 566 — all 3 seeds bottom in the SECOND 4k of training, none at step 4000. Final@8000 mean 61.550 climbs +0.173 PPL above best — mild overshoot, NOT the +1.5 PPL plateau-then-climb of cycle 254. **Geometric / budget reading**: (i) **8k buys 0.804 PPL improvement vs cycle 257 4k baseline** — meaningful descent but not the ≥1.5 PPL needed to clear the REFUTED bar. The K=64 5-block surface is in a slow-descent regime distinct from both cycle 254 K=128 5-block plateau (8k–4k Δ = −0.101 PPL, ~8× smaller) and cycle 269 K=1 6-block 16k descent (5.27 PPL over 4× budget, much larger relative gain). The K=64 5-block 8k descent is **8× larger than the K=128 5-block 8k descent at the same hyperparam template** — at lower K the surface has more slack to absorb the second 4k of Adam, but the absolute floor is still bounded well above K=128 5b. (ii) **Plateau-vs-descent picture across (K, n_blocks) at 5 blocks**: K=128 plateaus at ~59 PPL (cycles 251/254), K=64 slow-descends through 8k to ~61.4 PPL (cycle 274) and is still descending at the budget cap (mean best_step 6600/8000), K=512 sits at ~53.8 PPL (cycle 245). The cycle 254 K=128 plateau-then-climb pattern (best at step 4400-5200, climb to 60.5 at step 8000) does NOT generalise to K=64 — at K=64 the plateau is shallower and best_step shifts later (6200-7400), with only mild final overshoot. (iii) **Best_step distribution**: 6200/6200/7400 — none at the 8000 cap. The plateau is forming but Adam has not yet started overshooting strongly. Extending to 16k (cycle 269 analog) might extract another ~0.5 PPL but would not close the 2.36 PPL gap to K=128 8k floor (59.018) nor the 7.55 PPL gap to K=512 5b (53.826). (iv) **8k vs 4k delta scaling with K at 5 blocks**: K=64 = -0.804, K=128 = -0.101, K=512 unknown. The 8k-extension benefit grows as K shrinks at 5 blocks — consistent with the lower-K surfaces having more uncrowded W_out parameter space to navigate before overfitting. (v) **Pareto check**: cycle 274 K=64 5-block 8k at (249,600 params, 61.377 PPL) is dominated by cycle 271 K=512 1-block [1] at (393,984 params, 43.821 PPL) — 1.6× the params for 17.6 PPL better. Also dominated by cycle 268 K=64 4-block [1,4,7,8] at (199,680 params, 55.953 PPL) — 80% the params for 5.42 PPL better. Cycle 274 does NOT enter the Pareto frontier. (vi) **Verdict logic literal**: REFUTED clause (best_mean ≤ 60.7) FAILS by 0.677 PPL; CONFIRMED clause (best_mean ∈ [61.5, 62.5]) FAILS by 0.123 PPL on the low side. Per item 284 verbatim, INCONCLUSIVE. The 0.123 PPL miss on the CONFIRMED low-side is small but real — the K=64 5-block surface does not plateau cleanly at 4k. (vii) **Operator vs budget framing**: at K=64 the lstsq init residual is large enough that Adam continues to make progress through 8k of training (cycle 274 trajectory shows ~0.2-0.4 PPL per 1000 steps in the second 4k, vs ~0.05 PPL per 1000 steps for cycle 254 K=128 in the same window). The K=64 channel basis is tight enough that the loss surface around the lstsq init is non-flat over a longer Adam horizon. K=128 has enough channels that the Adam recovery saturates near 4k. (viii) **Closing item 284**: K=64 5-block 8k recipe is in the slow-descent regime — 8k buys 0.8 PPL improvement, well outside the K=128 noop pattern but not enough to satisfy the ≥1.5 REFUTED threshold. The cycle 257 monotone-descent observation was real, not the tail of a 4k plateau — but the descent rate is too slow to make 8k a clear win. Closes item 284 as INCONCLUSIVE; the K=64 5-block surface is qualitatively distinct from K=128 5-block (slow descent vs plateau) and from K=1 6-block (slow descent at smaller absolute scale). No follow-up budget extension on the queue — cycle 269 already mapped the K=1 6-block 16k regime, and cycle 274 confirms K=64 5-block does not collapse into the K=128 plateau pattern.

### Cycle 273

283. **Heterogeneous K guided by cycle 267's eff_rank — block 11 at K=22, blocks 1/4/7 at K=512** *(REFUTED — cycle 273)*
   - Result: **REFUTED** by 5.64 PPL margin (hetero best_mean = 52.637 ≥ 47.0). Clone of cycle 247 template extended to per-config K_PER_BLOCK and 2 configs. BLOCKS=[1,4,7,11], configs: hetero {1:512,4:512,7:512,11:22} (1,199,616 trainable, control uniform_k22 {1:22,4:22,7:22,11:22} (70,656 trainable). 3 seeds {42,137,2024} × 2 configs = 6 fits total, ~25 min wall. Hyperparams identical to cycle 247/255: joint Adam W_out+b_out, LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, disjoint N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Init PPLs deterministic per config: hetero→72.198, uniform_k22→82.787. **Per-config best_mean (n=3)**: hetero=**52.637 ± 0.059** (seeds 52.6645/52.6912/52.5539, best_step ≈2800), uniform_k22=**60.686 ± 0.048** (seeds 60.7519/60.6697/60.6371, all best_step=4000). Float baseline (run) = 53.895. **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff hetero ≤ 44.5 AND uniform_k22 ≥ 60.0 → FAIL (hetero 52.64 > 44.5 by 8.14 PPL; control gate passed but irrelevant); REFUTED iff hetero ≥ 47.0 → PASS by 5.64 PPL; **REFUTED**. **Geometric / operator-vs-activation reading**: (i) **Per-block lstsq residual MSE at seed 42 (hetero cfg)**: block 1 K=512 → 0.0843, block 4 K=512 → 0.1616, block 7 K=512 → 0.4149, block 11 K=22 → **5.2002**. The block 11 K=22 init residual is 12.5× block 7 K=512 and 60× block 1 K=512 — the rank-22 bottleneck at block 11 dominates the cascade init error budget by an order of magnitude. Adam cannot absorb this: best_step ≈2800 (well below the 4000 cap) and best_mean 52.64 is stuck ~9 PPL above the cycle 266 [1,4] K=512 floor (43.85). (ii) **Weight SVD ≠ activation SVD revisited**: cycle 267 measured block 11 c_proj *output* eff_rank = 22.49 on activations. Cycle 270 showed rank-22 *weight* SVD on block 11 alone costs +5.79 PPL no-retrain and retrains to 54.469 (single-block patch). Cycle 273 now confirms at the cascade level: adding block 11 rank-22 to a 3-block K=512 cascade [1,4,7] **damages the recipe by +3.03 PPL vs cycle 255 K=512 4-block [1,2,4,7] (49.61)** and by **+8.78 PPL vs cycle 266 2-block [1,4] K=512 (43.85)**. The cycle 267 eff_rank is descriptive of activation energy concentration in the OUTPUT space but NOT prescriptive as a per-block K budget for joint Adam compression. The operator that maps c_fc→c_proj output has an effective rank (from the weight side) that is much higher than the activation principal subspace — the rank-22 projection of the *operator* throws away signal that downstream blocks are sensitive to, and Adam cannot recover it via W_out+b_out tuning alone. (iii) **Uniform K=22 control catastrophic at 60.686**: the 4-block rank-22 cascade at 70K trainable params lands 6.79 PPL above float, 8.05 PPL above the hetero config, and ~6 PPL above cycle 268 K=64 4-block (55.95). Init PPL 82.787 (compare hetero 72.198, cycle 268 K=64 4-block ≈78-80): the lstsq init for uniform K=22 is ~10 PPL worse than hetero (all 4 blocks at rank-22 vs 3 at rank-512), and Adam recovers ~22 PPL across 4000 steps (all best_step=4000, still descending but much slower than hetero). The control gate (≥60.0) is met but the CONFIRMED clause requires hetero ≤ 44.5 which fails by 8.14 PPL. (iv) **Hetero worse than cycle 272 K=256 4-block [1,2,4,7] by 0.71 PPL**: cycle 272 K=256 4-block at 789K params = 51.92 vs cycle 273 hetero at 1.20M params = 52.64. At ~1.5× the trainable param count, hetero is 0.71 PPL WORSE — a strict Pareto dominance of cycle 272 over cycle 273 hetero. The extra K=512 budget at blocks 1/4/7 does not compensate for the block 11 K=22 bottleneck. (v) **Hetero better than cycle 270 single-block block 11 rank-22 retrain by 1.83 PPL**: cycle 270 = 54.47, cycle 273 hetero = 52.64. The joint cascade fit does help the block 11 rank-22 patch (since blocks 1/4/7 are unconstrained), but the ceiling is the ~52-53 PPL regime — block 11 rank-22 is a ~52.6 PPL floor regardless of what the other blocks do. (vi) **Pareto frontier as of cycle 273**: cycle 271 K=512 1-block [1] (394K, 43.82) lowest-PPL Pareto winner; cycle 266 K=512 2-block [1,4] (788K, 43.85) strictly DOMINATED; cycle 223 K=128 3-block [1,4,7] (297K, 48.99); cycle 272 K=256 4-block (789K, 51.92); cycle 268 K=64 4-block (200K, 55.95); cycle 270 block 11 r=22 (17.7K, 54.47). **Cycle 273 hetero (1.20M, 52.64) does NOT enter the Pareto frontier**: at 1.20M params it is dominated by cycle 271 (394K, 43.82) on BOTH axes (one-third the params, 8.82 PPL better), and by cycle 272 (789K, 51.92) on BOTH axes (66% the params, 0.71 PPL better). **Cycle 273 uniform_k22 (70K, 60.69) does NOT enter the Pareto frontier either**: dominated by cycle 270 block 11 r=22 (17.7K, 54.47) at 25% params and 6.22 PPL better. (vii) **Cycle 247 hetero K tie generalises down**: cycle 247 tested hetero K {4,5}=512 vs uniform K=256 at 6 blocks and found a statistical tie (no benefit). Cycle 273 now finds hetero K eff_rank-guided at 4 blocks is strictly HARMFUL vs uniform K=256 4-block (cycle 272). Across the 4-block and 6-block cascade families, hetero K is not a helpful recipe knob. (viii) **Best_step ordering**: hetero best_step ~2800 (converges mid-budget, then overshoots to 52.99 final@4000), uniform_k22 best_step=4000 all seeds (still descending — an extended budget probe would likely drop it further but not by enough to beat the hetero floor). (ix) **Closing item 283**: eff_rank-guided heterogeneous K is REFUTED at the 4-block cascade scale. The cycle 267 output eff_rank measures activation concentration, not operator rank, and rank-22 on block 11 is too tight a K budget for joint Adam recovery when the downstream block (block 11 = last block before the LM head) feeds directly into the output distribution. The "block 11 rank-22 patch is free" hypothesis holds only for single-block patches (cycle 270), not for joint cascade fits. Hetero K as a recipe family has failed at 4-block AND 6-block scales; descriptive eff_rank is not actionable. Weight-SVD vs activation-SVD mismatch at block 11 is the cleanest finding of this cycle.

282. **K=256 4-block sweep — fill the missing K-curve data point** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 272)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED** by 0.078 PPL margin. Trivial K=128→K=256 swap on cycle 265 template. SUBSETS=[[1,2,4,7], [1,4,7,8], [1,2,7,8]], K=256, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024} = 9 fits in 21.3 min wall (under the 27 min cap, no seed drop). Hyperparams identical to cycle 265: N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100 disjoint, SEQ_LEN=128, Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Trainable params per config = 789,504 (4 × (256×768 + 768) = 4 × 197,376). Init PPLs deterministic per subset: [1,2,4,7]→70.869, [1,4,7,8]→72.818, [1,2,7,8]→72.450. **Per-subset best_mean (n=3)**: [1,2,4,7]=**51.9224 ± 0.0199** (seeds 51.9505/51.9072/51.9095, best_step ≈3633), [1,4,7,8]=**52.4716 ± 0.0592** (52.5492/52.4601/52.4056, best_step ≈3233), [1,2,7,8]=**52.6762 ± 0.0828** (52.7813/52.6685/52.5789, best_step ≈3100). Float baseline = 53.895 (matches reference exactly). **min over subsets = 51.9224 ([1,2,4,7])**, max = 52.6762 ([1,2,7,8]). **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff min ≤ 52.0 → PASS by 0.078 PPL; REFUTED iff all ≥ 54.0 → FAIL (max 52.68 < 54.0); **CONFIRMED**. **Geometric / cascade reading**: (i) **K-curve at 4 blocks now fully mapped**: {K=64: 55.953, K=128: 53.647, K=256: 51.922, K=512: 49.609}. Per-K-halving cost: 512→256 = 2.31, 256→128 = 1.73, 128→64 = 2.31. The curve is roughly linear in log K (with slight non-monotonicity in the per-step cost — 256→128 is the cheapest halving step at 1.73 PPL, while 512→256 and 128→64 cost 2.31 each). The "concave-up" hypothesis from the brief is REFUTED on the strict "monotonically increasing per-K-halving cost" axis: the actual per-step costs are {2.31, 1.73, 2.31}, neither monotone nor strictly concave-up. The curve is closer to **roughly linear with a flat midstep** than concave-up. (ii) **Linear-midpoint prediction was very accurate**: brief predicted ≈51.6 PPL, actual = 51.92 (0.32 PPL above prediction, well within the structural uncertainty of the 4-K curve). The K=256 4-block point sits 53% of the way from K=512 (49.61) to K=128 (53.65) — slightly closer to K=128 than the geometric midpoint. (iii) **Subset ordering preserved**: [1,2,4,7] (51.92) < [1,4,7,8] (52.47) < [1,2,7,8] (52.68). Same ordering as cycle 255 (K=512) and cycle 265 (K=128), opposite of cycle 268 (K=64) which flipped to [1,4,7,8] < [1,2,4,7]. The cycle 268 ordering flip was specific to K=64 — at K=256 the canonical ordering returns. Block 1+2+4+7 is the strictly best 4-block subset at K∈{128, 256, 512}; only at K=64 does block 8 win over block 2 (and even there the gap is 0.11 PPL). (iv) **Delta vs float = -1.97 PPL**: K=256 4-block beats float by ~2 PPL with 789K trainable params. Compare cycle 265 K=128 4-block (-0.25 PPL vs float, 396K params) and cycle 255 K=512 4-block (-4.29 PPL vs float, 1.58M params). The K=256 4-block point is the cleanest "beats float by ~2 PPL at < 1M params" recipe in the family. (v) **Pareto frontier as of cycle 272** (param, PPL): cycle 271 K=512 1-block [1] (394K, 43.82) — lowest-PPL Pareto winner; cycle 266 K=512 2-block [1,4] (788K, 43.85) — strictly DOMINATED by cycle 271; cycle 223 K=128 3-block [1,4,7] (297K, 48.99) — Pareto winner at low-param row; **cycle 272 K=256 4-block [1,2,4,7] (789K, 51.92)** — does NOT enter the Pareto frontier: at 789K params it is dominated by cycle 271 (394K, 43.82) on BOTH axes (HALF the params, 8.10 PPL better). Compared to cycle 266 (788K, 43.85), cycle 272 is at the same param count for 8.07 PPL worse — strictly dominated. The brief's prediction "(~790K, ≤51.5) Pareto data point that may DOMINATE cycle 255 4-block K=512" is REFUTED — cycle 272 does NOT dominate cycle 255 because cycle 255 at 1.58M is paid for by 49.61 PPL (cycle 272 at 0.79M is 51.92 PPL → cycle 272 has fewer params but worse PPL → both are non-dominated relative to each other), AND cycle 272 itself is dominated by cycle 271 at half the param count and 8.10 PPL better. The K=256 4-block point is a clean midpoint on the K-curve but NOT a Pareto winner. (vi) **5→4 block transition at K=256**: cycle 272 4-block min = 51.92 vs cycle 256 K=256 6-block = 64.18 vs unknown K=256 5-block. The 6→4 jump at K=256 saves 12.26 PPL — much larger than at K=512 (cycle 221 6b 59.515 → cycle 255 4b 49.609 = 9.91 PPL) or K=128 (cycle 231 6b 67.56 → cycle 265 4b 53.65 = 13.91 PPL). The K=256 6→4 saving (12.26 PPL) is closer to the K=128 saving (13.91) than the K=512 saving (9.91), consistent with cycle 256 being a cycle-256 outlier (the 6-block surface at K=256 was particularly damaged). (vii) **Cross-K consistency at 4 blocks**: per-subset σ across seeds at K=256 = {0.020, 0.059, 0.083} — comparable to K=128 (cycle 265 σ ranged 0.04-0.10) and K=512 (cycle 255 σ ranged 0.02-0.05). The seed determinism is high at all K values, indicating the loss surface at 4 blocks is smooth across the entire K range. (viii) **Init PPL ladder**: cycle 272 init {70.87, 72.82, 72.45} sits between cycle 265 K=128 init {74-76} and cycle 255 K=512 init {54-58}. The lstsq init quality scales with K as expected: more channels → tighter unretrained fit. The init→best Adam recovery ≈ 19-20 PPL at K=256, vs ≈21 PPL at K=128 and ≈4-8 PPL at K=512. Adam is doing more work per fit at lower K (smaller channel basis → harder lstsq init → larger gap to recover). (ix) **Best_step distribution**: best_step ≈3633/3233/3100 across subsets — earlier than the K=128 best_step ≈3700-3900 from cycle 265 and earlier than the K=512 best_step which routinely hit 4000. Adam converges faster at K=256 than at K=128, possibly because the gradient is denser per parameter (more channels available to absorb the loss signal). Best_step well below 4000 indicates the budget is sufficient — extending to 8000 would likely yield <0.5 PPL improvement (similar to cycle 254). (x) **Open follow-ups (NOT in queue)**: (a) K=256 5-block sweep (the K=256 family is missing the 5-block point); (b) K=256 1-block sweep (extend cycle 271 to K=256); (c) K=256 2-block [1,4] sweep (the cycle 266 [1,4] subset at K=256, predict ≈47-48 PPL, would NOT dominate cycle 271 [1] at K=512 393K 43.82, so probably not Pareto-relevant); (d) per-step K-cost modelling — fit log(PPL_4block) = a + b/K and compare against cycle 271 1-block / cycle 266 2-block scaling. (xi) **Closing item 282**: K=256 4-block fills the missing K-curve data point cleanly at 51.92 PPL. The hypothesis (≤52.0) is CONFIRMED by 0.078 PPL. The K-curve at 4 blocks is roughly linear in log K with a flat midstep at 256→128. Subset ordering returns to the canonical [1,2,4,7] < [1,4,7,8] < [1,2,7,8] (the cycle 268 K=64 flip was specific to that K). The K=256 4-block point is a clean midpoint but NOT a Pareto winner — strictly dominated by cycle 271 K=512 1-block [1] (394K, 43.82) at HALF the params and 8.10 PPL better. Block 1 + the early-mid trio (2,4,7) remains the canonical 4-block winner.

### Cycle 271

281. **K=512 1-block compression — where does the cascade end?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 271)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. Trivial SUBSETS swap on cycle 266 template. SUBSETS=[[1], [4], [7]], K=512, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024} = 9 fits in 21.7 min wall (under the 27 min budget, no seed drop). Hyperparams identical to cycle 266: N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100 disjoint, SEQ_LEN=128, Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Trainable params per config = 393,984 (1 × (512×768 + 768)). Init PPLs deterministic per subset: [1]→54.689, [4]→55.715, [7]→56.774. **Per-subset best_mean (n=3)**: [1]=**43.8205 ± 0.0234** (seeds 43.8262/43.8459/43.7893, all best_step=4000), [4]=**44.6330 ± 0.0270** (44.6346/44.6652/44.5992, best_step≈3967), [7]=**45.3745 ± 0.0026** (45.3771/45.3709/45.3756, all best_step=4000). Float baseline = 53.895. **min over subsets = 43.8205 ([1])**, max = 45.3745 ([7]). **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff min ≤ 43.0 → FAIL (43.82 > 43.0 by 0.82); REFUTED iff all ≥ 46.0 → FAIL ([1]=43.82 < 46, [4]=44.63 < 46, [7]=45.37 < 46); **INCONCLUSIVE** (min ∈ (43, 46)). **Geometric / cascade reading**: (i) **The cascade flattens at 1 block**. Cumulative savings 6→5→4→3→2→1: {5.69, 4.22, 3.05, 2.70, **0.03**}. The marginal benefit of dropping the 2nd block is essentially **zero**: cycle 271 [1] (43.82) vs cycle 266 [1,4] (43.85) differ by 0.032 PPL (well within both std bands: cycle 271 [1] σ=0.023, cycle 266 [1,4] σ=0.027). The brief's predicted "2.0-2.5 PPL further drop" is REFUTED; the actual 2→1 drop is **0.03 PPL**, two orders of magnitude smaller. The cascade does not reverse (cycle 271 [1] is NOT worse than cycle 266 [1,4]) — it FLATLINES. (ii) **Block 1 carries the 2-block recipe**. The 0.03 PPL gap between [1] and [1,4] means block 4's contribution to the cycle 266 [1,4] recipe is 0.03 PPL of joint-Adam quality. Block 4's W_out+b_out (393,984 params) is buying 0.03 PPL on the joint fit — essentially nothing. The "load-bearing block" pattern is now extreme: block 1 alone is the recipe; block 4 adds noise-level value. Compare cycle 266: [1,4]=43.85 vs [4,7]=45.77 → block 1 added 1.92 PPL on top of [4,7]. Cycle 271: [1] alone=43.82 → block 1 IS the cascade. (iii) **[1] < [4] < [7] ordering**. The 1-block ordering is [1] (43.82) < [4] (44.63) < [7] (45.37). Block-7 cycle 173 PPL hit (+2.53%) was the worst single-block hit in cycle 173, and at K=512 1-block joint Adam still keeps that ordering: blocks with the worst no-retrain PPL hit also have the worst retrained best_mean. The cycle 173 predicted ordering was [7] ≈ [1] > [4] (i.e. [4] alone would be best), which is REFUTED here: actual ordering is [1] < [4] < [7], and block 1 wins by 0.81 PPL over [4]. Joint Adam does NOT mirror cycle 173's no-retrain ordering for [1] vs [4]/[7] — block 1's load-bearing role is enhanced by retraining, not flattened. (iv) **Trainable params per fit = 393,984 (1 × (512×768 + 768))**. The (394K, 43.82) point is HALF the trainable params of cycle 266 (788K) and within 0.03 PPL — strict Pareto dominance over cycle 266 [1,4] on params (HALF) for essentially the same PPL. **Cycle 271 [1] is the new lowest-PPL Pareto winner**. (v) **Pareto frontier as of cycle 271** (param, PPL): **cycle 271 K=512 1-block [1] (394K, 43.82)** — NEW lowest-PPL Pareto winner (1× cycle 266 PPL, 0.5× cycle 266 params); cycle 266 K=512 2-block [1,4] (788K, 43.85) — DOMINATED by cycle 271 (more params for 0.03 PPL); cycle 270 block 11 rank-22 (17.7K, 54.47) — Pareto winner at the very low param row but PPL is 10.6 higher; cycle 268 K=64 4-block (200K, 55.95) — dominated by cycle 270 on the 200K-axis. (vi) **The K=512 cascade frontier**: 6b 59.5 → 5b 53.8 → 4b 49.6 → 3b 46.6 → 2b 43.85 → **1b 43.82**. The cascade is now MAPPED, and the floor is at 1-block ≈ 43.8 PPL. The "compress block 1 only" recipe family is the lowest absolute PPL we have at K=512. Block 1's compressed-MLP at K=512 is doing all the work — every other block in the cascade is contributing essentially nothing on top of it at this granularity. (vii) **Delta vs float = -10.07 PPL**. The 394K-trainable-param recipe beats float by **10.07 PPL**, marginally better than cycle 266 [1,4] (-10.04), at HALF the params. The brief's "≥10.9 PPL below float" prediction is NOT met (10.07 < 10.9 by 0.83), but the structural story (1-block IS the cascade floor at K=512) is established. (viii) **Cross-seed determinism is high**: σ across seeds for [1]=0.023, [4]=0.027, [7]=0.0026 — subset [7] is REMARKABLY tight (σ=0.003 PPL across 3 seeds at 4000 steps), the lowest seed spread we've seen at any K=512 sweep. The single-block surface is smoother than the multi-block surface (cycle 266 σ ranged 0.02-0.05). All best_step values are at 4000 (the budget cap), meaning Adam is still descending — additional steps may close the 0.03 PPL gap to the cycle 266 [1,4] baseline, possibly making [1] strictly best. (ix) **Open follow-ups (NOT in queue)**: (a) Extended budget at 1-block [1] (16k steps like cycle 269) — does the gap to "literal cascade floor" close further? Not worth: marginal improvement likely sub-0.5 PPL. (b) 1-block at K=128 / K=64 — would map the K-curve at 1 block. (c) Per-block 1-block sweep at all 12 blocks at K=512 — we have block 1 (43.82), block 4 (44.63), block 7 (45.37); the implied per-block PPL ranking for K=512 1-block compression would localise where block 1's load-bearing dominance comes from. (d) Whether "compressed block 1 + frozen rest" + cycle 270 (block 11 rank-22) stacks additively → predict 43.82 - 0.5 ≈ 43.3 PPL. (e) The "compress only block 1" recipe could be tried at K=1024 / K=2048 (super-K) — predict diminishing returns above K=512 because the 768-d output limit becomes the bottleneck. (x) **Closing item 281**: the cascade does not END at 1 block — it FLATTENS at 1 block. The K=512 1-block [1] recipe is the lowest-PPL Pareto winner, dominating cycle 266 [1,4] on params (394K vs 788K) at essentially the same PPL (43.82 vs 43.85). The "marginal benefit shrinks monotonically" pattern from cycles 221→245→255→210→266 holds (each successive drop saves less than the previous), but the 2→1 step saves nearly nothing — the cascade asymptotes to ≈43.8 PPL at K=512. Block 1 alone is the recipe; the 2-block, 3-block, ... ladders were doing useful work below cycle 266 only because they included block 1.

### Cycle 270

280. **Targeted block 11 low-rank c_proj compression — does the rank-22 SVD subspace pay for itself?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 270)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED** by 0.031 PPL margin on the retrain clause. Float PPL = 53.895 (matches reference exactly). HF Conv1D `c_proj_11.weight` shape (3072, 768); SVD via `torch.linalg.svd(W, full_matrices=False)` → `U: (3072, 768), Σ: (768,), Vh: (768, 768)`. Rank-r approximation `(U[:, :r] · Σ[:r]) @ Vh[:r, :]` replaces the weight; bias unchanged. **No-retrain rank-r sweep**: r=5 → PPL=60.991 (+7.10, +13.17%; energy=0.0745); r=22 → **59.683** (+5.79, +10.74%; energy=0.1414); r=50 → 58.634 (+4.74, +8.79%; energy=0.2249); r=100 → 57.306 (+3.41, +6.33%; energy=0.3483). The no-retrain curve is monotone-decreasing in r as expected; rank-22 no-retrain (59.68) is **WELL ABOVE** the CONFIRMED ≤ 55.0 threshold and ALSO at the REFUTED ≥ 60.0 boundary (just under by 0.32 PPL). **Retrain at r=22** (3 seeds, joint Adam LR=1e-4, 4000 steps, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 17,664 trainable params = 22×768 (B factor) + 768 (bias); A=(U[:, :22]·Σ[:22]) frozen as buffer; CompressedMLP-style wrapper that recomputes `W = A@B` each forward and applies HF Conv1D semantics `x @ W + b`): seed 42 best=54.4673@step 3800 (init=59.6834); seed 137 best=54.4405@step 3600 (init=59.6834); seed 2024 best=54.4984@step 3900 (init=59.6834). **best_mean = 54.4687 ± 0.0237**, max=54.4984, min=54.4405. Init PPL deterministic at 59.6834 across all 3 seeds (matches noretrain[r=22]=59.683 — script-internal assert passed). All 3 best at step ≥3600 (still descending at the budget cap). Per-seed restored best matches recorded best within 1e-3 (script-internal assert passed). **Verdict logic** (item 280 verbatim): CONFIRMED iff no-retrain ≤ 55.0 OR retrain best_mean ≤ 54.5 → SECOND clause passes by 0.031 PPL (54.4687 < 54.5); REFUTED iff no-retrain ≥ 60.0 AND retrain ≥ 58.0 → FAILS (59.683 < 60.0 AND 54.47 < 58.0); **CONFIRMED**. **Geometric reading**: (i) **Weight SVD ≠ activation SVD**. Cycle 267 measured `eff_rank(c_proj_11 OUTPUT) = 22.49` on the activations in R^768. The WEIGHT matrix is full-rank and the rank-22 weight approximation captures only **14.14% of the operator energy** — not 70% as one might expect from cycle 267's "top-22 covers the signal" finding. The two are different objects: cycle 267's eff_rank measures concentration of activations in the OUTPUT space (driven by both the weight and the input distribution from c_fc), whereas weight SVD measures the principal directions of the linear MAP. The mismatch is the headline finding: rank-22 weight SVD costs 5.79 PPL no-retrain, NOT the ≤1 PPL the brief predicted. (ii) **Adam recovery is large**: 59.68 → 54.47 = -5.22 PPL with only 17K trainable params. Block 11 alone, at the rank-22 weight bottleneck, recovers ~90% of the retrained-recipe quality of cycle 266's 2-block K=512 [1,4] (54.47 vs 43.85; cycle 266 has 788K trainable params vs 17K here, 46× the budget for 10.6 PPL improvement). The rank-22 factor B is a remarkably efficient bottleneck IF Adam is allowed to retrain it. (iii) **The "structurally cheap" hypothesis is HALF right**: cheap WITH retraining (-5.22 PPL of free recovery for 17K params), NOT cheap without (rank-22 weight is a +5.79 PPL hit, similar to a fully-zeroed block 11). The geometric story is that cycle 267's eff_rank-22 OUTPUT subspace is real but is **not aligned with the top-22 right singular vectors of the weight matrix** — the activations live in a 22-dim subspace of R^768 that is created by the joint action of c_fc (3072-dim hidden) AND c_proj (3072→768), not by the top-22 directions of c_proj alone. (iv) **Rank-cost curve at block 11**: r=5 → +7.10, r=22 → +5.79, r=50 → +4.74, r=100 → +3.41. The curve is concave-down: doubling r from 22 to 50 saves 1.05 PPL; from 50 to 100 saves 1.33 PPL. There's no sharp inflection at r=22 — the eff_rank=22.49 is NOT visible in the weight-SVD rank-cost curve. To match cycle 267's claim ("the OUTPUT subspace is rank-22"), one would need to project c_proj.weight onto the activation top-22 subspace (V_out from cycle 267), which is a different experiment than the literal weight SVD this cycle ran. (v) **Cross-cycle**: 17K-param recipe at 54.47 PPL is on a NEW row of the Pareto frontier — between cycle 269 (9K, 91.61) and cycle 268 (200K, 55.95). The (17,664, 54.47) point dominates cycle 268 at K=64 4-block on PPL (54.47 vs 55.95) AT 8.7% of the trainable params — block 11 single-MLP rank-22 retrain is the lowest-param recipe that beats float by less than 0.6 PPL gap. Compared to float (53.895), gap is +0.574 PPL. (vi) **Energy fractions reveal the weight matrix is well-conditioned**: top-100 SVs only carry 34.83% of energy, and even r=768 (full rank) is needed for full recovery — the weight is closer to "uniform" than "rank-concentrated", whereas the OUTPUT covariance (cycle 267) was sharply concentrated (top-1 = 48.95%). This is the cleanest evidence that operator-level low-rank ≠ activation-level low-rank for transformer FFN c_proj. (vii) **Open follow-ups (NOT in queue)**: (a) Project c_proj_11.weight onto cycle 267's saved V_out top-22 (the activation-side subspace) instead of weight-SVD top-22 — predict the no-retrain PPL would be ≤55 (matching the brief's original prediction), because that IS the subspace cycle 267 measured. (b) Joint cycle 270 + cycle 266: replace block 11 c_proj with the rank-22 retrained version AND apply cycle 266 K=512 [1,4] simultaneously — predict best_mean ≤ 44.0 (additive of the two recipes since they touch disjoint blocks). (c) Item 283 (P1) hetero K [1,4,7,11] with K_11=22 — same hypothesis at the cascade level. (d) Whether the LR=1e-4 / 4000 step horizon is enough for B22+bias retrain to fully converge — best_step is 3600-3900 across seeds, suggesting still-descending trajectories; longer horizon may close the 0.6 PPL float gap. (viii) **Closing item 280**: the verdict is technically CONFIRMED on the retrain clause (54.469 just under 54.5) but the no-retrain clause is FAR from confirmed (59.68 vs 55.0). The literal "rank-22 weight SVD pays for itself" hypothesis is mostly wrong; the "rank-22 subspace + retrain pays for itself" hypothesis is correct by a hair. The geometric headline is that cycle 267's OUTPUT eff_rank does NOT translate directly into a free weight-SVD bottleneck — operator vs activation rank are different objects, and the 17K-param rank-22 retrain recipe is a real win but on Adam's account, not the SVD's.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/block11_lowrank_cycle270.py`, `autoresearch/plans/cycle_270.md`, `autoresearch/briefs/cycle_270.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_270_block11_lowrank.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_270_summary.md`, `autoresearch/logs/cycle_270_run.log`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_270_manifest.txt`

### Cycle 269

279. **K=1 6-block extended budget (16000 steps) — does cycle 240's catastrophe budget out?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 269)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED (structural)**. Trivial N_STEPS=4000→16000 + EVAL_EVERY=100→400 swap on cycle 240 template. BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8], K=1, W_out+b_out joint Adam LR=1e-4, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42,137,2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200]/eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Trainable per fit = 9,216 = 6 × (768 + 768). Wall ~18 min (under the 25 min cap; faster than the 4× cycle 240 estimate because the per-step MPS cost dominates over the per-eval cost and EVAL_EVERY=400 cuts evals from 40-per-fit at cycle 240's density to 40-per-fit at cycle 269's density — same total eval count, 4× fewer evals per train step). Init PPL deterministic = 203.246 (matches cycle 240 to 3 dp). **Per-seed best (n=3)**: seed42=91.657, seed137=91.613, seed2024=91.568, all best at step 16000 (still descending, no overfitting). **best_mean=91.6126 ± 0.0366**, max=91.657, min=91.568 — extraordinarily tight (std 0.04 PPL across seeds at 16k steps). **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff best_mean ≥ 90.0 → PASS by 1.61 PPL; REFUTED iff best_mean ≤ 80.0 → FAIL (91.61 > 80). Trajectory descent: step4000 96.886 (matches cycle 240 to 3 dp seed-by-seed: cycle 240 was 96.943/96.786/96.929 — cycle 269 step4000 is 96.943/96.786/96.929 IDENTICAL, then continues 92→91 over the next 12k steps). At step 4000 → 96.89, step 8000 → 93.7, step 12000 → 92.2, step 16000 → 91.6. Per-1000-step Δ: steps 0-4k → 26.6, 4-8k → 3.2, 8-12k → 1.5, 12-16k → 0.6. **Optimisation has not flatlined but is asymptotically slow**: 4× the budget bought only 5.27 PPL improvement, and the rate at step 16k is ~0.15 PPL per 1000 steps — extrapolating linearly to 32k would give ~91.6 - 2.4 ≈ 89.2 (still well above 80). **Geometric / cascade reading**: (i) Cycle 254's "extended budget is a noop" finding generalises to K=1 6-block in spirit but not in letter — there IS a small monotone improvement (5.27 PPL over 12k extra steps), so it is NOT literally a noop, but the rate of improvement is so slow that even 4× the budget cannot bring it within 30 PPL of any non-K=1 baseline. The "structural" verdict is on the *practical* axis: 9K trainable params on 6 blocks at K=1 cannot reach a useful PPL within any reasonable Adam horizon. (ii) The K=1 vs K=128 vs K=512 6-block gap is now mapped at 16k steps (cycle 269) and at 4k steps (cycles 240, 231, 221): K=1 16k=91.61, K=128 4k=67.56, K=512 4k=59.52. The K=1 vs K=512 gap is **+32.10 PPL** at the longer 16k horizon (vs +37.37 at 4k) — extending K=1's budget closes only 14% of the K=1-vs-K=512 gap. The bulk of the gap is structural (channel capacity), not optimisation horizon. (iii) Comparison to K=1 3-block (cycle 230 = 59.245): K=1 6-block 16k = 91.61 is **+32.37 PPL** above K=1 3-block. The "doubling block count at K=1" cost is +32 PPL — this is the cleanest measurement of the K=1 cascade-depth multiplicative penalty we have. The 3-block K=1 surface has enough mid-cascade slack to absorb single-channel projections at each block; the 6-block K=1 surface does not, and Adam cannot budget-its-way out. (iv) **Float gap**: 91.61 - 53.895 = +37.72 PPL — the K=1 6-block recipe lands ~70% above float at 16k steps. This is the worst gap-to-float of any retrained recipe in the K=1/K=128/K=512 × {3,4,5,6}-block sweep family. (v) **Cross-seed determinism**: std 0.037 PPL is the lowest cross-seed spread we have observed in any 6-block 3-seed sweep — suggests the loss surface at K=1 6-block is smooth (no multimodality), and Adam's trajectory from a deterministic init converges to essentially the same minimum regardless of seed. The seed variance at cycle 240 4k was ±0.071; at 16k it shrinks to ±0.037, consistent with the trajectories converging tighter as the run goes on. (vi) **Pareto frontier impact**: NONE — the K=1 6-block recipe at 91.61 PPL is dominated by every other recipe in the cascade family (cycle 268 K=64 4-block at 200K params + 55.95 PPL is strictly better on PPL; cycle 240 K=1 6-block at 9K params + 96.89 PPL is on the same 9K-param row but at worse PPL by 5.27, so cycle 269 dominates cycle 240). The 9K-param low-extreme row is now (9216, 91.61) instead of (9216, 96.89). (vii) **Open follow-ups (NOT in queue)**: (a) K=1 6-block at 32k steps would land near 89-90 by the linear-decline extrapolation — interesting only if we want to characterise the asymptote precisely; not worth running because already CONFIRMED structural. (b) K=2 or K=4 6-block at 4k or 16k steps would map the K-curve at 6 blocks at the very-low-K end (K=1=91.61, K=128=67.56, K=512=59.52 — the K=2 to K=64 wing is unmapped at 6 blocks). (c) Whether the Adam trajectory at K=1 6-block can be accelerated (higher LR with warmup, AdamW, second-order method) — but no prior cycle has shown LR > 1e-4 helps at this trainable-count scale, so likely a dead end. (viii) **Closing item 279**: cycle 240's catastrophe is *real and structural*. The K=1 6-block surface is in a bowl whose depth is bounded below by ~89-91 PPL no matter how long Adam runs. The K-vs-block-count cascade compounds multiplicatively at the K=1 endpoint: cycle 230 (K=1, 3 blocks) was within 0.27 PPL of cycle 221 (K=512, 6 blocks), but cycle 269 (K=1, 6 blocks) is 32 PPL above both. The K=1 endpoint cannot pay for cascade depth.
