### Cycle 268

278. **K=64 4-block sweep — extend cycle 257's K=64 to 4 blocks** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 268)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED** by 0.05 PPL margin. Trivial K=128→K=64 swap on cycle 265 template. SUBSETS=[[1,2,4,7], [1,4,7,8], [1,2,7,8]], K=64, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024} = 9 fits in 20.9 min wall (under the 30 min cap, no seed drop). Hyperparams identical to cycle 265: N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100 disjoint, SEQ_LEN=128, Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Trainable params per config = 199,680 (4 × (49,920 = 64×768 + 768)). Init PPLs deterministic per subset: [1,2,4,7]→78.258, [1,4,7,8]→78.873, [1,2,7,8]→79.628. **Per-subset best_mean (n=3)**: [1,2,4,7]=**56.0675 ± 0.0443** (seeds 56.125/56.060/56.017, best_step ≈3900), [1,4,7,8]=**55.9533 ± 0.0923** (56.017/56.020/55.823, best_step ≈3967), [1,2,7,8]=**57.2766 ± 0.1199** (57.350/57.372/57.108, best_step ≈3900). Float baseline = 53.895. **min over subsets = 55.9533 ([1,4,7,8])**, max = 57.2766 ([1,2,7,8]). **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff min ≤ 56.0 → PASS by 0.047 PPL; REFUTED iff all ≥ 65.0 → FAIL (max 57.28); **CONFIRMED**. **Geometric / cascade reading**: (i) **Subset ordering FLIPS at K=64**: [1,4,7,8] (55.95) < [1,2,4,7] (56.07) < [1,2,7,8] (57.28). At both K=128 (cycle 265) and K=512 (cycle 255) the ordering was [1,2,4,7] < [1,4,7,8] < [1,2,7,8]. The flip is small (0.11 PPL, ~1.2 σ on the [1,4,7,8] std) but the [1,2,7,8] subset is consistently last across all three K values — block 4 and the early-mid pair {2,4} matter for the larger-K subsets but at K=64 the contribution of "block 2 vs block 8" inverts in favour of block 8. Mechanically: K=64 forces each block to compress to a much smaller channel basis (≈8.3% of 768 vs 16.7% at K=128, 66% at K=512), and the late-block 8 absorbs more of its compression slack than block 2 does at K=64 — block 2's compression burden swamps block 8's at this K. (ii) **K-curve at 4 blocks** is now mapped: {K=64, 128, 512} = {55.95, 53.65, 49.61}. Per-K-halving cost: 512→128 = 4.04 PPL, 128→64 = 2.31 PPL. Concave-up holds (cost shrinks as K halves), exactly as predicted from the 5-block K-curve {62.18, 59.12, 53.83} where 512→128 = 5.29, 128→64 = 3.06. The 4-block curve is uniformly ~3.5 PPL flatter than the 5-block curve (cycles 245 K=512 5b: 53.83, cycle 251 K=128 5b: 59.12, cycle 257 K=64 5b: 62.18 vs cycle 268 K=64 4b: 55.95) — fewer blocks compress more cleanly at all K. (iii) **5→4 block transition at K=64 saves 6.23 PPL** (62.18 → 55.95). Compare 5→4 at K=128 = 5.47 PPL (cycle 251 → cycle 265), 5→4 at K=512 = 4.22 PPL (cycle 245 → cycle 255). The marginal benefit of dropping a 5th block GROWS as K shrinks: ΔPPL/dropped block = {K=512: 4.22, K=128: 5.47, K=64: 6.23}. The K=64 regime gets the most relative benefit from having fewer blocks because each compressed block at K=64 is the most damaging — removing one helps the most. (iv) **Delta vs float = +2.06 PPL**: 4-block K=64 is 2 PPL ABOVE float (does NOT beat float). Compare cycle 265 K=128 4-block min = 53.65, which beat float by 0.25 PPL. Halving from K=128 to K=64 costs 2.31 PPL of float-margin. The "sub-300K-param recipe that beats float" prediction in the brief is REFUTED on the float-tie axis: 4-block K=64 at 200K trainable params lands 2 PPL above float. Cycle 257 5-block K=64 was at 62.18 (8.3 PPL above float); cycle 268 4-block K=64 closes most of that gap but does not cross it. (v) **Pareto frontier as of cycle 268** (param, PPL): cycle 266 K=512 2-block [1,4] (788K, 43.85) — lowest-PPL anchor; cycle 223 K=128 3-block [1,4,7] (297K, 48.99) — Pareto winner at low param count; **cycle 268 K=64 4-block [1,4,7,8] (200K, 55.95)** — new low-param anchor, 33% fewer params than cycle 223 but 6.97 PPL worse. The (200K, 55.95) point opens the LOW-param wing of the frontier. Cycle 265 K=128 4-block (396K, 53.65) is dominated by cycle 223 (297K, 48.99). Cycle 251 K=128 5-block (495K, 59.12) is dominated by cycle 268 (200K, 55.95) — fewer params AND better PPL. (vi) **Block 1 dominance holds**: every winning subset across cycles 255/265/266/268 includes block 1. The [1,2,7,8] subset (without block 4) is the worst at every K — block 4 is the second most load-bearing block after block 1. (vii) The 5-block K=64 init PPL deterministic was 84.X (cycle 257); 4-block K=64 init drops to 78.26-79.63 — the lstsq fit at K=64 is weaker than at K=128 (74-76 init at K=128) but the joint Adam recovery is stronger relative to init (init→best Δ ≈ 22 PPL at K=64 vs ≈ 21 PPL at K=128) — Adam compensates for the weaker init by ~1 extra PPL of headroom. (viii) **Open**: 1-block K=64? predict 56-58 (concave extrapolation suggests minimal further descent), and 2-block K=64 sweep at the cycle 266 [1,4] subset to extend the 2-block cascade to K=64. The K=64 frontier is now bracketed by 5-block (62.18) and 3-block (50.78) and 4-block (55.95) sits cleanly between them. **Hypothesis prediction**: best_mean ≤ 56.0; actual = 55.953. Predicted best_mean "between 49.61 and 62.18 weighted toward K=512" — actual sits 51% of the way from K=64 5-block to K=512 4-block, very close to the midpoint. Hypothesis CONFIRMED on the threshold but the "weighted toward K=512" part is slightly wrong — the K=64 4-block point sits at the midpoint, not the K=512 side.

### Cycle 267

277. **Block 11 c_proj output rank decomposition — what makes block 11 special at PPL?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 267)*
   - Concentration story holds. Block 11 c_proj output eff_rank = 22.49 (≤50 threshold); top-1 eigenvector carries 48.95% of variance, top-5 carries 68.18%, top-20 carries 74.87%. Block 5 comparison eff_rank = 179.19 (mid-block high-rank anti-pattern: top-1 = 24.62%, top-5 = 30.79%); block 1 eff_rank = 1.94 (near rank-1 operator: top-1 = 92.94%). The ordering block 1 (1.94) << block 11 (22.49) << block 5 (179.19) inverts the "mid-block structurally normal" picture: both earliest and latest blocks have concentrated c_proj outputs; the mid block is high-rank.
   - Alignment story REFUTED for all blocks. No top-5 V_mlp direction aligns with top-200 lm_head rows at >0.35; median of top-5 is ≤0.159 for all three blocks (block 1 = 0.159, block 5 = 0.130, block 11 = 0.123). Opening to ALL 50257 lm_head rows raises block 11's first two V_mlp max cos to 0.586 and 0.623, but only top 2/5 hit high values (3rd onward ≤0.24), median stays 0.239. PPL load of block 11 is NOT from reading into high-norm unembedding rows.
   - Operator-level V_wp (SVD of c_proj.weight) mirrors: block 11 top V_wp max cos 0.353 with top-200 lm_head rows, median top-5 = 0.147.
   - c_proj.weight top SV: block 1 = 28.71, block 5 = 27.62, block 11 = 60.99 (block 11's operator has ~2× overall scale); 20th SV ratios similar (≈3-4×).
   - Compression implication: block 11's ~22-dim concentration opens a targeted probe — compress block 11's OUTPUT side on a rank-22 (or rank-5, which already captures 68%) subspace. Block 5 is the opposite: distributed across ~179 dims, output-side low-rank compression will fail; must compress the 3072-dim INPUT side instead. Matches the 4-/2-block sweep pattern where block 5 is rarely in winning subsets (K=512 2-block min is [1,4]).

### Cycle 266

276. **2-block K=512 sweep — how far below float does the count-driven cascade go?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 266)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED**. Trivial SUBSETS swap on cycle 255 template. SUBSETS=[[1,4], [1,7], [4,7]], K=512, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024} = 9 fits in 22.2 min wall (under the 27 min budget, no seed drop). Hyperparams identical to cycle 255: N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100 disjoint, SEQ_LEN=128, Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Trainable params per config = 787,968 (2 × (393,984 = 512×768 + 768)). Init PPLs deterministic per subset: [1,4]→57.057, [1,7]→58.018, [4,7]→59.777. **Per-subset best_mean (n=3)**: [1,4]=**43.8525 ± 0.0273** (seeds 43.866/43.877/43.814, best_step ≈3900), [1,7]=**43.9048 ± 0.0508** (43.946/43.833/43.935, best_step ≈3933), [4,7]=**45.7703 ± 0.0164** (45.752/45.767/45.792, best_step ≈3867). Float baseline = 53.895. **min over subsets = 43.8525 ([1,4])**, max = 45.7703 ([4,7]). **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff min ≤ 45.0 → PASS by 1.15 PPL; REFUTED iff all ≥ 47.0 → FAIL ([4,7]=45.77 < 47.0); **CONFIRMED**. **Geometric / cascade reading**: (i) The count-driven cascade EXTRAPOLATES cleanly: 6→5 saves 5.69 (cycle 221→245), 5→4 saves 4.22 (245→255), 4→3 saves 3.05 (255→210), 3→2 saves **2.70 PPL** (210→266 min). The marginal benefit of dropping blocks SHRINKS as block count drops — the "save ~3-6 PPL per block" rule of thumb in the brief is correct on the upper end but the actual delta narrows monotonically (5.69 → 4.22 → 3.05 → 2.70). The cascade is **concave**: each successive block dropped buys less. Linear extrapolation 4-block 49.609 minus 4.22 = 45.39 would have been an over-prediction; the actual 43.85 is 1.54 PPL BETTER than that linear walk because the [1,4] subset (cycle 210's two best blocks of [1,4,7]) is the cleanest 2-block target. (ii) Subset ordering: **[1,4] (43.85) < [1,7] (43.90) < [4,7] (45.77)**. The early-block hypothesis from item 276 ([1,4] should win if cycle 255 ranking generalises) is CONFIRMED — but the [1,4] vs [1,7] gap is only 0.05 PPL (within seed std), whereas [4,7] is 1.92 PPL worse. **Block 1 is load-bearing**: subsets that include block 1 ([1,4] and [1,7]) are nearly identical and both ~1.9 PPL better than [4,7]. This matches cycles 255/265 — block 1 is in every winning subset across K and across block counts. The fact that [1,4] and [1,7] are within 0.05 PPL means at 2 blocks the *second* block choice between {4, 7} is essentially free as long as block 1 is in. (iii) Delta vs cycle 210 K=512 3-block [1,4,7] (46.556): **−2.704 PPL** — dropping block 7 from cycle 210 saves 2.70 PPL, dropping block 4 saves 2.65 PPL. Both 2-block subsets [1,4] and [1,7] strictly improve on the 3-block [1,4,7], which is geometrically interesting: the 3-block recipe is suboptimal vs the best 2-block at K=512. The third block (whichever is dropped from [1,4,7]) is doing harm at the joint Adam fit, NOT helping. (iv) Delta vs cycle 255 K=512 4-block min (49.609): **−5.757 PPL** — dropping 2 of the 4 blocks ([2, then either 4 or 7]) saves ~5.8 PPL. Per dropped block: 2.88 PPL. (v) Delta vs float (53.895): **−10.042 PPL** — the 2-block K=512 [1,4] recipe beats float by **10 PPL**, which is the largest float-margin we have at K=512 (cycle 255 4-block was -4.29, cycle 210 3-block was -7.34). The hypothesis predicted "≥8.9 PPL below float" — actual margin is 10.04, beating the prediction. (vi) Trainable params per fit = 787,968 (~50% MORE than cycle 255 4-block at 1.58M? — actually 1.58M / 787,968 = 2.0×, so 4-block has 2× the trainable params for 5.8 PPL WORSE quality). The 2-block K=512 recipe is a genuine **Pareto winner over both cycle 210 3-block and cycle 255 4-block**: fewer trainable params AND better PPL than either. **Pareto frontier as of cycle 266** (param, PPL): cycle 223 K=128 3-block [1,4,7] (297K, 48.99); **cycle 266 K=512 2-block [1,4] (788K, 43.85)** — new lowest-PPL Pareto winner; cycle 255 K=512 4-block [1,2,4,7] (1.58M, 49.61) — DOMINATED by cycle 266; cycle 210 K=512 3-block [1,4,7] (1.18M, 46.56) — DOMINATED by cycle 266; cycle 251 K=128 5-block (495K, 59.12). The (788K, 43.85) point is the new low-PPL anchor of the K=512 cascade. (vii) The "compress only N blocks at K=512" recipe family is now mapped: 6-block 59.5 → 5-block 53.8 → 4-block 49.6 → 3-block 46.6 → **2-block 43.85**. Whether the cascade continues at 1-block is the obvious follow-up — predict 1-block K=512 [1] or [4] would land at ~42.5-43 PPL (concave-down extrapolation suggests the marginal benefit at the 1-block step is 1.0-1.5 PPL). **Open questions** (NOT in queue): (a) Is 1-block K=512 still a good recipe? Predict ~42.5 PPL; would close the cascade. (b) The [1,4] vs [1,7] tie at K=512 may not survive at smaller K — at K=128 the K-bandwidth penalty was non-uniform across blocks (cycle 265). (c) Block 1 dominance is now the cleanest cross-K cross-count finding: every winning subset includes block 1. Why? (d) The ratio (PPL improvement per block dropped) / (param increase per block kept) is favourable at small block counts — more blocks at smaller K is dominated by fewer blocks at larger K throughout the explored range.

275. **K=128 4-block sweep — extend the K=128 curve and find the Pareto winner** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 265)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. Trivial K=512→K=128 swap on cycle 255 template. SUBSETS=[[1,2,4,7], [1,4,7,8], [1,2,7,8]] × 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024} = 9 fits in 21.0 min wall (well under the 30 min cap, no seed drop). Hyperparams identical to cycle 255: K=128, N_CALIB=200/N_EVAL=100 disjoint, SEQ_LEN=128, Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, MPS, GPT-2 small, wikitext-2 val. Trainable params per config = 396,288 (4 × (98,688 = K·768 + 768)). Init PPLs deterministic per subset across seeds: [1,2,4,7]→74.216, [1,4,7,8]→76.107, [1,2,7,8]→75.862. **Per-subset best_mean (n=3)**: [1,2,4,7]=**53.647 ± 0.066** (seeds 53.695/53.693/53.553, best_step≈3933), [1,4,7,8]=**53.732 ± 0.098** (53.771/53.827/53.598, best_step≈3900), [1,2,7,8]=**54.488 ± 0.137** (54.594/54.575/54.294, best_step≈3900). Float baseline = 53.895. **min over subsets = 53.647 ([1,2,4,7])**, max = 54.488. **Verdict logic**: CONFIRMED iff min ≤ 53.0 → FAIL by 0.647 PPL; REFUTED iff all ≥ 56.0 → FAIL (max is 54.488); **INCONCLUSIVE** (53.647 sits 21.6% of the way from CONFIRMED to REFUTED in the (53.0, 56.0) band). **Geometric / Pareto reading**: (i) Linear K=128 extrapolation from cycle 231 K=128 6-block (67.562) → cycle 251 K=128 5-block (59.119) predicted ~50.7 at 4 blocks; the actual 53.647 is **2.95 PPL above the linear prediction** — the K=128 cascade is **sub-linear** at low block counts (the marginal benefit of dropping from 5 to 4 blocks at K=128 is 5.47 PPL, vs 8.4 PPL/block at the 6→5 step). The K-penalty does NOT vanish at 4 blocks the way it does in the K=512 cascade (where 5-block 53.826 → 4-block 49.609 saves 4.22 PPL). (ii) Delta vs cycle 255 K=512 4-block min (49.609): **+4.04 PPL** — K=128 buys ~4 PPL of damage at 4 blocks, much larger than the 0.7 PPL ratio implied by cycle 251 vs cycle 245 at 5 blocks (5.29 PPL gap). The K-bandwidth penalty is structural and non-monotone in block count: smaller block counts have LARGER K-bandwidth penalties because each compressed block carries more of the recipe's load. (iii) Delta vs cycle 251 K=128 5-block (59.119): **−5.47 PPL**, only marginally larger than the 5→4 K=512 jump (4.22 PPL). The "compress fewer blocks" lever still works at K=128 but **less than at K=512** — consistent with the sub-linear cascade reading. (iv) Delta vs cycle 223 K=128 3-block [1,4,7] (48.989): **+4.66 PPL** — at K=128, going from 3-block to 4-block COSTS 4.66 PPL even though the 4-block subset includes [1,4,7] plus block 2 or 7-8. Adding block 2 to the K=128 cascade is harmful, NOT helpful — block 2 at K=128 cannot pull its weight, and the joint Adam fit is dominated by blocks 1, 4, 7 doing the work in cycle 223. The 3-block K=128 [1,4,7] recipe is the K=128 floor for *this* subset family. (v) Delta vs float (53.895): the min subset [1,2,4,7] **beats float by 0.25 PPL** and [1,4,7,8] beats float by 0.16 PPL; only [1,2,7,8] is above float (+0.59). All 3 subsets are within ±1 PPL of float — the **K=128 4-block recipe is float-tied at 396K params** (vs cycle 251 K=128 5-block 495K params for 59.12 PPL, +5.22 above float). This is a real Pareto improvement at the parameter axis but NOT at the PPL axis vs cycle 255 K=512 4-block (1.58M params, 49.61 PPL, **−4.29 below float**). (vi) Pareto frontier as of cycle 265: cycle 223 K=128 3-block [1,4,7] = (297K, 48.99); cycle 265 K=128 4-block [1,2,4,7] = (396K, 53.65); cycle 251 K=128 5-block = (495K, 59.12); cycle 255 K=512 4-block [1,2,4,7] = (1.58M, 49.61). The (297K, 48.99) and (1.58M, 49.61) are the two Pareto winners; cycle 265 is dominated by cycle 223 on both axes (more params for worse PPL). **The naive linear extrapolation prediction "K=128 4-block ≈ 50.7" was wrong** — the K-bandwidth-vs-block-count cascade is non-linear and the K=128 surface gets WORSE per block when block count drops, the opposite of K=512. (vii) Subset ordering: [1,2,4,7] < [1,4,7,8] < [1,2,7,8] — exactly the same subset ranking as cycle 255 K=512 — early-mid blocks beat late-mid blocks for both K. The cycle 255 ranking generalises across K. The 0.84 PPL gap between [1,2,4,7] and [1,2,7,8] at K=128 is similar to the 0.60 gap at K=512 — the subset choice contributes ~1 PPL of variance in both K regimes. **Open questions** (NOT in queue): (a) Why does K=128 plateau ~53.6 across 4 blocks while cycle 223 K=128 3-block hits 48.99? Hypothesis: block 2 specifically is hard to compress at K=128 (it would also explain why [1,2,7,8] is the worst 4-block subset at K=128 — it has block 2 and lacks block 4 which might compensate). Test: cycle 223-style K=128 3-block with [1,2,7] and [2,4,7] would isolate block 2's K=128 cost. (b) The cycle 251 K=128 5-block 59.12 result includes block 8; the cycle 265 4-block subsets that include block 8 are [1,4,7,8]=53.73 and [1,2,7,8]=54.49 — block 8 is mildly worse than the alternative early-mid block at K=128. Together (a)+(b) suggest the K=128 cascade is *block-specific*, not just count-driven. (c) The K=128 4-block result is **float-tied** which is itself useful — at 396K params we have a recipe that loses essentially nothing vs float, just not the −4 PPL margin of K=512.

### Cycle 264

274. **Pullback curvature κ_l on per-block partial swaps — heatmap of where the bump is load-bearing** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 264)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. For each k ∈ {0..11}, installed `ReluPlusBleak(b_leak[k])` (cycle 258 recipe: per-channel `b_leak[k] = E_x[gelu(z) − relu(z)]` over the standard 200×128 wikitext-2 val calib slice, seed 42, MPS) at GPT-2 small block k while leaving the other 11 blocks GELU, collected the residual stream trajectory via `aleph.pullback_geometry.collect_residual_trajectory`, and computed κ_l for layers 1..11 via `compute_curvature(reduce='mean')`. Built the 12×11 Δκ matrix Δκ[k, l] = κ_swap_at_k[l] − κ_baseline[l] (rows = swap location k, columns = κ index l = layer l+1). Baseline (all-GELU) PPL = 45.064 (matches cycle 258 exactly); per-swap PPL deltas range from +0.022 (k=0) to +1.048 (k=4). Post-restore PPL = 45.064 (round-trip clean). **Δκ matrix exhibits a strict causal lower-triangle pattern**: swap_at_k only perturbs κ at indices l ≥ k − 1 (entries with l < k − 1 are bit-identical zero because the residual stream up to and including block k − 1 is unchanged when the ReLU patch is at block k). Of 132 entries, 46 are nonzero. **Per-swap ‖Δκ‖₁** (descending): k=2 → 1.78e-3, k=3 → 1.22e-3, k=1 → 6.0e-4, k=4 → 4.36e-4, k=0 → 4.19e-4, k=5 → 1.49e-4, k=6 → 4.32e-5, k=7 → 1.14e-5, k=8 → 6.11e-6, k=9 → 3.79e-6, k=11 → 2.48e-6, k=10 → 1.04e-6 — early-middle blocks (k = 1..4) dominate disruption by ~2 orders of magnitude over late blocks. **Diagonal band statistics** (band = κ indices {k−1, k, k+1} ∩ {0..10}): max|Δκ|_diag = 8.00e-4 at (k=2, l=2), mean diag = 1.15e-4. **Off-diagonal**: 100 entries, 54 are bit-zero (the strict lower triangle), nonzero off-diag mean = 2.15e-5, nonzero off-diag median = 1.24e-6. The diag/offdiag ratio is `inf` (median offdiag = 0 because of the causal zeros), so clause 1 trivially passes — but the mean ratio (1.15e-4 / 9.89e-6) ≈ 11.6× and the diag-vs-nonzero-offdiag mean ratio ≈ 5.4× both confirm the diagonal band IS materially hotter. **Late-layer monotonicity (clause 2): 0/4 columns pass**. For l ∈ {7, 8, 9, 10} the column over k = 0..6 is NOT monotonically non-decreasing as k decreases — e.g., l=7 column is [1.0e-6, 1.4e-6, 2.8e-6, 4.7e-6, 1.9e-6, 9.7e-6, 1.9e-6], oscillating with no clear amplification trend; l=10 column is [6.5e-9, 2.7e-7, 1.6e-7, 5.1e-8, 3.6e-7, 2.4e-7, 8.6e-8] — a factor of 50× spread but non-monotonic. **REFUTED clause** (`diag/offdiag<1.0` OR `max_diag<median_offdiag`) does NOT trigger. **Verdict: INCONCLUSIVE** — the diagonal band is real (clause 1 substantively passes once you exclude the causal zeros), but the hypothesis's "downstream amplification, monotone in k" half is empirically FALSE: swap_at_k=0 does NOT propagate more strongly to layer 10 than swap_at_k=6 does. The "where the bump is load-bearing" question has a clean answer at the *local* depth (the bump matters most when inserted at blocks 1-4, peak at k=2 with ‖Δκ‖₁ = 1.78e-3), but the *downstream propagation* tail is structureless at the κ resolution. **Geometric reading**: (i) the strict lower-triangle pattern is a sanity check that the experiment is causally correct: a residual swap at block k cannot affect κ_l for l < k. (ii) The L1 ranking k=2 ≫ k=3 > k=1 > k=4 > k=0 ≫ k=5..11 puts the geometric load-bearing zone at blocks 1-4, consistent with cycle 258's finding that the GELU bump κ peaks at layers 2-4 (κ_GELU = 2.23e-3, 2.22e-3, 1.85e-3, 1.09e-3 at l = 2, 3, 4, 5) — swapping the activation at exactly the blocks that feed those high-κ layers produces the largest κ shift. Block 0 has rank 5 in disruption despite being the "earliest" because κ_0 (layer 1) is small (5.87e-5) and the κ structure at blocks 0-1 is just developing. (iii) The fact that swap_at_k=11 (last block) has L1 = 2.48e-6 — 700× smaller than swap_at_k=2 — and only perturbs κ at l=10 (the only κ index that depends on block 11's output) directly confirms the causal structure. (iv) The lack of monotone downstream amplification REFUTES the "early swaps have systematically more downstream impact" intuition: the propagated κ shift at l=10 from swap_at_k=0 is 6.5e-9 (essentially zero), while from swap_at_k=4 it is 3.6e-7 — *late-middle* swaps have more downstream impact than the very earliest swap, because the propagated perturbation must accumulate enough magnitude through 6+ blocks to register, and a single block 0 swap with a self-correcting b_leak does not generate enough perturbation to register at layer 10. (v) The cycle 173 single-block C3 PPL ranking (block 4 max +5.91%, block 11 +4.02%) does NOT cleanly track the κ-disruption ranking found here (k=2, k=3, k=1, k=4, k=0 are the top 5 for κ but cycle 173 found block 4 max for PPL). The κ profile and the PPL profile are measuring different things: κ is sensitive to *trajectory bending* under G, PPL is sensitive to the final output distribution. The fact that block 11 dominates cycle 173's PPL ranking but is the 11th-most disruptive in κ is the cleanest illustration: late-block PPL damage is from direct loss-of-output-channel structure, NOT from trajectory geometry. **Follow-ups (NOT in queue)**: (a) compute the analog matrix using `‖Δx^l‖_G` (per-layer residual stream displacement, not curvature) — this would lower-bound the propagation amplitude and is easier to interpret as a wave equation; (b) measure the same matrix with the un-corrected ReLU (no b_leak) to see whether the b_leak first-moment correction is what kills the downstream propagation (cycle 263 found the b_leak smoothing washes out other ReLU vs GELU distinctions); (c) compute the diag/offdiag ratio after restricting off-diagonal to only the non-causal entries (i.e., l ≥ k − 1 strictly) which would give a ratio of 5.4× — passing clause 1 with margin to spare and tightening the verdict logic for any future re-run.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/per_block_swap_curvature_cycle264.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_264_per_block_swap_curvature.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_264_summary.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_264_swap_kappa_heatmap.png`, `autoresearch/logs/cycle_264_run.log`

### Cycle 263

273. **Local linearity defect ε_l(x, h) = ‖f(x+h) − f(x) − J(x)h‖_G / ‖h‖²_G — sectional second-derivative of GELU vs ReLU FFN block** *(REFUTED — cycle 263)*
   - Result: **REFUTED**. 64 calib tokens (token positions 0..63 of the first wikitext-2 val sequence in the 200×128 cycles 218/258/260 loader, seed 42) × 32 isotropic Gaussian unit directions × 4 ε scales {0.01, 0.03, 0.10, 0.30} × ‖x‖_G × 12 blocks × 2 σ ∈ {GELU, ReLU+b_leak}. Vectorised: per (l, σ, token) compute J = W_fc · diag(σ'(z)) · W_proj once, f(x) once, then batch all 128 perturbed inputs through one MLP forward → ~40 s wall total. b_leak per block = E_x[gelu(z) − relu(z)] over the full 25,600-token calib slice (cycle 258 recipe). All metrics computed under G = U^T U via `pullback_norm`. **n = 2,048 (token, direction) pairs per (l, σ, scale)**, trainable_params = 0. **Per-block ratios at smallest scale (ε = 0.01·‖x‖_G)**: median ε_G/ε_R ranges over the 12 blocks: 0.838, 0.748, 0.911, 0.971, 1.006, 1.036, 1.055, 1.082, 1.077, 1.115, 1.120, 1.127 (block 0..11). p99 ratio ε_R/ε_G: 1.202, 1.218, 1.101, 1.019, 1.016, 0.993, 1.025, 1.021, 0.968, 0.987, 0.987, 0.964 (block 0..11). **Verdict logic**: clause A (median ε_G/ε_R ≥ 5 in ≥3 of blocks 0..4) → **0/5 — FAIL** (max ratio in early band is 1.006 at block 4, three orders of magnitude below the 5× CONFIRMED threshold). Clause B (p99 ε_R/ε_G ≥ 1 in ≥3 of blocks 0..4) → 5/5 PASS (1.20, 1.22, 1.10, 1.02, 1.02), but tightly clustered around 1.0 — the early-band ReLU tail is *barely* above the GELU tail, not the dramatic hyperplane-crossing spikes the hypothesis predicted. **REFUTED clause** (all 12 blocks: median G/R ratio ∈ [0.5, 2] AND p99 G/R ratio ∈ [0.5, 2]) → **TRIGGERED**: median ratios span [0.748, 1.127], p99 G/R ratios span [0.821, 1.034] — every single (l, ratio) pair sits inside the [0.5, 2] tolerance band. **Verdict: REFUTED**. **Geometric reading**: (i) the per-channel activation function — GELU vs ReLU+b_leak — is essentially geometrically interchangeable for the local linearity defect of the *full block MLP function* f(x) = c_proj(σ(c_fc(x))). The ε_defect ratios are within 13% at every block (median) and within 22% (p99), meaning swapping σ produces a 10-20% perturbation of the per-direction curvature, NOT the 5× / hyperplane-spike contrast the hypothesis predicted. (ii) The p99 ε_R IS systematically slightly above p99 ε_G in blocks 0..4 (+1-22%), so the predicted "ReLU has fatter tails from hyperplane crossings" effect is *qualitatively* present but quantitatively trivial — the b_leak first-moment-corrected ReLU does NOT exhibit dramatic kink-spikes because the b_leak term smooths the per-channel offset. (iii) The hypothesis's "GELU is everywhere-curved AND ReLU is mostly-flat-with-spikes" picture is FALSE for the *block-function* curvature: when you factor in the c_fc and c_proj linear maps wrapping the elementwise nonlinearity, the resulting f(x): R^768 → R^768 has nearly the same per-direction local-quadratic-form magnitude under either σ. The W_up · diag(σ') · W_down composition is dominated by the *linear envelope* (W_up, W_down) and not by the choice of σ — exactly the "REFUTED" branch's stated alternative ("W_up stretches dwarf the elementwise nonlinearity contribution"). (iv) **Scale dependence**: median ε(ε_scale) for both σ types DECREASES monotonically with ε_scale at every block (GELU block 0: 0.146 → 0.054 → 0.027 → 0.012; GELU block 11: 0.804 → 0.585 → 0.253 → 0.093). For a *purely quadratic* f(x), ε_defect = const (independent of ‖h‖). The observed ~12-13× decrease across the 30× scale range means the residual ‖f(x+h) − f(x) − J(x)h‖_G grows ~LINEARLY in ‖h‖ rather than QUADRATICALLY — the higher-order Taylor terms are NOT dominating; the residual is dominated by truncation/discretisation error scaling slower than ‖h‖². The sub-quadratic scaling holds for BOTH GELU and ReLU+b_leak with nearly identical slopes — another way of saying the activation choice is not the controlling factor. (v) **Block 11 dominates magnitude** (ε ~10× higher than blocks 1-9 at every scale, both σ types): the last block has the most curvature because c_proj maps directly into the residual-stream input of LN_f / lm_head and is structurally different from intermediate blocks. Block 0 also has elevated ε relative to blocks 1-9 (the first block is operating on the post-embedding distribution, which differs from the deep residual stream geometry). (vi) The cycle 260 finding that ΔJ has medium rank (~335/768) is consistent with the cycle 263 finding that the full block-function curvature is σ-insensitive: the operator-level *derivative disagreement* between GELU and ReLU is medium-rank but distributed (cycle 260), and when integrated into the block function f, the disagreement averages out across the 3072-channel intermediate space leaving a near-identical per-direction curvature signature (cycle 263). **The hypothesis was geometrically correct *at the activation function level* (gelu''(z) ≠ 0 everywhere; relu''(z) is a delta at z = 0) but the block-function context — c_fc on the input, c_proj on the output, b_leak on the offset — completely washes out the elementwise distinction.** Follow-ups (NOT in queue): (a) re-run on the *un-corrected* ReLU (no b_leak) to test whether the b_leak smoothing is what kills the fat-tail prediction; (b) measure the per-block per-direction ε for f(x) = σ(c_fc(x)) only (skipping c_proj) to isolate where the activation choice matters; (c) measure the linearity defect of c_fc(x) → c_proj(σ(c_fc(x))) at the *channel level* (R^3072) rather than the block-output level (R^768) — the σ disagreement should be visible there because c_proj's averaging hasn't happened yet.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/local_linearity_defect_cycle263.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_263_local_linearity_defect.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_263_summary.md`, `autoresearch/logs/cycle_263_run.log`

### Cycle 262

268. **K=1 + c_fc bias trainable on 3 blocks — does the c_fc bias become useful at the smallest K?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 262)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. BLOCKS=[1,4,7], K=1, FullCompressedMLP-style trainable surface = 3 × (W_out 768 + b_out 768 + b_fc 1) = **4,611 trainable params** (the brief said "= 2,313" but the per-block count of `768 + 1 + 1 = 770` in the brief omits the 768 entries of W_out — the actual surface is 4,611 with W_out included). N_CALIB=200, N_EVAL=100, SEQ_LEN=128, disjoint splits, Adam LR=1e-4, 4000 steps, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore. Per-seed init PPL **71.409** for all 3 seeds (deterministic topk+lstsq init from a fixed-seed model). Per-seed best PPL: seed 42=59.098, seed 137=59.165, seed 2024=59.130. **best_mean = 59.131 ± 0.027**, all best at step 4000 (still improving when training stopped). Final per-block b_fc values (post-training): block 1 ≈ +0.39 to +0.43, block 4 ≈ +0.35 to +0.36, block 7 ≈ +1.166 (very stable across seeds — block 7 b_fc moves the most, +0.18 from the topk init since the original c_fc bias for that channel was 0.984). **Delta vs cycle 230 K=1 3-block W_out-only baseline (59.245): −0.114 PPL** (≈0.2% improvement, 4× tighter than the seed std). Verdict thresholds: CONFIRMED ≤ 58.5 (would need −0.745 PPL drop), REFUTED ≥ 59.5 (−0.255 PPL margin from current). best_mean = 59.131 sits squarely in the (58.5, 59.5) inconclusive band, **31% of the way from REFUTED to CONFIRMED** — the bias contributes ~0.1 PPL (well below the ~0.745 hypothesised threshold), but ~3× the seed std, so it is *not* literally a noop, just a small improvement that does NOT clear the "bias matters at small K" bar. **Geometric reading**: (i) the b_fc parameter DID move at all 3 blocks (a frozen b_fc would have stayed at the topk-slice values 0.0 / −0.04 / 0.984; the trained values 0.39 / 0.36 / 1.17 represent shifts of +0.4 / +0.4 / +0.18), so the optimizer found a non-trivial direction for the bias and it consistently shifts the K=1 channel's GELU operating point UPWARD (positive shift on all 3 blocks, all 3 seeds — the chosen topk channel is always under-active relative to where Adam wants it for the joint task loss). (ii) The fact that all 3 seeds reach essentially the same b_fc values (std ≈ 0.02 on b_fc[7], 0.02 on b_fc[1], 0.003 on b_fc[4]) AND essentially the same best PPL (59.131 ± 0.027) shows the optimisation is converged and seed-stable — this is NOT seed noise. (iii) The 0.114 PPL gain is real but tiny, consistent with the cycle 213/241/248 finding that c_fc bias retraining is a near-noop on top of W_out, **and the K-starvation hypothesis fails**: even at K=1 where the W_out direction is most starved, the bias only contributes ~0.1 PPL because the GELU operating point on a single channel does not have enough leverage to compensate for what W_out alone cannot. (iv) The cycle 213 (K=512), cycle 241 (K=512 6-block), cycle 248 (K=128 6-block W_fc full) and now cycle 262 (K=1 3-block) span the full K range 512 → 128 → 1; bias retraining is **a uniformly-tiny effect across 4 endpoints**, meaning the W_out path absorbs the available degree of freedom regardless of K. The cleanest reading is that for this 3-block setup at the operating points sampled, bias and W_fc retraining are *both* noops on top of W_out+b_out, and the residual ~0.1 PPL the bias buys is the upper bound of how much pre-GELU shift can ever matter on top of a converged W_out. (v) For cycle 230 (K=1 3-block W_out-only) the init PPL was reported at 71.409 in this run, identical to cycle 262 — same calib slice, same init quality, same final converged W_out path. The 0.114 PPL difference is stable across the same data, the same seeds, the same step count, so it is a real bias contribution, just not large enough to confirm the "bias matters at small K" thesis. **Notes on the brief's claim of 2,313 trainable params**: the brief wrote "= 3 × (768 + 1 + 1) = 2,313" — the 768 in that decomposition is b_out only, and the W_out's 768 entries (1 channel × 768 output dim) are silently dropped from the count. The actual cycle 230 trainable count was 4,608 (3 × 1536), and cycle 262's count is 4,611 (3 × 1537), 3 scalars more (one per block). The 2,313 number does NOT reflect any code path. The verdict thresholds 58.5/59.5 are still the right gates because they were chosen relative to cycle 230's 59.245, which is what this cycle compares against. **Follow-ups (NOT in queue)**: (a) at K=2/4/8 the bias contribution might be slightly larger because the W_out absorption window is wider — but cycle 230's K=2 result (49.6 vs 59.2 for K=1) suggests the W_out direction is the dominant lever, and the 0.1 PPL bias gain at K=1 will only shrink at higher K. (b) the b_fc per-block values being uniformly positive shifts (all 3 blocks across all 3 seeds) suggests the topk-by-(act_std × W_proj_norm) channel selection picks under-active channels that benefit from a small positive shift to push more of their activation into the GELU's positive regime — a small structural finding about the channel-selection rule.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/k1_wfc_bias_cycle262.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_262_k1_wfc_bias.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_262_summary.md`, `autoresearch/logs/cycle_262_run.log`, `autoresearch/plans/cycle_262.md`, `autoresearch/briefs/cycle_262.md`

### Cycle 261

272. **W_down column geometry vs per-channel sign frequency σ_c — the rescue-note experiment** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 261)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. Single forward pass over the standard 200×128 wikitext-2 val calib slice (seed 42, 25,600 tokens) with c_fc input hooks on all 12 GPT-2 small blocks. Per block: `preact = ln2_out @ W_fc + b_fc` shape (25600, 3072) on CPU; `σ_c = (preact > 0).float().mean(dim=0)` ∈ R^{3072}; partition channels into mostly_neg (σ ∈ [0, 0.1]), balanced (σ ∈ [0.45, 0.55]), mostly_pos (σ ∈ [0.9, 1.0]); SVD of corresponding row subsets of `c_proj.weight` ∈ R^{3072×768} (each row is a W_down column in R^768); top-20 right singular vectors `V_g` of each subset; principal angles `cos = svdvals(V_pos.T @ V_neg)` reduced to degrees and median; null = 100 random shuffles preserving (n_pos, n_neg) per block, seeds 42..141. **σ_c per-block mean is 0.12-0.27 across blocks** — the GELU pre-activation distribution is left-shifted (most channels mostly-negative). **Group sizes per block (neg, balanced, pos)**: b0 (2057, 37, 145), b1 (1188, 489, 12), b2 (1692, 186, 6), b3 (1897, 80, 7), b4 (1562, 64, 8), b5 (1665, 72, 6), b6 (1618, 72, 7), b7 (1592, 54, 3), b8 (1587, 47, 4), b9 (1628, 37, 2), b10 (1277, 35, 5), b11 (1084, 49, 9). The mostly_pos group is **tiny in every block ≥ 1** (2-12 channels except block 0 with 145), so the rank of its row subset is mechanically capped at the group size. Block 1 is the only block with a sizeable balanced group (489) — most channels in deeper blocks live in the mostly_neg tail. **Median principal angle (pos vs neg, deg) per block**: b0=76.27, b1=71.87, b2=62.30, b3=81.08, b4=75.28, b5=76.99, b6=73.21, b7=73.59, b8=72.32, b9=21.35, b10=75.71, b11=76.96. Most blocks sit at ~70-80°, near orthogonality. Block 9 is an outlier at 21.4° but the mostly_pos group has only n=2 channels at block 9 (k_used=2), so the principal angle is from a 2-D subspace and the null std is correspondingly large (8.5°), giving z=−5.66 — this z is large but the underlying subspace is degenerate (rank 2). **Block 2 mostly_pos top-1 SV energy fraction = 0.5517 (rank_95 = 5 out of 6 svdvals)** — does NOT pass the 0.70 cycle 174 rank-1 threshold. The block-2 mostly_pos subspace has 6 channels with svdvals [6.82, 4.45, 2.82, 2.19, 1.84, 1.36] — the top-1 captures only ~55% of energy, more like rank ~3 than rank ~1. **Verdict logic**: clause (a) block-2 pos top-1 ≥ 0.70 → **FAIL** (0.5517). Clause (b) median angle blocks 1→11 strictly monotone decreasing AND total drop ≥ 30° → **FAIL** (not monotone — block 9 dips, block 11 recovers; total_drop = b1 − b11 = 71.87 − 76.96 = −5.09° i.e. angle slightly INCREASES through depth, opposite the prediction). Clause (c) all blocks 1..11 outside null mean ± 2σ → **FAIL** (only b1, b2, b3, b9 are outside 2σ; b4-b8, b10, b11 sit inside null). REFUTED clause (all 12 inside ±2σ) → **FAIL** (5 of 12 are outside null). Verdict INCONCLUSIVE because neither CONFIRMED nor REFUTED triggers. **Geometric reading**: (i) the mostly_pos group is structurally SMALL — across 12 blocks the average mostly_pos count is ~17 (skewed by block 0); ignoring block 0 the average is ~6. The "directions come from W_down columns" framing is technically defensible but only because the positive-regime sub-multiset has just a handful of channels per block. Block 2's positive-regime token-dependent signal being rank-1 (cycle 174 finding) is consistent with the size of the underlying column set (6 channels) but the columns themselves are NOT 1-D — they span a ~3-D subspace with top-1 energy 55%. Cycle 174's rank-1 must therefore arise from how those 6 columns are *combined by the activation magnitudes α_c*, not from a degenerate column geometry. (ii) The pos-vs-neg principal angles are mostly NEAR 70-80° even when small groups would suggest random alignment closer to ~70° (randomly drawn 7-D and 1500-D subspaces in R^768 have median angle ~75-80° from null) — most blocks sit AT the null mean, which is what the inside_2σ count is reporting. Blocks 1, 2, 3 are SIGNIFICANTLY BELOW their null (more orthogonal than random — z = −3.94, −4.18, +4.02), and block 3 is above (more aligned): the blocks 1-3 region has a real signal but it is *not monotone* — it oscillates above/below random. (iii) The hypothesis "early blocks pos⊥neg, late blocks pos≈neg" is REFUTED on the depth-monotonicity half: late blocks are NOT more aligned than early blocks. The hypothesis "block 2 mostly_pos is rank-1" is REFUTED: it is rank ~3 (top-1 = 0.55, top-3 = 0.84). Cycle 174's rank-1 finding stands but its column-geometry "explanation" does not. (iv) The "balanced" group is informational and large only at block 1 (489 channels), supporting the cycle 218 / cycle 260 / cycle 258 picture where block 1 is structurally distinct (the disagreement-zone explosion at b1 with rank_95 = 407 in cycle 260 maps to the same block having the largest balanced sigma_c group here). **Summary**: the W_down column geometry does NOT cleanly recover the cycle 174 rank-1 finding via sigma_c partitioning. Three blocks (1, 2, 3) DO show statistically significant departures from null but in different directions (b1, b2 more orthogonal, b3 more aligned), and the mostly_pos column subspace is too small (n ≤ 12 in 11/12 blocks) to test the rank claim cleanly. The rescue-note framing — "directions come from W_down columns" — is consistent with the data but is NOT load-bearing in the way the original argument suggested: the "pos columns" subspace is essentially empty (≤12 channels) in deep blocks, so almost all of the FFN output direction comes from the mostly_neg group and the small balanced group, not from a curated "positive direction" set. Follow-ups: (a) widen the σ thresholds (e.g. σ ∈ [0.7, 1.0]) so the mostly_pos group is large enough for stable rank tests; (b) use *weighted* group covariance (rows scaled by E[α_c | z_c > 0]) instead of uniform-row SVD — the rescue note's directions are *magnitude-weighted* combinations, not unit-row-PCA; (c) test the angle directly between the magnitude-weighted positive sub-direction and the magnitude-weighted negative sub-direction (a 1-D vs 1-D angle, no SVD subspace required).
   - Artifacts: `experiments/wdown_sign_geometry_cycle261.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_261_wdown_sign_geometry.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_261_summary.md`, `autoresearch/logs/cycle_261_run.log`

271. **Jacobian delta ΔJ_l(x) = J_GELU − J_ReLU per token, per block — operator-level GELU vs ReLU comparison** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 260)*
   - Result: **INCONCLUSIVE**. For 256 calib tokens × 12 blocks (first 2 sequences of the cycle 218/258/259 wikitext-2 val loader, seed 42, SEQ_LEN=128), computed operator-level ΔJ_l(x) = W_proj.T @ diag(σ'_GELU(z) − σ'_ReLU(z)) @ W_fc.T ∈ R^{768×768} via c_fc forward hook for ln2_out capture, `preact = ln2_out @ W_fc + b_fc`, fresh-leaf autograd for σ'_GELU(z) = `grad(F.gelu(z, approximate='tanh').sum(), z)`, and `σ'_ReLU(z) = (z > 0).float()`. delta_diag bounded elementwise in [−0.5, +0.5] (sanity: analytic bound at z=0 where GELU'=0.5, ReLU'=0). Per-token rank_95 via `torch.linalg.svdvals` (no U/V), per-block running-mean DJ_avg then SVD for top-20 left singular vectors (mode-1 subspace). **Per-block median rank_95 (256 tokens each): block 0=360, 1=407, 2=367, 3=334, 4=332, 5=332, 6=335, 7=338, 8=337, 9=335, 10=334, 11=332** — remarkably flat at ~335/768 (≈44% of full rank) across all 12 blocks, with only block 1 crossing the 400 mark (407). **Clause 1 (rank_95 ≤ 100 in ≥6 blocks): 0/12 — FAILS.** **REFUTED clause (rank_95 ≥ 400 in ≥6 blocks): 1/12 — NOT triggered.** The operator is "medium-rank" not "low-rank": ΔJ sits between Δ̃'s zeroth-order rank_95 ≤ 64 (cycle 217, 9/12 blocks) and J_GELU's rank_95 ~472 (cycle 195), closer to the full J side. The diagonal perturbation (σ'_GELU − σ'_ReLU) is concentrated near z = 0 but 57-74% of the 3072 channels have |delta_diag| > 0.1 (frac_large per block: 0.62, 0.74, 0.61, 0.56, 0.59, 0.57, 0.59, 0.59, 0.58, 0.57, 0.57, 0.59), so the diagonal is broadly non-sparse — that's the reason rank_95 stays ~335: the disagreement-zone is spread over hundreds of channels, not dozens. **Clause 2 alignment (mode-1 top-20 left SVs of DJ_avg vs c_fc reader of block l+1, top-5 principal cos sum ≥ 2.0 in ≥6 pairs): 10/11 pairs PASS — CLAUSE 2 PASSES STRONGLY.** Per-pair top-5 cos sums (random baseline 1.256 ± 0.045, cycle 218's random ~1.27 confirmed): 0→1=2.727, 1→2=2.902, 2→3=3.043, 3→4=2.893, 4→5=3.459, 5→6=3.487, 6→7=3.358, 7→8=3.239, 8→9=3.260, 9→10=2.563, 10→11=1.926. All 11 pairs clear the 3σ significance bar vs random; only 10→11 misses the 2.0 threshold (1.926 is still +15σ above the random mean). Peak alignment 3.487 at 5→6 — the middle blocks have the strongest mode-1 ↔ next-block c_fc reader alignment, consistent with cycle 258's finding that the GELU bump's geometric load concentrates in layers 3-5. The alignment finding is stronger than cycle 218's centred Δ̃ result (top-5 cos ~2.97) — ΔJ_l's mode-1 subspace is MORE targeted at the next block's reader than Δ̃_l itself. **Frobenius norm of per-token ΔJ grows 80 → 192 through depth** (median ||ΔJ||_F: block 0=80.7, 1=113.5, 2=95.5, 3=82.6, 4=88.9, 5=88.5, 6=99.7, 7=109.6, 8=122.3, 9=140.1, 10=162.1, 11=192.5) — monotone increasing after block 3, matching the general "operator discrepancy grows with depth" intuition. **DJ_avg top singular values 8-27** (σ₀ ranges 9.0-26.8 across blocks, block 11 σ₀=26.8 highest) shows the avg operator is dominated by a few stable directions even though each per-token ΔJ is medium-rank — the rank-reduction happens through the average, not through any single token. **Geometric reading**: the cycle 218 "bump writes into next block's c_fc reader" finding at the zeroth order (output Δ̃) survives and strengthens at the operator level (ΔJ), BUT the "low-dimensional operator" half of the hypothesis fails — ΔJ is medium-rank (~335/768) not low-rank, because the GELU-vs-ReLU derivative disagreement is spread over 60-70% of the 3072 intermediate channels rather than concentrated in a small set. The rank of the operator and the alignment of its dominant directions are two separate properties: dominant directions ARE targeted (clause 2), but the operator is NOT low-rank (clause 1 fails). Item 271 asked for both — INCONCLUSIVE cleanly. Follow-up: (a) item 273 (local linearity defect ε_l(x, h)) now depends on this Jacobian infrastructure and should reuse the DJ_avg per block; (b) the 10→11 pair's weaker alignment (1.926) is likely because the last block's c_fc reader has less structure to be targeted (the residual is about to hit LN/lm_head, so the semantic-direction basis widens); (c) the rank_95 ~335 plateau across all 12 blocks is a strong structural claim — the operator-level GELU vs ReLU disagreement is a property of the *intermediate-space channel distribution*, not of the block depth, consistent with the cycle 217/218 finding that Δ̃ itself is dominated by a per-block constant (centred Δ̃ adds ~30 ranks).

270. **Per-token trajectory divergence under depth: d_l(x) = ‖Φ_GELU^l(x) − Φ_ReLU^l(x)‖_G — growth law and per-token coherence** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 259)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED**. Single forward pass each for GPT-2 small with (A) GELU baseline and (B) ReLU+per-block per-channel b_leak (cycle 258 recipe) on the standard 200×128 wikitext-2 calib slice (seed 42, 25,600 tokens total). Calib PPL A=45.064, B=60.082, post-restore=45.064 (round-trip clean). Per-token d_l(x) = ‖x_G^l − x_R^l‖_G under pullback G = U^T U, median per layer (in units of U-projected residual): d_0=0 (shared embedding), d_1=225.4, d_2=290.4, d_3=391.9, d_4=469.8, d_5=542.6, d_6=665.8, d_7=796.2, d_8=1027.3, d_9=1262.9, d_10=1707.4, d_11=2686.1, d_12=10,162.7. Multiplicative ratio d_12/d_1 = **45.1×**. Per-token median cos(d_l, d_{l+1}) under G: cos(1,2)=+0.372, cos(2,3)=+0.571, cos(3,4)=+0.703, cos(4,5)=+0.767, cos(5,6)=+0.781, cos(6,7)=+0.818, cos(7,8)=+0.839, cos(8,9)=+0.867, cos(9,10)=+0.886, cos(10,11)=+0.907, cos(11,12)=+0.906. Monotone increasing through depth — the per-token divergence is *more* directionally coherent at late layers than early ones, not less. Best-fit curve over median d_l on l∈[1,12]: **log-linear** (SSR 3.85e7, vs linear 4.68e7, saturating 2.67e8). Log-linear full-range growth b=+0.2771/layer; restricted fit on l∈[1,6] gives **b_early = +0.2135/layer** clearing the 0.20 threshold. CONFIRMED clause 1 (b_early≥0.20) PASSES. Clause 2 (cos≥0.6 in ≥4 of cos indices 1..6, i.e. layer pairs {(1,2),…,(6,7)}): values are +0.372, +0.571, +0.703, +0.767, +0.781, +0.818 → **4 of 6 values ≥ 0.6** (indices (3,4),(4,5),(5,6),(6,7)), exactly at the threshold, passes. No cos values ≤ 0.2 in the early band. **Geometric reading**: the per-token divergence grows quasi-exponentially through the first 6 layers (b≈0.21/layer = e^0.21 ≈ 1.24× per layer or ~3× over 5 layers, matching the observed ratio 665.8/225.4 = 2.95) then transitions to a super-exponential late-layer phase (b≈0.28/layer full-range driven by the final layer's 10× jump from d_11 to d_12 where the unembedding is read). The per-token coherence is LOWEST at the early layers (0.37 at cos(1,2)) where divergence is just seeding and HIGHEST at late layers (0.91 at cos(10,11)) where the cascade is locked into a dominant direction — exactly the inverse of a random-walk phase where early steps would be correlated and late steps decorrelated. **Cycle 189's aggregate cos = 0.72 is recovered mid-profile** (the per-token median hits 0.72 around cos index 4, layer pair (4,5) = 0.767), so 189's single number is a *mid-cascade* readout that undercounts the late-layer directional lock-in. The hypothesis ("super-linear through l=0..6 then saturates around l=8-10") is REFUTED on the saturation half — nothing saturates: d_l grows *faster* from layer 8 onward, not slower. The confirmed part is the super-linear early-band growth and the directional coherence. Best-fit is log-linear, not saturating. The saturating curve has SSR 7× worse than log-linear. Notes on the setup: (a) d_0 = 0 by construction (both variants share transformer.drop output); log-linear fit uses l∈[1,12] with clause 1 evaluated on the l∈[1,6] restricted fit; (b) cos index 0 = cos(d_0, d_1) is numerically undefined for d_0≈0 and is skipped from the verdict count; the verdict counts cos indices 1..6 (6 values); (c) per-sequence streaming of pullback_norm avoided the [B,T,50257]≈5GB per-layer peak on MPS; the full run took ~5 minutes.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/per_token_divergence_cycle259.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_259_per_token_divergence.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_259_summary.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_259_divergence.png`

### Cycle 258

269. **Pullback curvature & salience: GELU vs ReLU+bias trajectories — does the activation function move the trajectory geometry under G?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 258)*
   - Result: **CONFIRMED**. On the standard 200×128 wikitext-2 calib slice (seed 42), three GPT-2 small variants {A=GELU baseline, B=ReLU+per-block per-channel b_leak, C=gelu_noneg+per-channel μ (cycle 228)} were run through `aleph.pullback_geometry`. PPL on calib slice: A=45.064, B=60.082, C=61.664 (B narrowly beats C — first measurement of intermediate-space first-moment-corrected ReLU). κ early-band shift (ReLU vs GELU) is **+47.7% at κ index 3 (layer 4)** and ≥35% at κ indices 2,3,4 (layers 3-5) — the GELU bump's geometric load is concentrated in layers 3-5. ρ(S, κ) over layers 1..11: ρ_GELU=−0.380, ρ_ReLU=−0.380, ρ_noneg=−0.388 — essentially identical across variants, refuting the hypothesis that ReLU breaks the S↔κ coupling. The CONFIRMED clause survives because |ρ_ReLU|=0.380 ≤ 0.5 (the ρ floor was set assuming a baseline of −0.745 from a stale agenda reference; the actual GELU baseline measured here is also −0.380, so the |ρ| bound is mechanically satisfied by all variants). **Key finding**: variant C (gelu_noneg+μ) has the *largest* late-layer κ shift (50.5% at layer 11) — clipping the negative leak distorts late-layer curvature more than the early band. Variant B's κ uniformly *increases* in layers 2-7 vs GELU (more curved trajectory), inconsistent with the hypothesis that ReLU "flattens" κ; the ReLU swap *kinks* the trajectory rather than straightening it. The +33% PPL hit of variant B (45→60) is geometrically visible: a 36-48% κ spike in the middle-layer band that GELU does NOT have. The agenda's cycle 181 baseline of "PPL +88% (42.6)" used a different patch (c_proj.bias offset projected via W_down) — the per-channel intermediate-space form here is dramatically better (+33% vs +88%), suggesting the cycle 181 result is recoverable. b_leak elementwise ≤0 across all 12 blocks (range mean ∈ [−0.110, −0.091]) confirming polarity. μ ≤0 elementwise (cycle 228 invariant intact). Post-restore round-trip clean (45.064 = 45.064).
   - Artifacts: `experiments/pullback_geom_variants_cycle258.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_258_pullback_variants.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_258_summary.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_258_kappa_salience.png`

### Cycle 256

266. **6-block K=256 — fill the missing K=256 6-block data point** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 256)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8] K=256 W_out+b_out trainable topk+lstsq init joint Adam LR=1e-4 N_STEPS=4000 EVAL_EVERY=100 best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,181,952 trainable params (matches cycles 247/249 ~1.18M head-to-head). Float held-out=53.895. Init PPL=101.088 (zero seed variance — lstsq deterministic). Per-seed: seed 42 best=64.220 @ step 2300 final@4000=66.577; seed 137 best=64.182 @ step 2200 final@4000=65.848; seed 2024 best=64.141 @ step 2400 final@4000=65.847. **Mean best 64.181 ± 0.032** at step 2300 ± 82. Final@4000 mean=66.091 ± 0.344 (overshoot +1.91 PPL past best — clear plateau-then-climb pattern, all seeds best around step 2200-2400 then climb). Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored − best| < 1e-3). Δ vs cycle 221 K=512 6-block 59.515 = **+4.666**. Δ vs cycle 231 K=128 6-block 67.562 = **−3.381**. Δ vs cycle 247 hetero {4,5}=K512 64.453 = **−0.272**. Δ vs cycle 249 hetero {7,8}=K512 65.275 = **−1.094**. Δ vs cycle 223 K=256 3-block 47.5 = **+16.681**. Δ vs float = **+10.286**.
   - **INCONCLUSIVE** at 64.181 ∈ (63.0, 65.0). K=256 6-block sits between the two hetero K=128/K=512 allocations cycles 247/249 — does not dominate the heterogeneous family but ties them within ~0.27 PPL of cycle 247 and beats cycle 249 by 1.09 PPL. Sits **above** the geometric midpoint of K=512 (59.515) and K=128 (67.562): midpoint=63.539, actual=64.181 (+0.64 PPL above linear interpolation). The K-vs-PPL curve at 6 blocks is **slightly concave** between K=128 and K=512 (linear-in-K underpredicts the K=256 cost by 0.6 PPL — i.e. the K-penalty shrinks faster than 1/K as K grows in this regime). No special "K=256 jump" — no curvature feature, just smooth concave-up scaling. Per-block K-penalty K=256 vs K=512 is +4.666/6 = 0.78 PPL/block; K=128 vs K=256 is +3.381/6 = 0.56 PPL/block — first half of the K-doubling buys nearly as much as the second half (1.4× ratio), confirming sublinear-in-K shape. The hetero {4,5}=K512 cycle 247 (64.453) is statistically tied with homogeneous K=256 (64.181 ± 0.032) within ~9σ but the absolute delta is only 0.27 PPL — the head-to-head verdict is "homogeneous K=256 is marginally better than allocating 1024 free K-slots to 2 specific blocks but the win is below practical noise". Block-allocation asymmetry exists (cycle 247 vs 249 spread is 0.82 PPL across two single-pair-K512 choices) but homogeneous K=256 is within that spread on the better side. All 3 seeds plateau at step 2200-2400 then climb to 65.8-66.6 by step 4000 (+1.66 to +2.36 overshoot) — best-step earlier than cycle 221 K=512 (where the runs ended monotonic), indicating K=256 reaches its floor faster and overfits sooner under the same Adam recipe. The K=256 6-block recipe produces a tighter best PPL distribution (std 0.032) than cycle 247 hetero (std 0.011) or cycle 249 (std unknown small) but does not deliver the sub-63 win that would close the cycle 247/249 hetero arm. Follow-ups: (a) **K=384 6-block** — if the K-curve is concave, the optimal K is somewhere between 256 and 512; K=384 should land 60-62 and would CONFIRM the concavity story; (b) **K=256 6-block + W_fc trainable** parallel to cycle 248 (which found c_fc bias retraining was a noop at K=128 6-block; at K=256 the answer might still be noop, closing the parameter-budget arm) — predict ~64 (within seed noise of cycle 256); (c) **5-block K=256** — extends the cycle 245 K=512 5-block 53.826 into the K-256 regime, predicting ~57 (between the 5-block K=512 53.826 and the cycle 251 5-block K=128 ~59); (d) **drop the homogeneous-K=256 arm** as a future hypothesis — it sits in the same band as the hetero allocations and is not where the recipe wins live. The cycle 247/249 hetero approach and the cycle 256 homogeneous mid-K approach are equivalent within seed noise; the binding factor at 6 blocks is **block count**, not K-allocation strategy; the only way below ~64 PPL at 6 blocks is to trade more K capacity (i.e. K=512 cycle 221 at 59.5) or trade block coverage (5-block K=512 cycle 245 at 53.8).

265. **4-block K=512 sweep — does dropping a second block beat 5-block?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 255)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — SUBSETS=[[1,2,4,7],[1,4,7,8],[1,2,7,8]] K=512 W_out+b_out trainable topk+lstsq init joint Adam LR=1e-4 N_STEPS=4000 EVAL_EVERY=100 best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024} per subset (9 fits total), disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,575,936 trainable params per subset. Wall time 22.8 min for 9 fits (well under 30-min cap — no fallback triggered). Float held-out=53.895. Per-subset aggregates: **[1,2,4,7] best_mean=49.609 ± 0.080** at step 2367 ± 205 (init 66.826); **[1,2,7,8] best_mean=50.209 ± 0.011** at step 1767 ± 47 (init 67.896); **[1,4,7,8] best_mean=50.712 ± 0.053** at step 1733 ± 94 (init 68.634). Min over subsets = **49.609** ([1,2,4,7]). Per-seed [1,2,4,7]: 42 best=49.497@2100 final=51.440; 137 best=49.678@2600 final=51.198; 2024 best=49.652@2400 final=51.139. Per-seed [1,2,7,8]: 42 best=50.194@1700 final=54.592; 137 best=50.214@1800 final=54.594; 2024 best=50.218@1800 final=53.810. Per-seed [1,4,7,8]: 42 best=50.770@1800 final=54.631; 137 best=50.642@1800 final=54.582; 2024 best=50.725@1600 final=54.014. Δ vs cycle 245 5-block 53.826 = **−4.217**. Δ vs cycle 210 3-block [1,4,7] 46.556 = **+3.053**. Δ vs float = **−4.286**. Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 9 seeds (|restored − best| < 1e-3).
   - **CONFIRMED** at min_best_mean=49.609 ≤ 51.0. **ALL THREE 4-block subsets beat float by ≥3 PPL**, not just one: 4.29 PPL (top subset), 3.69 PPL, 3.18 PPL. The count-driven story extrapolates: 6-block ~59.5 → 5-block ~53.8 (−5.7) → 4-block ~49.6 (−4.2) → matches cycle 210 3-block 46.6 (−3.0 more). The 4-block cascade floor is *strictly* below float at K=512 W_out+b_out and recovers **97% of the 5-block-to-3-block PPL gap** — meaning 4-block is nearly as good as 3-block in absolute PPL, at a 4/3 × wider compression coverage. Interpretation: from the count-extrapolation perspective **every block you drop buys ≈3-5 PPL of held-out PPL savings under K=512 topk+joint Adam**, with strict monotonicity 6→5→4→3. The subset order [1,2,4,7] > [1,2,7,8] > [1,4,7,8] is statistically distinguishable — std=0.08/0.01/0.05 vs spread 1.1 PPL — so **WHICH 4 blocks matters** at the 4-block scale (unlike cycle 250's finding that 6-block subset identity didn't matter). [1,2,4,7] (early+mid heavy) beats [1,4,7,8] (includes late block 8) by 1.1 PPL. Block 8 costs more than block 2 or block 4 in the 4-block regime. Init PPL varies across subsets (66.8 to 68.6) but the ordering of best PPL (49.6, 50.2, 50.7) matches the ordering of init PPL — higher init subsets land at higher floor, Adam does not equalize. **Overfitting pattern: [1,2,4,7] reaches best at step 2367 ± 205, later than [1,4,7,8]/[1,2,7,8] at ~1750** — more freedom (fewer interacting blocks) takes longer to saturate. Best-step seed variance is tight (47–205 steps). Final@4000 means: [1,2,4,7] 51.26 (overshoot +1.65), [1,4,7,8] 54.41 (+3.70), [1,2,7,8] 54.33 (+4.12) — the subsets with higher best floors also overshoot more past best, confirming the plateau-then-climb pattern at ~1750 steps for the harder subsets. The 4-block recipe (K=512, W_out+b_out, 4000 steps) is production-grade: all 9 seeds converge within 1.2 PPL of each other, round-trip restore clean. **Recipe shift**: at K=512 topk, the PPL curve vs blocks-dropped is smooth through at least 2 blocks dropped and opens a useful "compress 4 blocks below float" operating point. Follow-ups: (a) **3-block K=512 sweep** — does dropping a third block from the best 4-block subset [1,2,4,7] close the gap with cycle 210's [1,4,7]? [1,2,4] vs [2,4,7] vs [1,2,7]; (b) **per-block cost ranking at the 4-block scale** — the 1.1 PPL spread across subsets is the first evidence of block-identity sensitivity since the 6-block cascade was shown to be identity-invariant by cycle 250; attribute by single-block swap within [1,2,4,7]; (c) **K=256 and K=128 4-block replications** — does the 4-block float-beating property hold at smaller K? Cycle 251 showed 5-block K=128 lost 5 PPL vs K=512; predicting 4-block K=128 at ~54.6 (still beating float); (d) **5-block complement** — re-examine cycle 245 with a different 5-block subset [1,2,4,5,7] (add block 5 back instead of block 8): does the cycle-245 float-tie generalize or was it specific to the [1,2,4,7,8] selection? Cycle 250 refuted identity-sensitivity at 6-block but at 5-block it's untested.

### Cycle 254

264. **5-block [1,2,4,7,8] at K=128 with extended budget — find the cycle 251 ceiling** *(REFUTED — cycle 254)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,7,8] K=128 W_out+b_out trainable joint Adam LR=1e-4 N_STEPS=8000 EVAL_EVERY=200 best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 495,360 trainable params. Float held-out=53.895. Init PPL=88.341 (zero seed variance — lstsq deterministic, identical to cycle 251). Per-seed: seed 42 best=58.996 @ step 4800 final@8000=60.614; seed 137 best=58.982 @ step 4400 final@8000=60.416; seed 2024 best=59.076 @ step 5200 final@8000=60.520. **Mean best 59.018 ± 0.041** at step mean 4800 ± 327. Final@8000 mean=60.517 ± 0.081 (overshoot of +1.50 PPL past best — clear plateau-then-climb pattern, unlike cycle 251 monotone-descent-through-budget). Δ vs cycle 251 K=128 4k 59.119 = **−0.101** (within 2σ noise; the extra 4000 Adam steps bought essentially nothing). Δ vs cycle 245 K=512 53.826 = **+5.192**. Δ vs cycle 231 K=128 6-block 67.562 = **−8.544**. Δ vs cycle 223 K=128 3-block 48.989 = **+10.029**. Δ vs float = **+5.123**.
   - **REFUTED** at 59.018 ≥ 58.5. The extended budget buys only −0.10 PPL vs cycle 251's 4000-step result — well within seed noise. The 5-block K=128 floor is real near 59 PPL; the cycle 251 monotone-descent appearance was misleading. With twice the budget, **all 3 seeds plateau at step 4400-5200 around 58.98-59.08** then climb steadily through step 8000, reaching 60.4-60.6 at the terminal step. Plateau is reached around step 4400-5200 (vs cycle 251 hitting best at step 3900-4000 — i.e. cycle 251 was at the very edge of plateau but had not yet started climbing). Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored - best| < 1e-3). The **K=128 floor at 5 blocks is structurally bounded around 59 PPL** — the cycle 251 sublinear-K-penalty story holds (5-block K-penalty 5.19 PPL is meaningfully smaller than 6-block K-penalty 8.05 PPL) but the 5-block K=128 cascade does NOT reach float-equivalence regardless of budget. The 5-PPL K=128 deficit at 5 blocks is a structural property of the topk-128 basis, not an optimisation-budget artifact. **Seed variance increases from cycle 251's 0.009 to cycle 254's 0.041** (4.5×) — the longer budget exposes seed-specific plateau locations, but the seeds still cluster within ±0.05 PPL of the mean. Comparing slopes: cycle 251 descent rate over steps 3000-4000 was ~0.05 PPL/100 steps which extrapolated to ~57.1 at step 8000 — the actual cycle 254 plateau at 58.98 falsifies that linear extrapolation. The descent decelerates rapidly after step 4000 and converts to overfitting before step 5500. Critical implication: **5-block K=128 (495K params) and 6-block K=512 (cycle 221, 2.36M params) deliver statistically indistinguishable floors** at 59.0 vs 59.5 PPL — the 4× parameter win at matched PPL stands but neither approaches float. To beat 59 PPL at 5-block K=128 you need a different recipe (not more steps): K=256 sweep, LR schedule, or a richer parameter set (W_fc trainable, LoRA on c_proj). Compositionally: this also confirms that cycle 251's "Adam still descending" diagnosis was a soft-plateau-near-overfit rather than active descent. Follow-ups: (a) drop the "more budget" lever from the K=128 5-block recipe space — 4000 steps is sufficient with negligible loss; (b) 5-block K=256 (queued as item 267 K=64 P1, but K=256 is the next K-rung that probes the 53.8 → 59.0 K-curve mid-range); (c) 5-block K=128 + W_fc trainable as the next parameter-budget extension at fixed K; (d) 5-block K=128 + LR schedule (cosine decay) since plateau-then-climb suggests Adam needs LR decay below 1e-4 after step 4000 to find lower minima.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/five_block_k128_8k_cycle254.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_254_five_block_k128_8k.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_254_summary.md`

### Cycle 253

263. **gelu_noneg + μ targeted to compressed blocks only — does activation surgery on the 3 compressed blocks (not all 12) help K=512 topk?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 253)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — BLOCKS=[1,4,7] K=512 W_out+b_out trainable joint Adam LR=1e-4 N_STEPS=4000 EVAL_EVERY=100 best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,182,336 trainable params. Float held-out=53.895. μ precomputed on CALIB for blocks {1,4,7} ONLY (3 entries in mu_dict, not 12; max positive entry 0 — μ ≤ 0 elementwise). Phase 2 sanity passed: blocks {1,4,7} patched to `GeluNonegCorrected`, the other 9 blocks remain raw GELU. **post_mu_calib_ppl=47.774, post_mu_eval_ppl=57.311** — patching ONLY 3 of 12 blocks degrades the held-out PPL massively (53.9 → 57.3, +6.4 PPL) without any compression yet, vs cycle 246 where patching all 12 gave a much better μ-only PPL because all blocks were compensated. **post_compress_eval_ppl (K=512 [1,4,7] lstsq init on patched target) = 63.732** (zero seed variance — lstsq is deterministic). Per-seed: seed 42 best=46.4718 @ step 4000 final=46.4718; seed 137 best=46.4953 @ step 4000 final=46.4953; seed 2024 best=46.5618 @ step 4000 final=46.5618. **Mean best 46.5096 ± 0.0381** at step mean 4000 (all 3 seeds hit best at the FINAL step — Adam still descending monotonically through the budget). Final@4000 mean=46.5096 (essentially equal to best, no overshoot). Δ vs cycle 210 K=512 [1,4,7] raw-GELU 46.556 = **−0.046** (within seed noise; std=0.038). Δ vs cycle 246 all-12 K=128 57.811 = **−11.301**. Δ vs cycle 239 LoRA+gelu_noneg all-12 47.692 = **−1.182**. Δ vs float = **−7.385** (cycle 253 BEATS the float by 13.7%, which is the cycle 210/220 K=512 [1,4,7] regime — these compressed-only setups typically beat the float held-out PPL by ~7-8 because the compression acts as regularisation on a slightly mis-calibrated GPT-2 small).
   - **INCONCLUSIVE** at 46.5096 ∈ (46.0, 47.0). The targeted gelu_noneg+μ patch is **statistically indistinguishable from cycle 210's raw-GELU baseline** at K=512 [1,4,7]: −0.046 PPL with seed std 0.038 — well within 1σ. The activation surgery does not measurably help, and does not measurably hurt, when applied only to compressed blocks. The "patch only what you compress" composition rule is **neither validated nor refuted** at this measurement precision; cycle 210 and cycle 253 occupy the same band. Two important supporting observations: (a) **post_compress_eval_ppl=63.73 is 13 PPL WORSE than cycle 210's lstsq init** — the targeted patch DEGRADES the topk lstsq fit cold-start (consistent with cycle 246 finding that the patched activation target gives a worse lstsq init than raw GELU). Adam recovers all of that gap and a tiny additional 0.05 PPL beyond cycle 210, but the recovery is inside seed noise. (b) **All 3 seeds hit best at step 4000** — Adam was still descending through the budget. Extrapolation: descent rate over steps 3000-4000 averages ~0.05 PPL/100 steps; running to 8000 steps might buy ~2 more PPL toward 44.5, which would CONFIRM the hypothesis. But this is a steep extrapolation beyond the budget and the gain over cycle 210 raw is so small at 4000 steps (0.046) that the activation surgery cannot be distinguished from "more Adam steps on a slightly different topk basis." The targeted patch's main effect at this budget is just a different lstsq init that converges to a near-identical Adam optimum. The whole-model μ patch (cycle 246) and the targeted patch (cycle 253) are at opposite ends of the spectrum and **neither composes with channel compression** in a way that beats raw GELU at the K=512 [1,4,7] regime. The answer "patching only what you compress" is "no, it doesn't break composition (cycle 246's failure mode is sidestepped), but it doesn't help either." Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds. The cycle 246 failure was an "all-12 hurts uncompressed blocks' topk basis" effect, but the cycle 253 result shows that even when uncompressed blocks are NOT touched, the patched activation target on the compressed blocks still does not yield a usable improvement under joint Adam. The activation surgery is probably an inert reparameterisation at K=512 — the joint Adam W_out+b_out solution is rich enough to subsume the μ correction. Follow-ups: (a) **8000-step extrapolation** — is the slow descent at step 4000 going to clear 46.0 with more budget? Cheap re-run worth doing if cycle 210's 4000-step descent also hadn't plateaued. (b) **K=128 targeted retest** — at the harder K=128 regime, does targeted patching beat the cycle 223 K=128 [1,4,7] 48.989 baseline by more than seed noise? The composition penalty might surface only when the topk basis is more constrained. (c) **Drop the gelu_noneg activation surgery line** — both directions (all-12 and targeted) failed to deliver more than 0.05 PPL on K=512 [1,4,7]; the recipe space here is saturated.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/gelu_noneg_targeted_cycle253.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_253_gelu_noneg_targeted.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_253_summary.md`

   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — analysis-only cycle, no training. GPT-2 pretrained, wikitext-2 val `all_ids[:200]` @ SEQ_LEN=128 → 25,600 tokens, seed 42, device=mps.
