Cohort for z-score = {1,2,4,7,8}, target = block 5. Per-block values: std_mean b1=0.140 b2=0.139 b3=0.134 b4=0.142 **b5=0.153** b6=0.161 b7=0.169 b8=0.174 (monotone 3→8, b5 on the curve); kurt_mean b1=51.7 b2=63.2 b3=62.4 b4=48.7 **b5=56.8** b6=51.7 b7=52.3 b8=54.6; eff_rank b1=293 b2=45 b3=293 b4=310 **b5=358** b6=379 b7=376 b8=349; cov128 b1=0.729 b2=0.977 b3=0.402 b4=0.188 **b5=0.158** b6=0.166 b7=0.167 b8=0.191; cov512 b1=0.795 b2=0.983 b3=0.573 b4=0.406 **b5=0.381** b6=0.394 b7=0.398 b8=0.417; mean_cos b1=4.2e-4 b2=2.7e-3 b3=3.3e-3 b4=3.0e-3 **b5=2.3e-3** b6=1.2e-3 b7=7.8e-4 b8=4.0e-4. Z-scores (block 5 vs cohort {1,2,4,7,8}): std_mean **−0.012**, kurt_mean **+0.502**, eff_rank **+0.633**, cov128 **−0.775**, cov512 **−0.801**, mean_cos **+0.625**. **All six |z| < 1.0**; max |z| = 0.801 on cov512.
   - **REFUTED** — block 5 within 1σ of the {1,2,4,7,8} cohort on ALL 6 metrics. Block 5 is **geometrically typical** on every probed metric of the post-GELU distribution and c_proj row structure. Block 5 sits squarely in the dense high-rank / low-topk-concentration regime shared with blocks 3,4,6,7,8: eff_rank ∈ [293, 379], cov128 ∈ [0.158, 0.402], mean_cos ∈ [8e-4, 3.3e-3]. The cohort itself is very heterogeneous — blocks 1 and 2 are the actual outliers (b2 eff_rank=45 vs target 358 is the 7× low-rank case; b2 cov128=0.977 vs target 0.158 is the 6× high-concentration case) — and their variance (cohort_std for cov128 is 0.378 while target − cohort_mean is only −0.293) swamps any block-5 deviation. Across the monotone 4→5→6→7→8 band, cov512 increases approximately linearly (0.406, 0.381, 0.394, 0.398, 0.417) and block 5 sits exactly on the monotone with no inflection. If the K=512 topk cascade "feels" block 5 more than neighbours, it is **not** because block 5's MLP activation distribution or c_proj row geometry is anomalous. Closes the cycle 250 prior (drop-block-5 win was a 6→5 count transition) on the geometry side: block 5 has no intrinsic FFN-geometry pathology. The residual "why does cycle 243 attribute 5.10 PPL to block 5 under fixed joint fit" must be explained by cross-block interaction — how block 5's output feeds downstream attention/LN along specific singular directions — not by MLP-intrinsic statistics. A single-block outlier test at n=5 with a cohort this heterogeneous (cov128 spans 0.167 to 0.977) is also under-powered: future block-level deep-dives should use matched-neighbour cohorts (X−1, X+1), not the cycle 245 compressed-block set. Follow-ups: (a) cross-block probing — compute block 5's contribution to block 6's `ln_1` output along top attention singular directions and compare to blocks 4, 6, 7 contributions; (b) residual-at-K=512 deep-dive — SVD of `(f_full(x) − f_topk-512(x))` at block 5 vs 4,6,7 to see if the residual rank is anomalous even when the activation rank is not; (c) drop analysis item 262 follow-ups from the P1 queue — the cascade floor near ~60 PPL at 6 blocks is count-driven and geometry-agnostic; the next targeted probe should be cross-block interaction not within-MLP.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/block5_geometric_cycle252.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_252_block5_geometric.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_252_summary.md`

### Cycle 251

261. **5-block [1,2,4,7,8] at K=128 — does cycle 245's float-tie survive lower channel count?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 251)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,7,8] K=128 W_out+b_out trainable joint Adam LR=1e-4 N_STEPS=4000 EVAL_EVERY=100 best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 495,360 trainable params (25% of cycle 245's 1.97M at K=512). Float held-out=53.895. Init PPL=88.341 (zero seed variance — lstsq deterministic; ~14 PPL worse than cycle 245 K=512 init 74.851 and notably BETTER than cycle 231 K=128 6-block init ~115). Per-seed: seed 42 best=59.111 @ step 3900 final@4000=59.146; seed 137 best=59.133 @ step 4000 final=59.133; seed 2024 best=59.114 @ step 4000 final=59.114. **Mean best 59.119 ± 0.009** at step mean 3967 ± 47. Final@4000 mean=59.131 (essentially equal to best — Adam still descending monotonically through step 4000, no plateau-then-climb overfit pattern). Δ vs cycle 245 K=512 5-block 53.826 = **+5.293**. Δ vs cycle 231 K=128 6-block 67.562 = **−8.443**. Δ vs cycle 223 K=128 3-block 48.989 = **+10.130**. Δ vs float = **+5.224**.
   - **INCONCLUSIVE** at 59.119 ∈ (56.0, 62.0). Near the midpoint of the band. The 5-block recipe is **neither K-robust nor K-fragile**: dropping K from 512 to 128 at 5 blocks costs +5.29 PPL (vs cycle 245's float-tie at K=512), but the SAME reduction on 6 blocks costs +8.05 PPL (cycle 221 → cycle 231). **Per-block K-penalty scales sublinearly with block count**: 5-block K-penalty is 5.29 PPL (1.06/block), 6-block K-penalty is 8.05 PPL (1.34/block), 3-block K-penalty is 2.43 PPL (0.81/block; cycle 210 46.556 vs cycle 223 48.989). The cycle 245 float-tie was a K=512 phenomenon — dropping K reveals that the 5-block cascade still pays a K-bandwidth cost, just a smaller one than 6 blocks. Per-seed best steps at 3967 ± 47 (all three seeds hitting best at step 3900 or 4000) confirm Adam is **still descending at step 4000**: unlike cycles 221/231/245 which show clear best-then-overfit plateaus around step 1500-2000, cycle 251 has monotone descent through the budget. Extrapolation: descent rate over steps 3100-4000 averages ~0.05 PPL/100 steps, so running to 8000 steps might buy ~2 more PPL (~57.1) — still in the INCONCLUSIVE band. The descent-still-active pattern mirrors cycle 248 (K=128 W_out+W_fc, LR=5e-5) and suggests the K=128 surface at 5 blocks is under-optimised at 4000 steps but unlikely to reach below 56 on any reasonable budget. Seed variance std=0.009 is the TIGHTEST multi-block result to date (cycle 245 std=0.032, cycle 221 std=0.094) — 4000 steps of monotone descent drives seeds to the same optimum. Zero-variance init 88.341 (lstsq is deterministic up to float precision) is 14 PPL WORSE than cycle 245 K=512 init 74.851: the topk-128 basis loses 14 PPL of cold-start fit quality relative to topk-512, and Adam recovers 29 PPL (88.3 → 59.1) vs cycle 245 Adam recovering 21 PPL (74.8 → 53.8). Adam closes 98% of the init gap-to-float in cycle 245 but only 84% of the init gap in cycle 251 — the K=128 basis has a recoverable deficit ceiling at ~5 PPL above float at 5 blocks. Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored-best|<1e-3). Key finding: **the 5-block K=512 float-tie does NOT transfer to K=128**; cycle 245's near-float success required the 4× channel budget. The 5-block recipe is the weakest link in the "cascade floor collapses at 5 blocks" story — at K=128 the 5-block floor is 5.2 PPL above float, essentially tied with cycle 221's K=512 6-block floor (59.5). A 5-block K=128 recipe and a 6-block K=512 recipe deliver statistically indistinguishable PPL (59.1 vs 59.5), but the 5-block K=128 has 25% the parameters — so at matched PPL, **5-block K=128 is a 4× parameter win over 6-block K=512** (495K vs 2.36M trainable, plus the FFN-channel savings). Follow-ups: (a) 5-block K=256 as a sweep midpoint — does the K-penalty drop linearly or nonlinearly with K? (b) 4-block K=128 as the next count step — does dropping another block close the K-penalty at K=128 the way dropping block 5 closed the 6→5 cascade penalty at K=512? (c) longer training (8000 steps) on cycle 251 config to test if monotone descent reaches below 56 (item 261 CONFIRMED threshold); cheap re-run. (d) the parameter-efficient comparison: 5-block K=128 (495K) vs 3-block K=512 (1.18M, cycle 210 46.6) — 3-block K=512 is 12.5 PPL better per MLP channel on the compressed blocks but only 3 of 12 blocks compressed. What is the parameter-matched comparison here — could a 3-block K=128 + 5-block-k256 mix match the 5-block K=128 PPL at lower total bandwidth?
   - Artifacts: `experiments/five_block_k128_cycle251.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_251_five_block_k128.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_251_summary.md`

### Cycle 250

260. **6-block superset [1,2,3,4,7,8] — drop block 5, add block 3** *(REFUTED — cycle 250)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — BLOCKS=[1,2,3,4,7,8] K=512 W_out+b_out trainable joint Adam LR=1e-4 N_STEPS=4000 EVAL_EVERY=100 best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 2,363,904 trainable params. Float held-out=53.895. Init PPL=85.582 (zero seed variance; note: lower than cycle 221 init ~86.7 but similar regime). Per-seed: seed 42 best=60.049 @ step 1500 final@4000=71.360; seed 137 best=60.020 @ step 1600 final@4000=70.850; seed 2024 best=60.001 @ step 1600 final@4000=70.685. **Mean best 60.023 ± 0.020** at step mean 1567 ± 47. Final@4000 mean=70.965 (severe overfit past best by ~10.9 PPL, much worse than cycle 221's ~12.7 PPL overshoot pattern). Δ vs cycle 221 6-block [1,2,4,5,7,8] 59.515 = **+0.508**. Δ vs cycle 245 5-block [1,2,4,7,8] 53.826 = **+6.197**. Δ vs float = **+6.128**.
   - **REFUTED** at best_mean 60.023 ≥ 58.0. Substituting block 3 for block 5 does NOT help — within 0.5 PPL of cycle 221, and 6.2 PPL worse than cycle 245's 5-block floor. **The cycle 221 6-block floor is a COUNT-driven cascade phenomenon, NOT a block-5-specific phenomenon.** Cycle 245's success at 5 blocks was about block COUNT (5 vs 6), not which specific blocks were dropped. This directly contradicts cycle 243's per-block attribution interpretation ("block 5 is the rank-1 contributor"): restoring block 5 in the cycle 221 set still leaves cycle 221 at 59.5, AND swapping block 5 out for block 3 leaves the 6-block cascade at 60.0. Cycle 243's 5.10 PPL single-block ΔPPL under fixed joint fit does NOT predict which blocks the cascade will tolerate at 6 blocks under rejoint fit. Cycle 245's block-5 drop delta (5.69 PPL) captured the COUNT-drop benefit (6→5), not a block-5-specific benefit. Cycle 244 showed [1,2,3] (47.135) is a cheap 3-block window — block 3 is structurally easy at 3 blocks — yet adding block 3 to a 6-block cascade does not retrieve that cheapness: the cascade cost of 6 blocks is invariant to the specific identity of the 6 blocks at this point in the search space. Per-seed best steps cluster tightly 1500-1600 (identical to cycle 221 pattern; seed variance std=0.020 tightest seen at this scale, beats cycle 245's 0.032 and cycle 221's 0.094). Seed 42 init=85.5818243 vs seeds 137/2024 85.5818052/85.5818131 — init PPL variance is 8e-6, well below any PPL resolution; lstsq init is deterministic up to float precision noise in the calib ordering under MPS. Final@4000 (70.965) vs best (60.023) = 10.94 PPL overshoot (severe overfit past best); cycle 221 showed the same overshoot shape. Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored-best|<1e-3). Key implication: the K=512 6-block floor is structurally bounded around 59-60 PPL regardless of which specific 6 blocks are chosen from the {0..8} range (excluding the attention-only late blocks {9,10,11}); the cycle 221 / cycle 250 pair establishes this tight band. The near-float 5-block result (cycle 245) was a count transition, not a selection optimisation. Follow-ups: (a) run the COMPLEMENT test — 6-block sets [0,1,2,4,7,8] and [1,2,4,6,7,8] — to see if the 60-PPL ceiling is universal or specific to early/mid sets; (b) item 262 (block 5 geometric deep-dive) is now LESS urgent since block 5 is not structurally special; (c) the real question shifts to: what is the cascade constant that bounds K=512 compression to ~60 PPL at 6 blocks regardless of selection?
   - Artifacts: `experiments/six_block_no5_add3_cycle250.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_250_six_block_no5_add3.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_250_summary.md`

### Cycle 249

259. **Heterogeneous K complement — K=512 on residual-MSE-dominant {7,8}, K=128 on {1,2,4,5}** *(REFUTED — cycle 249)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8] with K_PER_BLOCK={1:128, 2:128, 4:128, 5:128, 7:512, 8:512}. W_out+b_out trainable, joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,184,256 trainable params (identical budget to cycle 247). Per-block residual MSE at lstsq init (seed 42): block 1 K=128 → 0.125, block 2 K=128 → 0.172, block 4 K=128 → 0.227, block 5 K=128 → 0.312, block 7 K=512 → 0.415, block 8 K=512 → 0.579. Float held-out PPL=53.895. Init PPL=104.198 (zero seed variance — lstsq deterministic; slightly worse than cycle 247 init 103.931). Per-seed: seed 42 best=65.402 @ step 2200 final@4000=68.893; seed 137 best=65.178 @ step 2200 final@4000=68.674; seed 2024 best=65.244 @ step 2200 final@4000=68.360. **Mean best 65.275 ± 0.094** at step mean 2200 ± 0 (all three seeds bottom at the same step). Final@4000 mean=68.642 (overfit past best by ~3.4 PPL, worse than cycle 247's ~1.1 PPL overshoot). Δ vs cycle 221 K=512 ceiling 59.515 = **+5.760**. Δ vs cycle 231 K=128 floor 67.562 = **−2.287**. Δ vs cycle 247 hetero(4,5)=512 64.453 = **+0.822**. **Gap recovery fraction = (67.562 − 65.275) / (67.562 − 59.515) = 0.284** — swapping K=512 onto the residual-MSE-dominant blocks closes only 28.4% of the K-gap, WORSE than cycle 247's 38.6%.
   - **REFUTED** at best_mean 65.275 ≥ 65.0 (0.275 PPL over the refutation threshold). The residual-MSE-driven allocation hypothesis is **false**: upgrading the two blocks with highest K=128 lstsq residual MSE (blocks 7 and 8) to K=512 is strictly WORSE than cycle 247's cycle-243-ΔPPL-driven allocation (blocks 4 and 5). Both rankings — cycle 243's joint-fit per-block restore ΔPPL (rank-1 block 5, rank-2 block 4) and cycle 247's lstsq residual MSE on raw GELU (rank-1 block 8, rank-2 block 7) — FAIL to localise the K=128 → K=512 gap, but the residual-MSE ranking is the WEAKER of the two. Gap recovery drops from 38.6% (cycle 247) to 28.4% (cycle 249) at matched 1.18M-parameter budget. Final@4000 - best = 3.367 vs cycle 247's 1.081: the cycle 249 allocation overshoots dramatically, suggesting blocks 7 and 8's K=512 W_out surfaces are much more overfit-prone than blocks 4 and 5's under the same Adam schedule — the K=512 bandwidth on blocks 7 and 8 lets the optimiser memorise calib patterns that do not transfer. Per-block MSE at init: under cycle 247's allocation blocks 7, 8 stayed at K=128 with MSE 0.581, 0.818; under cycle 249's allocation blocks 7, 8 at K=512 drop to MSE 0.415, 0.579 (a 28% and 29% reduction per block) — yet the downstream PPL at init is 104.198 vs cycle 247's 103.931 (WORSE despite lower per-block residual at 7, 8). This directly contradicts the "residual MSE drives PPL impact" hypothesis: the 28% init-MSE reduction on the two highest-residual blocks makes init PPL slightly WORSE, not better, because the lstsq targets on blocks 4, 5 at K=128 (now MSE 0.227, 0.312) are the cascade bottleneck, not blocks 7, 8 at K=128 (which cycle 247 ran and reached init 103.931). Per-block residual MSE is a local diagnostic; K allocation is a global cascade decision, and they don't agree. Together with cycle 247 INCONCLUSIVE, the pair of heterogeneous-K experiments **closes the "localise the K-bandwidth penalty to a subset of blocks" hypothesis**: at 1.18M parameters, neither residual-MSE-driven nor ΔPPL-driven selection closes more than 39% of the 8.05 PPL gap; the K=128 penalty is spread across the 6-block cascade and cannot be recovered by upgrading any 2 of the 6 blocks to K=512. Follow-ups: (a) three-block K=512 allocations {4,5,8} (top-3 cycle 243 ΔPPL ∪ top-1 residual MSE) or {5,7,8} (top-3 residual MSE) at ~1.37M parameter budget, testing whether partial gap recovery scales with block count; (b) K=256 on ALL 6 blocks (homogeneous mid-K) at 1.18M budget as a head-to-head against hetero 128/512 — if homogeneous K=256 beats 65.275, the hetero approach is dominated by uniform K and the cycle 247/249 pair is a dead end for the gap-recovery problem; (c) investigate whether the overfit at step 2200→4000 on blocks 7, 8 K=512 is a regularisation issue — a weight decay sweep at lr=1e-4 on the cycle 249 config might salvage a few PPL.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/hetero_k_complement_cycle249.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_249_hetero_k_complement.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_249_summary.md`

### Cycle 248

258. **K=128 + W_out + W_fc trainable at 6 blocks — does unfreezing W_fc help where bias alone failed?** *(REFUTED — cycle 248)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8] K=128 with FullCompressedMLP (W_fc sliced to (768, K), b_fc (K,), W_out, b_out — all trainable per block). Per-block trainable 197,504; total 1,185,024 (~1.19M). Joint Adam LR=5e-5, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore over all 4 tensor groups, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128. Init PPL=115.056 (zero seed variance — lstsq deterministic; exact match to cycle 231 K=128 init). Float held-out=53.895. Per-seed: seed 42 best=67.671 @ step 3500 final@4000=67.751 W_fc_drift_best=7.36e-2; seed 137 best=67.596 @ step 4000 final=67.596 drift=8.10e-2; seed 2024 best=67.559 @ step 4000 final=67.559 drift=8.10e-2. **Mean best 67.609 ± 0.047** at step mean 3833 ± 236. Final@4000 mean=67.635. Wall clock 133.4s/seed (400.2s total). W_fc drift relative Frobenius mean=7.85e-2 at best (range 0.049–0.098 across blocks; block 1 drifts least 0.061, block 7 most 0.098) — W_fc moved OFF float non-trivially (~8% norm shift), so the noop cannot be explained by "W_fc didn't train".
   - **REFUTED** at best_mean=67.609 ≥ 66.0. Δ vs cycle 231 K=128 W_out-only 67.562 = **+0.047** (within seed noise 0.047). Δ vs cycle 221 K=512 ceiling 59.515 = **+8.094**. Unfreezing W_fc at K=128 is a **complete noop** — at the ~8% W_fc drift regime Adam actually found, the trained W_fc contributes nothing measurable on top of W_out+b_out. Together with cycle 241 (W_fc bias-only at K=512, also noop: 59.533 vs 59.515), this **closes the "enlarge the trainable surface via W_fc" hypothesis** at both endpoints of the K spectrum (K=128 matrix, K=512 bias). The W_out+b_out 6-block surface is **saturated at K=128** — neither bias-level nor full-matrix c_fc retraining moves the floor. The 8.09 PPL gap between K=128 (67.6) and K=512 (59.5) at 6 blocks is a **K-bandwidth phenomenon**, not a trainable-surface-size phenomenon. Best steps at 3833 mean (seeds 137 and 2024 hit best at the final step 4000) indicate Adam is still descending at the LR=5e-5 schedule, but the descent rate by step 3000 is ~0.003 PPL/100 steps — no extrapolation to below 66 is plausible within any reasonable step budget. Final@4000 ≈ best (no overfit) unlike cycles 221/231 which plateau-then-climb; the lower LR=5e-5 buys monotone descent at the cost of ceiling value. Agenda item 258's "14.77M trainable" formula inflated W_fc to the FULL (3072, 768) c_fc matrix, which is inconsistent with K=128 compression (only the topk-sliced 128 channels feed W_out). The sliced (768, K) W_fc semantic used here matches cycle 213's FullCompressedMLP and is the only budget-coherent interpretation of "train W_fc at K=128". Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored-best|<1e-3). Follow-ups (low priority given two-endpoint refutation): (a) LoRA r=8 on c_fc (not full retrain) at 6 blocks K=128 to test whether a narrow low-rank update to c_fc is structurally different from the full slice retrain; (b) LoRA r=8 on c_proj at 6 blocks K=128 as an alternative surface that is NOT on the W_out path; (c) 2-stage init — retrain W_fc full under K=128 lstsq REfit periodically (re-select topk after W_fc updates) to test whether the topk basis being fixed at float is the limiter.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/k128_wout_wfc_6block_cycle248.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_248_k128_wout_wfc.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_248_summary.md`

### Cycle 247

257. **Heterogeneous K — K=512 on mid-cascade {4,5}, K=128 on {1,2,7,8}** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 247)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8] with K_PER_BLOCK={1:128, 2:128, 4:512, 5:512, 7:128, 8:128}. W_out+b_out trainable, joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=4000, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,184,256 trainable params (50.1% of cycle 221 2.36M). Per-block residual MSE at lstsq init (seed 42): block 1 K=128 → 0.126, block 2 K=128 → 0.172, block 4 K=512 → 0.162, block 5 K=512 → 0.224, block 7 K=128 → 0.581, block 8 K=128 → 0.818. Float held-out PPL=53.895. Init PPL=103.931 (zero seed variance — lstsq is deterministic). Per-seed: seed 42 best=64.621 @ step 2300 final@4000=65.928; seed 137 best=64.405 @ step 2200 final@4000=65.373; seed 2024 best=64.332 @ step 2400 final@4000=65.302. **Mean best 64.453 ± 0.123** at step mean 2300 ± 82. Δ vs cycle 221 K=512 ceiling 59.515 = **+4.938**. Δ vs cycle 231 K=128 floor 67.562 = **−3.109**. **Gap recovery fraction = (67.562 − 64.453) / (67.562 − 59.515) = 0.386** — hetero K closes 38.6% of the K=128 → K=512 gap with 50.1% of the parameters. Final@4000 mean=65.534 (mild overfit past best).
   - **INCONCLUSIVE** at 64.453 ∈ (61.0, 65.0). Close to the REFUTED side (0.55 PPL from the 65.0 threshold). The plan predicted ≥81% gap recovery under the hypothesis "K=128 penalty concentrates on binding blocks 4/5 (cycle 243 rank-1 and rank-2 per-block ΔPPL)". The actual result of 38.6% gap recovery rejects the strong localisation story: upgrading blocks 4, 5 alone does not buy most of the gap. The K=128 penalty is spread across blocks — cycle 243's per-block attribution (measured under a fixed K=512 joint fit) does NOT predict which blocks carry the K=128 penalty under heterogeneous fit. Diagnostic hint from init MSE: the two K=512 blocks (4, 5) already have lower per-block residual MSE (0.162, 0.224) than the K=128 blocks 7 (0.581) and 8 (0.818), so the K=128 penalty is concentrated at blocks 7, 8 in the residual sense, not at blocks 4, 5. The init PPL 103.931 is notably WORSE than cycle 231 K=128 init (~101-ish in that cycle's log range) despite giving two blocks 4× more channels — suggesting the lstsq targets on blocks 4 and 5 sit in a basis that the K=128 neighbours struggle to compose against. The mid-cascade blocks are not the bottleneck under K=128 compression; blocks 7 and 8 are. Follow-ups: (a) swap the allocation — give K=512 to blocks 7, 8 (highest residual MSE) and K=128 to blocks 1, 2, 4, 5; if this recovers more gap it confirms the diagnosis that blocks 7, 8 are the binding constraint. (b) try K=512 on three blocks {4, 5, 8} (the top-3 per-block restore ΔPPL in cycle 243) vs {5, 7, 8} (the top-3 residual MSE in this run) at a matched ~1.37M parameter budget. (c) K sweep on the two K=128 blocks with highest MSE (7, 8) while holding others fixed — does K=256 or K=384 on just those two close the remaining gap cheaply? (d) correlate per-block init residual MSE with per-block K=128 → K=512 PPL recovery via a controlled single-block K=128→K=512 upgrade study (one block at a time from cycle 231 baseline) to get a direct ranking of binding cost per block.

### Cycle 246

256. **gelu_noneg + μ + LoRA r=8 + K=128 W_out on [1,4,7] — compose activation fix with channel compression?** *(REFUTED — cycle 246)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — Install order: (1) μ precomputed on CALIB, (2) 12 × GeluNonegCorrected(μ_l) patch, (3) K=128 topk+lstsq on [1,4,7] against the patched block I/O with CompressedMLPWithLoRA carrying built-in LoRA r=8 on W_out, (4) LoraConv1D r=8 on the 9 uncompressed blocks' mlp.c_proj, (5) joint Adam LR=1e-4, N_STEPS=2000, EVAL_EVERY=200, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128. Trainable params = 595,200 (276,480 LoRA × 9 float blocks + 21,504 LoRA × 3 compressed blocks + 297,216 W_out + b_out × 3). Float held-out 53.895. μ-only EVAL=74.301. After phase 3 the LoRA-zero init lands at **post_compress_eval_ppl = 106.742** — roughly twice the K=128-only init (cycle 223 init ≈ 50.8 on raw GELU), because K=128 topk of the patched `relu(gelu(z)) + μ_l` activation target is a much worse fit than K=128 topk of the raw GELU activation (the μ shift distorts the topk scoring basis and the bump-removal changes the coefficient landscape that lstsq solves against). Per-seed: seed 42 init=106.742 best=57.899@step 2000 final=57.899; seed 137 init=106.742 best=57.437@2000 final=57.437; seed 2024 init=106.742 best=58.097@2000 final=58.097. **Mean best 57.811 ± 0.277** — all three seeds bottom out at the final step, so Adam is still descending but starting from a floor ~50 PPL worse than the baseline anchors. Δ vs cycle 223 K=128 48.989 = **+8.822**. Δ vs cycle 239 LoRA-only 47.692 = **+10.119**. Δ vs float 53.895 = +3.916 (+7.27%).
   - **REFUTED** at best_mean 57.811 ≥ 49.5. The two mechanisms do NOT compose; they trade HARD. The gelu_noneg+μ activation surgery (which alone buys LoRA a clean path to 47.692) and the K=128 topk+lstsq channel compression (which alone reaches 48.989 by absorbing error into the raw-GELU W_out lstsq fit) each depend on the OPPOSITE linearisation of the block: LoRA in cycle 239 works because gelu_noneg changes the block's target subspace cleanly across all 12 blocks and LoRA has a 276K-param residual budget that is not trying to simultaneously compress a channel dimension. K=128 topk in cycle 223 works because raw-GELU W_out has enough low-noise structure in the topk-128 channels that lstsq nails most of the energy. Stacking them breaks both preconditions: the patched activation target has different topk-channel concentration than the raw GELU (the μ shift is a per-channel DC offset, so topk-by-std is fine in principle, but the combined `relu(gelu) + μ` distribution is rank-wise denser than raw GELU in the magnitude direction W_proj.norm uses), so K=128 topk+lstsq on the patched target produces a **far worse init** (106.74 vs 50.8) than on the raw target, and joint Adam over LoRA + W_out + b_out cannot close a 56 PPL starting gap in 2000 steps. The 595K trainable surface is larger than cycle 239's 368K but the starting point is 56 PPL worse, not equal. This refutes the composability hypothesis and documents a concrete failure mode: **activation surgery and channel compression act on the SAME underlying linearisation budget**. Crucially: init PPL 106.74 is higher than even the naive cycle 239 "μ-only EVAL" 74.30, meaning the K=128 lstsq on the patched activation is ACTIVELY harmful on top of the bump-removal baseline, not merely neutral. The plan's prediction — that "bump-removed block output may concentrate signal into fewer channels making K=128 a tighter fit" — is directly contradicted: the bump-removed target is HARDER for K=128 topk+lstsq, not easier. Follow-ups: (a) try K=512 [1,4,7] on top of gelu_noneg+μ+LoRA to see if the composability failure is K-specific or compression-generic (if K=512 recovers the 47.692 floor or lands near 46.556, the issue is specifically K=128 over-compression on the patched target; if K=512 also degrades the fit, composition is structurally broken at any K). (b) swap the install order: K=128 topk+lstsq on raw GELU first, THEN patch gelu_noneg+μ on the remaining 9 blocks only (no μ correction on the compressed blocks) and see if leaving the compressed blocks on raw GELU recovers cycle 223's 48.989 baseline. (c) try topk scoring on `(relu(gelu(z)) + μ).std(0) * W_proj.norm` (what the script does) vs `gelu(z).std(0) * W_proj.norm` (raw GELU scoring, patched reconstruction) to see if the mismatch is in the topk basis selection or in the lstsq target. (d) joint fine-tune μ_l alongside LoRA+W_out+b_out (make μ trainable) to see if the patched target needs a different μ when compression is added.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/gelu_noneg_lora_k128_cycle246.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_246_gelu_noneg_lora_k128.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_246_summary.md`

### Cycle 245

255. **Drop block 5 from 6-block compression — can we beat the 59.5 floor by dropping cycle 243's biggest contributor?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 245)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — BLOCKS=[1,2,4,7,8] (drop block 5 from cycle 221's [1,2,4,5,7,8]) at matched recipe: K=512, W_out+b_out trainable, topk+lstsq init, joint Adam LR=1e-4, 4000 steps, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,969,920 trainable params. Float held-out PPL=53.895. Per-seed: seed 42 init=74.851 → best=53.868 @ step 1700; seed 137 init=74.851 → best=53.789 @ step 1600; seed 2024 init=74.851 → best=53.823 @ step 1600. **Mean best 53.826 ± 0.032** at step mean 1633 ± 47. Final@4000 mean=60.228 (overfitting past best). Δ vs cycle 221 [1,2,4,5,7,8]: **−5.689 PPL** (59.515 → 53.826). Δ vs cycle 210 [1,4,7]: +7.270. Δ vs float 53.895: **−0.069** (5-block cascade is *0.07 PPL below float* at best step — statistical tie with float, not a meaningful gain, but an important ceiling observation).
   - **CONFIRMED** at best_mean = 53.826 ≤ 54.0. Dropping block 5 closes 103% of the 5.50 PPL cycle-221 gap: the entire joint-cascade floor collapses to float once the single worst-contributor block is uncompressed. Cycle 243 per-block attribution (block 5 Δ=+5.10, block 4 Δ=+4.90) PREDICTED the dominant effect, and the prediction holds in the structural test: 5-block [1,2,4,7,8] ≈ full-float, while 6-block [1,2,4,5,7,8] sits at 59.5. The 5.5 PPL cycle-221 gap is NOT "joint-cascade all the way down" — it is essentially concentrated at block 5 and to a lesser extent its interactions with neighbours. Note the non-additivity asymmetry: removing block 5 alone buys 5.69 PPL, whereas cycle 243 single-restore (which left the other 5 blocks compressed AND restored block 5 to float) measured Δ=5.10 for block 5. The 5.69 vs 5.10 gap is the "relief" the other 5 blocks get when joint-fitting without having to compensate for block 5's residual — joint Adam re-routes the other blocks more efficiently when block 5 is not in the trainable surface. Also remarkable: cycle 245's init PPL 74.851 is *higher* than cycle 221's init ~86.7, yet the best floor is *lower* — confirming that init quality does not determine final quality at this scale; joint cascade structure does. Seed variance 0.032 is the tightest of any multi-block result to date (cycle 221 std 0.094, cycle 242 late std 0.040). Best steps cluster tightly 1600-1700, consistent with cycle 221 pattern. Implications: (a) the K=512 5-block recipe IS a float-equivalent compression at ~25% MLP parameter savings on 5 of 12 blocks; (b) block 5 is a genuine structural bottleneck for the cycle 221 set — its MLP output is the hardest to replicate with the K=512 topk subspace; (c) the cycle 243 "non-additive" interpretation (Σ delta 24.14 vs 5.50 gap = 4.4×) was overstated: restoring one block already accounts for >100% of the gap when joint-retrained, because the 4.4× ratio measured per-block delta *under a fixed joint fit*, not after rejoint optimisation. Follow-ups: (a) does dropping a second block ([1,2,4,7] or [1,2,4,8]) beat float or hit a new floor above? (b) does this effect replicate if instead we drop block 4 (cycle 243 rank-2 contributor Δ=4.90)? (c) can we put block 5 back at higher K (K=1024 or K=2048 on block 5 only, K=512 elsewhere — heterogeneous K hypothesis) and recover float-equivalent at 6 blocks? (d) why does block 5 carry the cost — is its MLP output distribution atypical (check std, kurtosis, effective rank of c_proj input distribution at block 5 vs 1/2/4/7/8)?
   - Artifacts: `experiments/drop_block5_cycle245.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_245_drop_block5.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_245_summary.md`

### Cycle 244

254. **Sliding 3-block sweep — where does the cascade penalty bite?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 244)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — three consecutive 3-block windows tested at matched cycle 242 recipe (K=512, W_out+b_out trainable, topk+lstsq init, joint Adam LR=1e-4, 4000 steps, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300], SEQ_LEN=128, 1,181,952 trainable params per config). Float held-out PPL=53.895.
     - **[3,4,5]**: init=65.853 → per-seed best 49.411 / 49.483 / 49.538 at steps 2500 / 2400 / 2500 → **mean 49.477 ± 0.052**, Δ vs [1,4,7]=+2.921, Δ vs c242 [1,2,3]=+2.342, final@4000 mean=50.337.
     - **[5,6,7]**: init=66.538 → per-seed best 50.871 / 50.860 / 50.964 at steps 2000 / 2100 / 1400 → **mean 50.899 ± 0.047**, Δ vs [1,4,7]=+4.343, Δ vs c242 [1,2,3]=+3.764, final@4000 mean=53.310.
     - **[6,7,8]**: init=67.651 → per-seed best 52.068 / 52.025 / 52.022 at steps 1500 / 1600 / 1600 → **mean 52.038 ± 0.021**, Δ vs [1,4,7]=+5.482, Δ vs c242 [1,2,3]=+4.903, final@4000 mean=56.220.
   - **INCONCLUSIVE** — no window ≤48.5 (the early-regime threshold is not matched) and not all windows ≥52.0 (only [6,7,8] clears it, by 0.04 PPL). Verdict logic falls in the straddling band. However, the scientific content is stronger than the verdict string: combined with the cycle 242 anchors the ΔPPL-vs-window-start curve is cleanly **monotone** in start index with very tight seed std (0.02-0.05) at every window:
     - [1,2,3]→47.135, [3,4,5]→49.477, [5,6,7]→50.899, [6,7,8]→52.038, [8,9,10]→55.192.
     - Differences are 2.342, 1.422, 1.139, 3.154 — the bulk of the late-block penalty (5.71 from [1,2,3] to [8,9,10]) is split smoothly across mid/late start indices, with the biggest single jump between [6,7,8] and [8,9,10] (3.15 PPL). No sharp cliff; no flat region. The "mid-cascade fragility" story from cycle 243 (blocks 4 and 5 heaviest per-block) does NOT reproduce as a dip for the 3-block window containing blocks 4 and 5: [3,4,5] is the LOWEST of the three new windows, not the highest. At fresh 3-block fit the penalty rises monotonically with the late block in the window. This means cycle 243's per-block ΔPPL ranking (which measured restoration in a jointly-fit 6-block cascade) does NOT predict fresh-fit 3-block difficulty: **the cascade cost is set by the deepest block in the window, not by "fragile" middle blocks**. The init PPL also rises monotonically (65.85 → 66.54 → 67.65 → 71.35), so the late penalty is already visible at lstsq init before any Adam training — late blocks have structurally worse K=512 topk projections on calibration, and Adam closes only part of the gap (init→best drop: 16.4, 15.6, 15.6, 16.2 across [3,4,5]/[5,6,7]/[6,7,8]/[8,9,10] — remarkably constant, meaning Adam contributes ~16 PPL of improvement no matter where in depth you sit, but the initial floor is what differs). Seed std is tightest on [6,7,8] (0.021) and loosest on [3,4,5] (0.052) — more variance at earlier cascade positions, consistent with the cycle 242 [1,2,3]/[8,9,10] split (0.093 vs 0.040). Follow-ups: (a) single-block ablations at block 8, 9, 10 to see which individual block drives the +3.15 jump from [6,7,8] to [8,9,10] (is it block 9 or 10?); (b) [4,5,6] and [7,8,9] to fill the remaining gaps in the sweep; (c) a K sweep at the worst window [8,9,10] to test whether the late penalty is K-sensitive (cycles 240/225 show K matters heavily at 6 blocks but weakly at 3 blocks [1,4,7]).
   - Artifacts: `experiments/sliding_3block_cycle244.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_244_sliding_3block.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_244_summary.md`

### Cycle 243

253. **Probe the 6-block 59.5 ceiling — per-block PPL decomposition under the [1,2,4,5,7,8] joint fit** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 243)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — Refit cycle 221 at seed 42 only (init=86.686 → best=59.391 at step 1300, exact match to cycle 221 seed-42 reference 59.391), then restored each compressed block's float MLP one at a time on held-out `eval_ids = all_ids[200:300]` (SEQ_LEN=128). Fully-compressed baseline=59.391, float=53.895, compression gap=+5.496 PPL. Per-block restoration (sorted by ΔPPL desc):
     - block 5: ppl=54.291, Δ=+5.100, mse=3.24e-1
     - block 4: ppl=54.495, Δ=+4.896, mse=2.12e-1
     - block 7: ppl=55.256, Δ=+4.135, mse=5.65e-1
     - block 8: ppl=55.608, Δ=+3.783, mse=9.28e-1
     - block 2: ppl=55.861, Δ=+3.530, mse=2.22e-1
     - block 1: ppl=56.699, Δ=+2.692, mse=1.02e-1
   - **CONFIRMED** — 5 of 6 blocks clear the ≤56.0 threshold individually (only block 1 at 56.70 misses). The confirmation criterion (∃X ppl_if_X_restored ≤ 56.0) is met by blocks 2/4/5/7/8. Interpretation: every compressed block carries a large share of the 5.5 PPL gap, and restoring **any** mid-cascade block (4, 5) brings the cascade within 0.5 PPL of float. The delta distribution is non-uniform (range 2.69–5.10, σ≈0.86) and non-additive (Σ delta = +24.14 vs 5.50 actual gap: 4.4×). Every restoration improves PPL — there is no single bottleneck block; instead the gap is a **compounded cascade effect**. Residual MSE does NOT correlate with ΔPPL (block 8 has highest MSE 0.928 but only rank-4 ΔPPL; block 5 has rank-1 ΔPPL 5.10 with MSE 0.324). Surprising: block 4 (middle of cascade) has larger ΔPPL than block 8 (deepest), contradicting the cycle 242 "late-block compression dominates" hypothesis when measured at the per-block attribution level. The cycle 242 late-block penalty (8.64 at [8,9,10]) and the current cycle's block 8 Δ=3.78 are consistent only if the late-block penalty is about INTERACTION between late blocks, not any single late block. Block 1 (earliest in cascade) contributes the least (2.69), matching the pattern from cycle 242's early-cascade-is-easy result. Remediation implication: targeted remediation at a single block won't close the gap; the 5.5 PPL gap is a **cascade phenomenon requiring joint re-optimisation** across all 6 blocks, not a single-block problem. The round-trip sanity check passed for every restoration (|rt − fully_compressed| < 1e-4). Follow-ups: 2-block and 3-block restoration scans (does restoring blocks {4, 5} jointly account for the full 5.5 gap or only ~2.0 as implied by single-block non-additivity?); investigate why block 8 has the largest residual MSE (0.928) but only rank-4 ΔPPL — is the compression error at block 8 largely in a null direction of downstream attention?
   - Artifacts: `experiments/6block_per_block_attribution_cycle243.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_243_per_block_attribution.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_243_summary.md`

### Cycle 242

252. **Early-only vs late-only 3-block compression — disentangle cascade depth from block count** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 242)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — Two 3-block configs tested at identical block COUNT vs cycle 210 BLOCKS=[1,4,7] baseline 46.556 ± 0.028. Joint Adam W_out+b_out, K=512, LR=1e-4, 4000 steps, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore, 3 seeds {42, 137, 2024}, disjoint calib=all_ids[:200] / eval=all_ids[200:300] SEQ_LEN=128. Trainable 1,181,952 params per config. Float held-out PPL=53.895.
     - **CONFIG A [1,2,3] (early)**: init=61.695 (zero seed variance) → per-seed best 47.205 / 47.003 / 47.198 at steps 2500 / 2400 / 2400 → **mean 47.135 ± 0.093, Δ=+0.579** (within ±0.8 band).
     - **CONFIG B [8,9,10] (late)**: init=71.349 (zero seed variance) → per-seed best 55.173 / 55.248 / 55.155 all at step **1400** → **mean 55.192 ± 0.040, Δ=+8.636** (far exceeds 2.0 threshold).
   - **CONFIRMED** at max(Δ_A, Δ_B) = +8.636 ≥ 2.0. Depth PLACEMENT matters, not just block count. Late-block compression is structurally harder: init PPL +9.65 above early (71.35 vs 61.70), trained floor +8.05 above early (55.19 vs 47.14), and the cascade barrier persists despite matched block count, matched topk+lstsq recipe, matched trainable budget, and matched optimisation (same optimiser, LR, step count, best-state restore, disjoint split). The three [8,9,10] seeds hit best at step 1400 with tight std 0.040 — convergence is genuine and early, not budget-bounded. The three [1,2,3] seeds converge later (step 2400-2500) and the full-float held-out eval baseline 53.895 is *between* the two configs, i.e. the early-only compressed cascade beats full fp32 held-out by 6.7 PPL while the late-only compressed cascade lands 1.3 PPL *above* full fp32. Together with cycle 233 [3..8] 6-block 67.826, this rules out the "block count is the sole driver" story from cycle 241's follow-up. The new null hypothesis: the cascade floor is dominated by late-block compression cost, not block-count arithmetic. Immediate follow-ups: (a) single-block ablations at block 8, 9, 10 to localise which of the three drives most of the +8.64; (b) [8,9,10] K sweep to check whether the late-block penalty is K-sensitive like the 6-block cycle 240 result; (c) [7,8,9] to see whether block 7 participates in the barrier or 10 is the outlier.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/early_vs_late_3block_cycle242.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_242_early_vs_late.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_242_summary.md`

W_fc matrix itself remains untested; the agenda's 2.38M budget explicitly forbids unfreezing the matrix. Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored − best| < 1e-3). Trajectory shape: rapid descent init 86.69 → ~60 by step 500, plateau 59.4-60 through step 1200-1600 (best), then linear climb to 71-73 by step 4000.

250. **K=1 Adam correction at 6 blocks — does the K=1 floor hold at cascade depth?** *(REFUTED — cycle 240)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — K=1 6-block (BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8], 9,216 trainable params) joint Adam W_out+b_out 4000 steps best_mean = **96.886 ± 0.071** (per-seed 96.943 / 96.786 / 96.929), init = 203.246 (zero seed variance, lstsq is deterministic). The 59.5 floor is **NOT** a structural cascade-depth barrier independent of K — channel capacity matters dramatically at 6 blocks, far more than the cycle 230 K=1 3-block result extrapolated to. The K-vs-PPL curve at 6 blocks penalizes K=1 catastrophically (init 203.2 = 3.77× float; trained 96.9 = 1.80× float). Cascade depth × low channel count is multiplicatively worse than either alone. K=1 worked at 3 blocks (within +10%) because the 3-block surface has enough mid-cascade slack to absorb extreme c_fc sparsity; at 6 blocks the same single-channel projection compounds across the cascade and cannot be repaired by W_out Adam alone. Best-state restore round-trip clean for all 3 seeds (|restored − best| < 1e-3, all best states are step 4000). Trajectory: linear descent from init 203 → ~96.9 over 4000 steps with no plateau — the surface is still optimisable but trapped in a basin two orders of magnitude above the K=512 floor.

249. **Cycle 234 rerun with disjoint calib/eval split — is the LoRA residual real?** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 239)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — re-ran cycle 234's gelu_noneg + per-channel μ_l + LoRA r=8 on every block's `mlp.c_proj` recipe with the cycle 206/210/223/225/230 safe disjoint split: `calib_ids = all_ids[:200]`, `eval_ids = all_ids[200:300]` (300 wikitext-2 val seqs at SEQ_LEN=128, seed-42 tokenization order), object-id and tensor-equality disjointness asserted. μ precomputation, GeluNonegCorrected patching, LoRA install, and 2000-step Adam training (LR=1e-4, EVAL_EVERY=200, best-state restore) all run exclusively on `calib_ids`; every printed PPL, best-state metric, and `final_ppl` is computed on the held-out `eval_ids` only. 3 seeds (42, 137, 2024). **Final eval PPL mean = 47.692 ± 0.143** (per-seed: 47.666 / 47.531 / 47.879), all three seeds reach best at step 2000 (still descending — budget-bounded, not converged). **CONFIRMED at threshold ≤48.0**, by 0.31 PPL. Δ vs float baseline (held-out, this run): −6.20 PPL (the held-out 100-seq float baseline is 53.895, harder than the 200-seq calib float 45.064; the LoRA-trained model with gelu_noneg+μ in fact beats float on this slice by 11.5%). Δ vs cycle 234 leaked 32.411: +15.28 PPL — most of cycle 234's apparent gain WAS calibration overfit, but the residual LoRA capacity that DOES generalise still clears the 48 confirmation threshold. Δ vs cycle 228 calib μ-only ref 61.664: −13.97 PPL. The held-out μ-only floor (LoRA-zero init eval PPL) is 74.301, much higher than the calib 61.664 — the eval split is structurally harder for the μ-corrected model than calib, so LoRA training has to absorb 74.30 → 47.69 = 26.61 PPL of held-out gap (vs cycle 234's calib slice where LoRA went 61.66 → 32.41 = 29.25 PPL). LoRA generalises in the same direction with similar magnitude. **Sanity clean**: len(calib)=200, len(eval)=100, disjoint by both checks; post-μ CALIB PPL = 61.6636 (matches cycle 228 ref); LoRA-zero init eval matches post-μ eval (74.3012) within 1e-3 across all seeds; block-0 base c_proj weight unchanged across full 6000 training steps; μ ≤ 0 elementwise (max = 0); 368,640 trainable params = 12 × (3072×8 + 8×768). The cycle 234 audit loop is closed: the rank-8 W_out residual on top of gelu_noneg + μ_l does meaningfully absorb the higher-order GELU curvature on held-out data, just not as dramatically as the leaked 32.411 suggested. Follow-ups: rank sweep r ∈ {1, 2, 4, 16} on this same disjoint protocol; SVD of learned A@B per block to characterize the per-block residual subspace; longer training budget (still descending at step 2000).

248. **T4: Activation patching across token pairs (bump-only)** *(REFUTED — cycle 238)*
   - **Outcome: REFUTED** — held-out wikitext-2 val 200×128, 100 (A, B, p) pairs sampled with positions p ∈ {32, 64, 96} and logit margin >2 over runner-up, top1(A, p) ≠ top1(B, p), seed 42. For each pair × block l ∈ {1, 3, 5, 7, 9}: computed Δ̃_l = (GELU − ReLU)(x @ W_fc + b_fc) @ W_proj − μ_l at position p for A and B (μ_l from cycle 235 T1 features), cached x_l from a plain forward pass via a forward hook on transformer.h[l]. Bump-only patch installed forward_pre_hook on h[l+1] that overwrites args[0][0, p, :] ← x_l(A) − Δ̃(A) + Δ̃(B); full-residual patch overwrites with x_l(B). Compared to clean logits cached from a baseline forward pass. Sanity clean: identity patch (v=x_l(A) no-swap) reproduces clean logits max |Δ|=0.00e+00. Per-block bump_attribution (n=100): block 1 mean=0.2121 median=0.0083 std=1.44 (frac>0.15=0.15, frac_full_positive=42/100); block 3 mean=0.1057 median=0.0117 std=0.90 (frac>0.15=0.15, n_full_pos=54); block 5 mean=0.0348 median=-0.0093 std=0.70 (n_full_pos=53); block 7 mean=0.2132 median=-0.0038 std=2.31 (n_full_pos=57); block 9 mean=2.7904 median=0.0167 std=27.15 (n_full_pos=64). **Mean and median disagree dramatically** at all 5 blocks — block 9 is the clearest case: mean 2.79 vs median 0.017 with std 27.15. Per the design review note, preferred median over mean (unbounded ratio tails inflate mean); stat_used=median. n_blocks_mean_above_0.15 = 3/5 (blocks 1, 7, 9); n_blocks_median_above_0.15 = 0/5. Median in all 5 blocks is < 0.02 (≪ 0.05 REFUTED gate), so **verdict REFUTED**. Additional caveat: the full-residual patch makes B's top1 logit go UP in only ~42-64% of pairs per block (the denominator of bump_attribution is not consistently positive), meaning the "full patch cleanly shifts A's prediction toward B's token" assumption the ratio relies on is not robust — half the pairs have denominators flipping sign, which is what inflates the mean tails in the first place. At the per-prediction / per-token level the centred bump Δ̃_l captures ~none of the inter-token logit shift on B's top1, despite cycle 237's strong ablation-PPL result showing the top-10 bump subspace is aggregate-critical. The two results are consistent under a "Δ̃ is aggregate compression-critical but not token-specific" story: the bump subspace carries broad LM signal whose removal ruins generation, but swapping Δ̃ between two token contexts does not transfer token-specific prediction signal at the ≥5% level.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/bump_t4_patching_cycle238.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_238_t4_patching.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_238_t4_examples.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_238_t4_summary.md`

### Cycle 237

247. **T3: Bump subspace ablation vs random subspace baseline** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 237)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — held-out wikitext-2 val 200×512=102,400 tokens, gpt2 on MPS fp32. For each of 12 blocks built `P_bump_l = V_l[:,:10] @ V_l[:,:10].T` from cycle 235's centred-Δ̃ right singular vectors. Installed per-block forward hooks on `transformer.h[l]` that replace `hs ← hs − hs @ P_l`. Baseline PPL 34.514. **Joint bump ablation (all 12 blocks): PPL = 2,344,385.8 (67,926× baseline)**; joint random k=10 baseline over 3 seeds (QR-orthonormal Gaussian): 101.30 ± 8.14 (2.94× baseline). Ratio joint_bump / joint_random = **23,143×** (gate ≥1.5). Per-block single-block ratios: [19.9, 12.9, 10.6, 30.2, 22.1, 25.2, 797.7, 11.2, 1.65, 1.45, 1.51, 928.4] — **9/12 blocks above 2× random** (gate ≥3). The bump subspace is massively load-bearing at the ablation-PPL level, especially at blocks 6 (797×) and 11 (928×) which have catastrophic single-block failures. Blocks 8-10 are the only ones where the top-10 bump is ~random (ratios 1.45-1.65). Full-FFN top-10 right-SV comparison: joint PPL = 3,114,155 (slightly worse than bump); per-block ratios show the full-FFN loud-channel directions are also structurally critical (block 3 = 11,317 PPL single-block; block 11 = 4,400 PPL single-block). Bump and full-FFN top-10 subspaces are both critical, but NOT in the same blocks: bump catastrophic at blocks 6, 11; full-FFN catastrophic at blocks 3, 11. Sanity clean: V_0 orthonormal (max err 1.07e-6), P_0 idempotent (max err 5.07e-7), restore baseline delta = 0.00e+00 after all hooks removed. This is a strong positive result — the centred bump directions from the cycle 235 T1 inventory are NOT just a statistical artifact: ablating them destroys the model far beyond what removing a random 10-dim slice does, directly falsifying a "bump is compression slack" story at the aggregate PPL level. Combined with cycle 236 T2 (causal intervention found real non-random logit shifts but T1's vocab decoding was the wrong probe), the bump subspace is causally load-bearing for LM prediction — the remaining open question is which readers downstream actually use it (logit-lens / downstream-V probes, not wte @ v_{l,i}).
   - Artifacts: `experiments/bump_t3_ablation_cycle237.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_237_t3_ablation_ppl.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_237_t3_summary.md`

### Cycle 236

246. **T2: Causal intervention on top bump directions** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 236)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — held-out wikitext-2 val 8×128=1024 tokens disjoint from calibration (wikitext-2 train 200×512). For each of 12 blocks, top-5 directions by variance_explained from cycle 235 intervened on residual stream entering block l+1 (or ln_f at l=11) with alpha ∈ {-3,-1,0,+1,+3} × std_l[i] (std recomputed from calibration). Random baseline: 10 unit-norm Gaussian seeds scaled by mean(std_l). Verdict gate: joint effect_size≥3 AND jaccard_top20≥0.3 in ≥3 of top-5 dirs in ≥6 of 12 blocks. **Result: n_blocks_pass = 0/12** — directions DO cause strong, highly non-random logit shifts (max effect size 29.23 at block 11 dir 0; 18/60 directions have effect ≥3x random; 4/12 blocks pass effect-only gate), but their top-20 boosted vocab does NOT match T1's wte@v predicted boost set — max Jaccard 0.333 (single direction: block 11 dir 4), only 1/60 directions satisfy jaccard ≥0.3. Per-block max effect size: [5.23, 3.62, 2.34, 2.72, 2.62, 2.20, 3.47, 2.85, 4.20, 5.88, 8.05, 29.23] — effect grows monotonically with depth, consistent with the post-ln_f + lm_head amplification of a direct writer direction at the last block. Sanity clean: α=0 no-op gives zero diff (max |Δlogit|=0.00e+00), α=±1 L2 always ≤ α=±3 L2, std_l finite and O(1) per block (means 0.67 → 1.42 increasing with depth). This is the "correlational alignment" failure mode the cycle 235 summary flagged: the centred bump directions are causally active (they DO shift logits far above random), but T1's vocab decoding via wte @ v_{l,i} does NOT predict which tokens they shift. The cycle 218 alignment story — "Δ̃ writes into the downstream reading subspaces the unembedding uses" — is refuted at the top-SVD-direction level as stated, but a weaker version ("Δ̃ writes into SOME downstream subspace, just not the unembedding column space") survives. Next: direct wte decoding was the wrong probe; a better probe would measure which residual-stream directions the boosted tokens correspond to (e.g. downstream V_l at later blocks, or logit-lens-style probes of the intermediate residual stream). T3 (item 247, subspace ablation PPL test) is independent and still worth running — it measures the aggregate compression relevance of the bump subspace, which does NOT depend on the per-direction T1 story surviving.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/bump_t2_intervention_cycle236.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_236_t2_logit_shifts.pt`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_236_t2_intervention.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_236_t2_examples.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_236_t2_summary.md`

### Cycle 235

245. **T1: Centred Δ̃ feature SVD with concrete example extraction** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 235)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — on wikitext-2 train split, N_SEQS=200 × SEQ_LEN=512 = 102,400 tokens, seed 42, gpt2 on MPS fp32. For each of 12 blocks extracted Δ̃_l = (GELU-ReLU)(c_fc_input @ W_fc + b_fc) @ W_proj − μ_l, SVD → top 32 right singular vectors V_l (768, 32), singular values S_l (32,), U_l (102400, 32). Per direction: top-100 corpus tokens by |U_l[:, i]| with 5-token contexts, top-50 boost/suppress via wte @ v, heuristic coherence label (punctuation/stopword/capitalized/number/repeated/mixed/incoherent). All 384 records (12×32) saved to cycle_235_t1_decoded.json; human-readable per-block top-5 examples in cycle_235_t1_examples.md. Verdict thresholds: CONFIRMED ≥6 blocks with ≥5 coherent directions; REFUTED all 12 blocks <2 coherent; else INCONCLUSIVE. Result: **12/12 blocks pass the ≥5-coherent threshold**, per-block coherent counts [27, 23, 24, 27, 21, 19, 19, 15, 17, 15, 12, 10] (descending with depth from block 0 → block 11). Dominant label by far is `repeated` (mode_count ≥ 30 in top-100), consistent with the left-singular coefficient putting most mass on a single high-frequency token id per direction; `stopword`, `capitalized`, `punctuation`, `number` appear as second-tier labels. σ₀ and σ₁ per block are ~2× the cycle 218 values (c218 used val split + SEQ_LEN=128 = 25.6K tokens; c235 uses train split + SEQ_LEN=512 = 102.4K tokens, so the singular values scale roughly with √(n_tokens/25600) = 2.0). The centred bump space admits interpretable surface features at this heuristic level in every block, unlocking T2/T3/T4 (items 246-248). Caveat: "repeated" is a cheap surface label — it says the top-100 mass is concentrated on one token, not that the direction causally boosts that token's logit. T2 (item 246) is the causal test: apply α·v_{l,i} to the residual stream and measure whether the actual logit shift matches the T1 vocab prediction. Until then, these are a correlational feature inventory.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/bump_t1_features_cycle235.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_235_t1_features.pt`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_235_t1_calibration_meta.pt`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_235_t1_decoded.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_235_t1_examples.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_235_t1_summary.md`

### Cycle 234

244. **gelu_noneg + first-moment + LoRA r=8 W_out residual — eat the 16.6 PPL residual?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — corrected from CONFIRMED, data leakage — cycle 234)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** (downgraded from CONFIRMED per G6, data leakage — see `autoresearch/audits/cycle_234.md`). Training loop optimises LoRA A, B against `eval_ids = all_ids[:200]` and the final metric is `eval_ppl(model, eval_ids)` on the SAME 200 sequences — 368K trainable params vs ~25.4K eval tokens gives the rank-8 residual enough capacity to memorise the calibration slice, and the reported 32.411 PPL falls 12.65 PPL BELOW float baseline on the same slice, which is only possible by overfitting. Cycles 220/228 share the `eval_ids = all_ids[:200]` bind safely because they never train (cycle 220 = activation patches only; cycle 228 = deterministic μ statistic + eval), so the calib==eval reuse is harmless in the no-gradient setting. Cycle 234 inherits the scaffolding and then introduces an Adam optimiser, turning the eval set into a training set. The safe pattern is cycles 206/210/223/225/230: `calib_ids = all_ids[:200]`, `eval_ids = all_ids[200:300]`, disjoint. The literal verdict (32.411 ≤ 48.0 → CONFIRMED) is met on calibration but the hypothesis "LoRA r=8 absorbs the higher-order GELU curvature for held-out data" is untested. Downgrade to INCONCLUSIVE. **Original executor findings retained for reference**: Cycle 228 protocol (N=200 wikitext-2 val, SEQ_LEN=128, seed 42, MPS) + LoRA r=8 on each of 12 blocks' `mlp.c_proj` (HF Conv1D shape (3072, 768)), A ∈ R^(3072, 8) init N(0, 0.01), B ∈ R^(8, 768) init zero, trained jointly via Adam LR=1e-4 for 2000 steps on CE against the same calibration sequences, EVAL_EVERY=200 with best-state restore. Init sanity clean: baseline 45.064, μ-only 12-block patch reproduces cycle 228 exactly (61.6636), LoRA-zero init matches post-μ PPL (61.6636 = 61.6636), trainable params = 368,640. Training trajectory monotone decreasing at every 200-step eval checkpoint: 61.664 → 42.724 → 39.474 → 37.725 → 36.568 → 35.461 → 34.808 → 34.054 → 33.480 → 32.883 → 32.411 (best at step 2000, still descending — no plateau, consistent with capacity-unbounded in-distribution fit).
   - Next: rerun with disjoint `calib_ids`/`eval_ids` split (e.g., `all_ids[:200]` / `all_ids[200:300]`). Expected held-out PPL band: (45.064, 61.664). If held-out ≈ 45 the hypothesis upgrades back to CONFIRMED; if held-out ≈ 61.66 the residual has nothing to do with a rank-≤8 W_out subspace and the hypothesis downgrades to REFUTED. Once the split is fixed, rank sweep r ∈ {1, 2, 4, 16} and SVD of learned A@B per block become meaningful.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/gelu_noneg_lora_cycle234.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_234_gelu_noneg_lora.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_234_summary.md`, `autoresearch/audits/cycle_234.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_234_audit.txt`

### Cycle 233

243. **Alternative 6-block selections — is BLOCKS=[1,2,4,5,7,8] representative?** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 233)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — Swept three alternative 6-block subsets under cycle 221's exact protocol (K=512, LR=1e-4, joint Adam W_out+b_out, 4000 steps, EVAL_EVERY=100, best-state restore), SEED=42 phase 1. Float PPL 53.895 (unchanged). Per-subset (init / best_ppl / best_step / final@4000): **[1,2,3,4,5,6]** contiguous early = 87.631 / **61.785** / 1300 / 76.961; **[3,4,5,6,7,8]** contiguous middle = 96.758 / **67.826** / 1100 / 86.267; **[1,3,5,7,8,10]** spread = 87.237 / **60.981** / 1400 / 75.466. Cycle 221 reference [1,2,4,5,7,8] = 86.686 / 59.515 ± 0.053 / ~1500 / 72.250. All three alternatives trained cleanly (zero collapses, restore sanity matched to < 1e-3). All three **overfit** past best_step and balloon toward step 4000 (+15–19 PPL above best by end of budget), mirroring cycle 221's trajectory shape. **Phase 2 gate NOT passed** — no subset cleared the 59.0 PPL threshold, so no 3-seed rerun was performed (saved ~20 min compute; single-seed numbers are sufficient to reject CONFIRMED). **Verdict band analysis**: CONFIRMED requires ≤58.0 (no subset passes), REFUTED requires all three ∈ [59.0, 62.0] (the middle subset [3,4,5,6,7,8] = 67.826 is **outside** the REFUTED band by +5.8 PPL), so the outcome is INCONCLUSIVE. **Findings**: (1) The spread subset [1,3,5,7,8,10] is the **best** of all four tested selections at 60.981, but only by 1.47 PPL vs cycle 221's 59.515 — and the single-seed phase 1 noise floor (~0.05 PPL std from c221) is far smaller than this gap, so c221's original [1,2,4,5,7,8] remains the best-known 6-block choice with full 3-seed evidence. (2) The middle subset [3,4,5,6,7,8] is a **clear structural outlier** at 67.826 (+8.3 PPL vs c221) with init 96.758 (+10.1 above c221's init) — blocks 3,4,5,6 form a consecutive mid-layer cascade that lstsq+Adam cannot absorb as efficiently, likely because consecutive-block error coupling amplifies the cascade residual. (3) Both [1,2,3,4,5,6] and [1,3,5,7,8,10] land in the [60.9, 61.8] band, ~1.3–2.3 PPL above cycle 221's 59.515 — suggesting the true 6-block floor for the best-case selection is in [59.5, 61.0] and mildly dependent on which 6 you pick. The 59.5 PPL ceiling from cycle 221 is therefore **approximately structural but selection-sensitive**: the 6-block W_out+b_out Adam surface has a floor near ~59–60 PPL regardless of the specific 6 non-extremal blocks chosen, with [1,2,4,5,7,8] (spread across low/mid) being locally optimal and consecutive middle subsets being strictly worse. Sub-58 PPL at 6 blocks is **not** reachable by re-choosing the block subset — the ceiling is closer to structural than selection-dependent, but not cleanly refuted in the strict [59, 62] band because the middle subset breaks the band from above.

Init 61.746 (deterministic). **Post-phase-1 (sequential) mean 47.253 ± 0.047** matches cycle 215 exactly (47.253 ± 0.047) — phase 1 sanity check clean across all 3 seeds with zero collapses. **Phase 2 polish final_mean = 46.911 ± 0.026** (per-seed: 46.878 / 46.916 / 46.941; all 3 seeds reach best at step 1000 = end of phase 2 budget, still descending, zero collapses). Gain from polish = **+0.342 ± 0.026 PPL per seed** — joint polish DOES help sequential init, but not enough. **Δ vs joint 4k W_out (c210) 46.556 = +0.355 PPL** (still worse); Δ vs joint W_out+W_fc (c213) 46.424 = +0.487 (worse); Δ vs sequential alone (c215) 47.253 = −0.342 (better by the polish gain). Hybrid final 46.911 is in the interval [46.5, 47.25] and exceeds the 46.5 REFUTED threshold — sequential warm-start does not open a better basin for joint Adam than running joint Adam from the topk+lstsq init directly. The 46.5 floor for 3-block W_out+b_out Adam correction at K=512 appears structural across all tested paths: joint-from-lstsq (c210: 46.556), joint-from-sequential (c232: 46.911), W_out+W_fc joint (c213: 46.424). The best-known remains cycle 213's W_out+W_fc at 46.424.

The cycle 230 finding "no compression cliff in [1, 512] at 3 blocks" does NOT generalise to 6 blocks — at 6 blocks the K-vs-PPL curve steepens dramatically.

240. **K ∈ {8, 4, 2, 1} Adam compression cliff sweep — find the absolute floor** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 230)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — BLOCKS=[1,4,7] K ∈ {8,4,2,1} Adam W_out+b_out, 3 seeds (42/137/2024), 4000 steps with eval-every-100 and best-state restore. Init PPL grows monotonically as K shrinks: K=8→69.223, K=4→69.830, K=2→70.256, K=1→71.409 (zero per-seed variance since lstsq init is deterministic up to numerics). Trained best_mean: K=8 = **56.323 ± 0.011** (+4.50% vs float), K=4 = **57.328 ± 0.026** (+6.37% vs float, well under the 59.28 CONFIRMED threshold), K=2 = **58.289 ± 0.035** (+8.15%), K=1 = **59.245 ± 0.029** (+9.93% — still within the +10% confirmed band). Full extended curve {512,256,128,64,32,16,8,4,2,1} → {46.556, 47.509, 48.989, 50.781, 53.038, 55.133, 56.323, 57.328, 58.289, 59.245} is strictly monotone with each halving costing ~1.0–1.2 PPL in the sub-16 regime (vs ~1.5–2.3 PPL per halving in the high-K regime). **No compression cliff** appears anywhere in [1, 512] — the curve is near-linear in log K. At K=1 each block has only 1×768 + 768 = 1,536 trainable params (4,608 total for 3 blocks, a **257× compression** vs K=512's 1.18M params) yet Adam recovers 12.16 PPL from init (−17.0%), identical in magnitude to the Δ at K=8 (−12.9 PPL). Every K passes the floor diagnostic (|Δ| ≥ 11.97, well above the 1.0 "Adam failed to learn" threshold) and none are within 2× of float. The "compression floor" for W_out+b_out Adam correction on 3 blocks is K=1 and it sits precisely at the +10% band. Adam can absorb arbitrary topk selection sparsity; the surface is not capacity-limited at any K tested. Matched-budget: K=4 (11,520 params) gets within +2.20 PPL of K=16 (39,168 params) for 3.4× less trainable memory. The new "free" ultra-compression operating point for 3-block surgical patches is K=4 (+6.37% vs float, 257× compression vs K=512 would be K=1 at +9.93%).

### Cycle 227

237. **Cycle 218 specificity control — Δ̃_l alignment with non-adjacent c_fc(j)** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 227)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — Reproduced cycle 218's adjacent-pair signal exactly (cycle 227 adjacent top-5 cos_sum mean = 2.9687 vs cycle 218 = 2.9687, Δ = 0.0000). Measured the heatmap of top-5 principal cosine sums for centred Δ̃_l vs c_fc(j) for ALL l < j ∈ {0..11}. Per-row specificity gap (max@l+1 − mean over j>l+1) ranges 0.40–1.00 across l ∈ {0..9}, **median = +0.791** (in the 0.3–1.0 INCONCLUSIVE band). The peak at j=l+1 IS larger than the average non-adjacent destination in every row, but **0/10 rows have all non-adjacent entries within 3σ of the random baseline**: ALL 55/55 non-adjacent (l, j) entries clear the 3σ threshold. Δ̃_l preferentially writes into block l+1's c_fc reading subspace, but it ALSO leaks into every later block's c_fc subspace at well above random — the universal-reading-basis interpretation cannot be ruled out. Cycle 218's CONFIRMED finding survives only as "Δ̃ aligns with c_fc subspaces in general" rather than "Δ̃ specifically targets block l+1". Random baseline per j: top5_mean ≈ 1.25–1.27 ± 0.03–0.05, top5_mean+3σ ≈ 1.34–1.43; non-adjacent values sit at 1.5–2.7 (well above). The "0/10 quiet" result is the dominant signal — adjacency specificity is real but small relative to the broad-band write.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/delta_specificity_cycle227.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_227_delta_specificity.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_227_summary.md`

### Cycle 226

236. **KD distillation baseline rerun with corrected loss normalization** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 226)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** — Loss normalization fix worked (kd_loss is now O(0.5–0.8), comparable to ce_loss O(4) in pre-softmax-temperature scale; with τ²=4 multiplier, contributions are balanced). α-sweep (SEED=42, 2000 steps) gave α=0.0 → 46.430 (best step 1200), α=0.5 → 47.096 (best step 2000, still descending), α=1.0 → 61.746 (no learning, the soft-teacher KL has zero gradient signal for the per-token CE objective being measured). Pure CE wins, so the full run at α=0 reproduces cycle 213's pure-CE result: best_mean = **46.364 ± 0.047** (best_step 1267 ± 94, init 61.746), Δ vs cycle 213 (46.424) = **−0.060 PPL**. The verdict band is INCONCLUSIVE (44.0 < 46.364 < 47.0), but more meaningfully **best α = 0** means KD as formulated provides no benefit over pure CE on this surface. The hypothesis "KD beats CE on W_out+W_fc" is not supported. Note that pure α=0.5 still descends through 2000 steps (47.096) so a longer run might give a different ranking — but at the matched 2000-step budget, CE alone is strictly better. The cycle 224 broken-norm result (60.938) was a method failure; cycle 226 confirms that with correct normalization the surface can train, but does not need KD to do so.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/distillation_corrected_cycle226.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_226_distillation_corrected.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_226_summary.md`

### Cycle 225

235. **K=64/32/16 Adam compression cliff sweep** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 225)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — BLOCKS=[1,4,7] K ∈ {64,32,16} Adam W_out+b_out, 3 seeds (42/137/2024), 4000 steps + best-state restore. K=64 best_mean = **50.781 ± 0.021** (init 66.036, best step ~3967), K=32 = **53.038 ± 0.009** (init 67.033, best at 4000), K=16 = **55.133 ± 0.010** (init 68.978, best at 4000). Float = 53.895; K=128 (cycle 223) = 48.989. The CONFIRMED clause is met: K=64 (and even K=32, by 0.86 PPL) still beats float despite running on only 149,760 / 76,032 trainable params for the entire 3-block correction surface. The cliff sits between K=32 and K=16: K=16 is the first row in the curve to cross above float (+2.30%). The curve {512, 256, 128, 64, 32, 16} → {46.556, 47.509, 48.989, 50.781, 53.038, 55.133} is monotone but each halving costs only ~1-2 PPL, so 32× compression of W_out (K=512 → K=16) costs +8.58 PPL relative to K=512 and +1.24 PPL relative to float. K=64 (149K params, 7.9× compression vs K=512) is the new "free" operating point.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/adam_lower_k_cliff_cycle225.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_225_lower_k_cliff.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_225_summary.md`

### Cycle 224

229. **Distillation baseline for 3-block compression — matched budget** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 224, corrected from REFUTED, method failure)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** (downgraded from REFUTED per G6, method failure — see `autoresearch/audits/cycle_224.md`). BLOCKS=[1,4,7] K=512, train W_fc+b_fc+W_out+b_out (2.36M params, same as cycle 213) on 50/50 KD+CE loss with float GPT-2 teacher, tau=2.0, LR=1e-4, 10000 steps × 3 seeds. best_mean = **60.938 ± 0.103**, init = 61.746 — best is only 0.6–0.9 PPL below init across all seeds with no monotone descent. **Root cause: `F.kl_div(..., reduction='batchmean')` on `(B,T,V)=(1,128,50257)` divides only by B=1**, so the returned KL is the SUM over T·V≈6.4M entries while CE averages over B·T=128 tokens. Empirically kd_loss≈30–50, ce_loss≈4 — the 50/50 mix is effectively ~10× KD weighting, the optimizer is dominated by a softened-teacher KL gradient against weak targets, and the trainable surface barely moves. Cycle 213's pure-CE run on the same surface reaches 46.424, so the surface is NOT the bottleneck — the loss formulation is. The literal verdict (60.938 ≥ 47.0 → REFUTED) does not test the hypothesis "KD beats CE on W_out+W_fc"; it tests "un-normalized KD+CE 50/50 trains the surface", which fails. Hypothesis remains untested.
   - Next: rerun with reshape `(B*T, V)` before `kl_div` (or `reduction='mean'`, or weight KD by `1/(T*V)`); sweep KD/CE ratios and tau on a short budget before re-committing to 10k steps; then re-evaluate item 229.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/distillation_baseline_cycle224.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_224_distillation.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_224_summary.md`, `autoresearch/audits/cycle_224.md`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_224_audit.txt`

### Cycle 218

232. **Where does Δ point? — centred Δ̃ alignment with next block's reading directions** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 218)*
   - **Outcome: CONFIRMED** — After centring Δ̃ = Δ − mean(Δ), top-20 right singular vectors of Δ̃_l exceed the random baseline (10 seeds Gaussian orthonormal 20×768, mean+3σ threshold ≈ 3.0) against at least one downstream reader in **11/11** cross-block pairs. Per-reader significant counts: c_fc 11/11, Q 10/11, K 6/11, V 8/11, residual-PC 11/11. Centred rank_95 jumps from raw median 1 (c217) to 552 (range 489-567 across blocks) — the per-block constant bias was hiding the true per-token geometry. σ₀ (centred) 260-1293 (block 11 highest), σ₁ 193-503. Mean ||Δ|| per block 16-153. Top-3 SV vocab projection: SV0 in blocks 2/3/10 loads on stopwords/punctuation (expected, the dominant per-token axis correlates with unigram frequency after centring); SV1-2 show mixed word/tokenizer-noise clusters (vocab heuristic counts all 12 blocks but that's primarily via SV2 word tokens, not semantically clean clusters). **Key finding**: the bump IS structured inter-block communication — downstream c_fc and residual stream both read from the Δ̃ subspace with margin, and Q/K/V attention reads partially from it. Connects to cycle 216 W_down→c_fc alignment baseline 0.649. Item 234 (surgical bump removal) is now well-motivated: killing the bump should disrupt cross-block information flow.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/delta_centred_alignment_cycle218.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_218_delta_centred_alignment.json`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_218_summary.md`

### Cycle 217

231. **Per-token Δ(x) = GELU_FFN − ReLU_FFN — rank, subspace, logit impact** *(INCONCLUSIVE — corrected from CONFIRMED — cycle 217)*
   - **Outcome: INCONCLUSIVE** (audit-corrected from CONFIRMED; semantic-cluster clause not met in spirit). Rank clause ✓: rank_95 ≤ 64 in 9/12 blocks (median 1; exceptions blocks 1/8/9 at 73-132). Interpretability clause ✗: TI/TD ratio 0.87-0.99 shows Δ is dominated by a **per-block constant bias**, not a structured token-dependent message. SV0 in rank_95=1 blocks is the mean direction projecting trivially to the unigram-frequency axis (stopwords boosted, tokenizer artefacts suppressed, or its sign-flip). σ₁ ≈ 200-350 vs σ₀ ≈ 3k-7k (3-9%). The per-token component lives in SV1+ and is untested here. Cross-block top-3 principal cosines Δ_l → c_fc_{l+1} left SVs: mean 0.369 vs random 0.168 vs cycle 216 W_down→c_fc baseline 0.649 — above noise but does not exceed structural baseline. Connects cycles 188 (C3 mean replacement +20.33% PPL) and 191 (constant residual correction +12 PPL): the constant IS the dominant Δ component but the model depends on the small SV1+ residual. Item 232 must centre Δ̃ = Δ − mean(Δ) to actually test the hypothesis. Audit: `autoresearch/audits/cycle_217.md`.

201. **Mean residual error correction — directional coherence-based bias (9216 params)** *(REFUTED — cycle 191)*
   - Corrected PPL=56.781 vs uncorrected=44.842 (WORSENED by 11.938 PPL). Mean error vectors computed from calibration data, applied as constant residual stream shifts, hurt subsequent layers by shifting their inputs outside operating range. Layer 0 correction≈0 (bias already compensates), layers 1-11 fraction_of_frob 0.13-0.35 (non-trivial mean) but applying it as constant bias is counterproductive. Confirms: residual stream corrections require downstream re-calibration.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/mean_error_correction_cycle191.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_191_mean_error_correction.json`

### Cycle 190

200. **Positive-regime shape isolation — GELU-neg + ReLU-pos hybrid activation** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 190)*
   - Hybrid (GELU for z<0, ReLU for z>=0) all-12 PPL=28.980 (+27.67%) — in the inconclusive 28-35 range. Positive-regime shape change alone costs +27.67%, comparable to negative-regime constant replacement (C3 +20.33%). Full ReLU+bias swap +88% implies superadditive interaction between both modifications. Non-monotone progressive: peaks at k=7 (28.484), decreases through k=10 (27.851), rises at k=11 (28.980) — some deep-layer self-correction.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/gelu_neg_relu_pos_cycle190.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_190_gelu_neg_relu_pos.json`

### Cycle 189

199. **Cascade error direction analysis — GELU vs ReLU+bias residual stream** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 189)*
   - Mean cos(e_l, e_{l-1})=0.716; all 11/11 inter-layer pairs exceed 0.3 threshold. Errors compound directionally: ||e||_F grows 685→11472 (17x) over 12 layers. Rank_90 stays high (320-553/768) — errors are diffuse in content but directionally coherent. Block 11 anomaly: ||e||_F jumps 6054→11472 (largest single-block amplification), rank_90 drops to 320 (consistent with cycle 184). Baseline PPL=35.9 (SEQ_LEN=128), full-swap PPL=44.8 (+25%).
   - Artifacts: `experiments/cascade_error_direction_cycle189.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_189_cascade_error_direction.json`

### Cycle 188

198. **Progressive C3 composition — per-channel mean replacement across all 12 blocks** *(INCONCLUSIVE — cycle 188)*
   - Baseline 22.700 PPL. Progressive C3: +0.53%, +1.79%, +3.30%, +5.26%, +7.94%, +10.76%, +13.34%, +15.22%, +17.01%, +18.30%, +18.98%, +20.33% for blocks 0-11. All-12 PPL=27.314 (+20.33%), between +15% and +40% thresholds. Shape-preserving replacement composes far better than ReLU+bias (27.3 vs ~42 PPL). Constant replacement itself accounts for ~4.6 PPL; shape change accounts for remaining ~14.3 PPL of the 18.9-PPL gap in cycles 181/182.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/c3_composition_cycle188.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_188_c3_composition.json`

### Cycle 187

196. **Layer-drop baseline — GPT-2 FFN skip blocks [1,4,7,11]** *(CONFIRMED — cycle 187)*
   - Baseline PPL 34.9. Block 0 critical (PPL 1923 when dropped). Blocks 1-10 all droppable (PPL 36.4–39.1, damage +1.5–4.3). Block 11 borderline (41.4 PPL). Multi-block drop [1,4,7,11]: 53.0 PPL (+18). GPT-2 FFN blocks are individually near-redundant but cascade interactions produce nonlinear damage (single-block damage 2-4 vs multi-block 18). Block 0 uniquely critical.
   - Artifacts: `experiments/layer_drop_cycle187.py`, `autoresearch/results/cycle_187_layer_drop.json`

### Cycle 186

195. **Fewer-block compression — GPT-2 FFN k=4 on 2 blocks vs 4 blocks** *(REFUTED — cycle 186)*
   - Best 2-block pair (1,4) achieves mean PPL 50.4 ±5.3 vs 4-block 59.4 ±8.2 — 9 PPL improvement from halving block count, but still above 48 threshold. High cross-seed variance (std ~5-8 PPL) driven by seed-42 teacher PPL of 57.6. All 6 pairs range 50.4–54.8, tightly clustered. Block pair (1,4) consistently best.
